# World Bible Vol VIII — The Game Layer

### What the Book One game adds to the world: what the owner has ruled, what follows the book, what was decided on the owner's behalf, and what is still only proposed. Compiled 29 October 2026.

---

## How to use this volume

The Book One game (*Echoes of the Shattered Octave*, the browser RPG in this repository) has grown its own places, people, customs, laws, songs and books over forty build phases. Most of it was written as **[PROPOSED]** invention, for the owner to keep or cut. Some of it the owner has ruled into canon. Since 28 October 2026 the game also follows the book wherever the two disagreed. This volume records all of it, so that nothing written for the game is lost and nothing in it is mistaken for settled canon.

**Precedence.**

- The **manuscript** of each book wins over every bible.
- The **Series Bible** and **World Bible Vols I–VII** (with the errata in Vol III (b) and the festivals in Vol VII (b)) win over this volume.
- Within this volume, an **owner's ruling** wins over a phase document.
- The **game follows the book**. The owner's ruling of 28 October 2026: *"Bettis Renn should be Ivo's wife just as in the book. Match any inconsistency to match the book."*

**Status of every entry.**

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **canon (ruled)** | The owner ruled it in. The ruling is cited. |
| **canon (book)** | It is already in a book or a bible volume, or restates them; the game shows it. |
| **canon (delegated)** | Claude decided it at the owner's request ("make the decisions here", 22 Oct). The owner can overturn any of these. |
| **[PROPOSED]** | Invented for the game and never ruled on. It stands in the game until the owner keeps or cuts it. |
| **cut or changed** | Ruled out, or replaced by a later ruling or the canon pass. What replaced it is named. |

Where an entry adds a new detail to something already canon, its status describes the new detail, and the entry names the canon it builds on.

**Where the rest lives.**

- Every line of in-game text, phase by phase, in English and French: `story/Game Layer/Phase N - New In-Game Text.md`.
- The owner's rulings: `story/Game Layer/Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md` and `story/Game Layer/Library of the Reach - Notes for the Owner.md`.
- The 65 library books and the Cantabile serial, in full: `story/Library of the Reach/`.
- The canon-pass design and the remaining contradictions with the book: `story/Game Layer/Story Brainstorm - Book One in play.md` §5.
- What only a playtest can judge: `PLAYTEST.md`. The work still to do: `ROADMAP.md`.

**How this volume was compiled.** Four read-only compilations of the repository were made on 29 October 2026, and their entries are kept here in full:

| | Covers | Entries | Ruled | Book | Delegated | Proposed | Cut or changed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Phases 6 and 9–14 | 279 | 4 | 61 | 4 | 208 | 2 |
| **B** | Phases 15–25, the Author Rulings, the Release Readiness report and the Game Design Document | 353 | 32 | 95 | 5 | 211 | 10 |
| **C** | The Library of the Reach and the Cantabile serial: 65 books catalogued, then the world facts they assert | 239 facts | 21 | 47 | 0 | 171 | 0 |
| **D** | Phase 23 and Phases 26–40: the release road (R1–R5) and the canon pass | 250 | 14 | 137 | 0 | 98 | 1 |

A fifth check (**E**) read the novels and the library books against current canon for anything that needs a book to change.

So about three entries in four are still **[PROPOSED]**. That is the honest state of the game layer: a large, consistent invention built against the bible, most of which the owner has not yet been asked to rule on one item at a time. Part VI collects every question that is waiting.

---

## Part I — The rulings that shaped the game

### 22 October 2026: the Phase 20 and 21 Author Rulings

- **The Watch Downs become a region** west of the clearing, the first map of the Sheep-Walk. Its features stand as canon: the Long Down and its beacon cairn, Hedge Knoll, the Scar, the sheepfold, the dew-pond, the boundary stones, the hawthorns, and the finger-post reading SHEEP-WALK. *Canon (ruled).*
- **The sheep went into the Hedge's wool.** The Downs flocks were driven down to be shorn for the Hedge in 417 and kept inside it, and over the two years Oakhaven lived shut in, they were eaten. *Canon (delegated).*
- **The panorama becomes reachable.** What is painted on Oakhaven's skyline is real and may be walked to: the snapped iron pylon and the standing-stone ring outside the Hedge, the broken tower stump in the Deep Woods, Greenroot's chapel spire, the farmsteads, barns and orchards round Oakhaven, and the colonnade and marble spires round Whisper-Grove. *Canon (ruled).*
- **The route** (*canon, delegated*): nothing new joins Oakhaven; each new place joins only the map it is seen from; painted landmarks keep their real bearings. The farmsteads and pylon are reached through the Downs region; the stone ring is off the Greenroot road.
- Everything else the Phase 20 and 21 documents proposed was confirmed as proposed (see Part II, compilation B).

### 27 October 2026: the library's thirteen rulings, and the R2 canon sweep

The owner set four rules: the manuscript wins over an in-game book or journal; a bible volume stands against an in-game book, and the book changes; a book that comes close to a spoiler stays unless it makes the twist too easy to guess; and new lives for minor canon figures are all fine. The thirteen rulings that followed are quoted in Part III. The ones that changed the world's dates:

- **Tobin Ashgrove was taken in the spring of 417, at the Pellow farm, before the Hedge** (the manuscript). His journal, register, cairn and the inquiry follow it.
- **The bells were taken in 417,** "the first year of the quarry", for the bore's teeth.
- **The Hedge was planted in the summer of 417,** "two summers ago", paid for by the Consortium. Book Four's "autumn" is left for that book.
- **What lifts a skyship:** both a Fortis-cut lodestone core that cancels the mass (Vol I) and drift-glass lift-tanks that give the lift (the manuscript).
- **The Greenroot Cantor is Mabyn Verrow,** because Ilse Brennan is canon (Book Four) and two Cantors called Ilse was one too many.
- **The Veil's absorption is dated c. 300 SR,** and the lore book *The Folding of the Silent Veil* was softened at the owner's call to a hint.

### 28 October 2026: the canon pass

The owner's ruling: *"Bettis Renn should be Ivo's wife just as in the book. Match any inconsistency to match the book."* The game was changed to follow Book One in nine pull requests (#73 to #81). Part IV has the full table of what changed. In short:

1. The talking crates are in the Drift-Kestrel's hold, not Hetty's cellar (ch. 4, 8).
2. Hob Wendle is the Hollow's field-hand who roots and dies aboard; the tanner is now **Abner Fenning** [PROPOSED].
3. The Office cutter hunts the Kestrel's own 432 cores; its hold holds twelve seized bottles.
4. The Wearer is met once, at the end. The Oakhaven mast is a voice and a hound pack; the Cloister porch is *Voice at the Door* (ch. 20).
5. The coda is the ring of lamps in the clearing (ch. 23). The Mausoleum choir keeps the Requiem as the game's own branch [PROPOSED].
6. The nine days happen in the book's order: Davin on Hearthday, Pim's lure on Massday, the Church on Dawnday (ch. 4, 6, 7, 9).
7. Halder Wick's table goes unanswered; the Cloister's ninth tape is not his son's.
8. Sable stays in Whisper-Grove until the Kestrel comes (ch. 14, 16). The scribe in the mist outside the Hedge is **Umber**, a runaway of the Veil [PROPOSED].

Text-level fixes in the same pass: Bettis Renn is Ivo's wife; Form 9's three questions; Old Tam's Essa; each bell's own last words; Aldric's count at the Greening; the herald's shaking hands; twenty-six souls at the Deep Woods camp; Emory Wain's name read in the ambush; the Madder birth on Nullday; and the hush-gift child renamed **Linnet** [PROPOSED], because the bible already has a Wren.

### What is waiting for a ruling now

Part VI lists every open question. The ones the canon pass itself created, or that are live in the game today:

- **Two Umbers.** The library already has a Veil scribe "styled Umber" (books dated 410 SR and c. 290 SR). The canon pass gave the same name to the Reach's runaway scribe, Sable's junior, in 419. One of them should change.
- **Two Linnets.** The renamed hush-gift child shares her name with Linnet Tarrow of the Phase 13 house quest.
- **Abner Fenning, Umber and the Requiem branch** were offered in `PLAYTEST.md` and have not been ruled on.
- **Live contradictions with the book in the game's text** (compilation B): Kaelen's mother and Pim's father each write a held letter, though Book One has her dead and Nell widowed; Vosk is "he", where canon has Adela; Teague is alive three days after his canon death. Under the 28 October ruling these should follow the book; they need game changes, not bible changes.
- **The Carders' blue door** stands on the north row in the game; Book One (ch. 6, 11) puts it in the lane behind the tannery.
- **"Salt mutton is the Reach's staple"** in the Author Rulings cites Vol I, but Vol I gives salt mutton to Fortis.

## Part II — The world the game adds

Every entry from the four compilations, grouped by kind and then by where in the build it came from. Each keeps its status, its source and whether the bible already had it. Where two compilations describe the same thing, both entries are kept: the later one usually says what changed.

### Places

#### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

##### Oakhaven, inside the Hedge

- **Cooper's Row, "the house with the green door"** — Rumours send the stranger to the Cantor's house, "Cooper's Row, the house with the green door", and say "do not sing on the way". During the Rite the shutter carries a proctor's seal, and the hearth is cold. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (green door, proctor's seal). Source: P6 (§1 Rumours, d_cantor_asks, d_lyra_taken). Bible: already in SB §Part Four — Coda (Cooper's Row is named only). B1 has Lyra's father's house on Cooper's Row, "two rooms and a loft … a hush-lamp in every window". The bible is incomplete: no house description.
- **The North Gate and Harrow's eleven years** — Harrow unbarred the North Gate after dusk for someone with the Cantor's word. "Eleven years he has kept it shut at night." The Gazette says "The gate has always been shut at dusk. It will continue to have always been shut." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_gate; §2 Gazette No. 32). Bible: already in SB §Part Two — The Root (North Gate). The eleven years contradicts the Hedge's 417 date (see Contradictions).
- **Depot Three** — This is where the reverse-phase coil was held. Under Gazette No. 33 it is "being reconciled by Factor Vosk. Reconciliation has found no irregularity. Reconciliation has not finished." Tobiah says its Legionnaire has stopped talking about the blue jug. Status: **canon (book)** (the depot, the coil and the blue-jug Legionnaire are in B1 ch2 and ch5). The Gazette wording is game text. Source: P6 (§2 Nos. 33 and 35; Tobiah letter). Bible: already in SB §Part One — The Narrow (ch5).
- **Depot Two** — Hew Callan was its pump-man ("Hew Callan, pump-man, Depot Two"). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Letter from Hew Callan). Bible: new.
- **The mooring mast "on the east side of the square"** — During the Greening, rumour sends people "to the mooring mast on the east side of the square. The corsair takes children first, four to a line." Gazette No. 37 says the mast "is not an official relocation point". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the location). Source: P6 (§1 d_greening; §2 No. 37). Bible: already in SB §Part Two — The Root (mooring mast). Contradicted: B1 puts the mast "a hundred feet above the Oakhaven quarry", to the north.
- **Tannery Row and the Tannery culvert** — The culvert key hangs on a nail at Hob Wendle's ("off its nail … first time in a month"). The twins' coats lie under the culvert. All of Tannery Row saw Hob's window lamp go dark. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the key, Tannery Row, the coats). Source: P6 (§1 d_culvert_key, d_twins_found, d_hob_told; Hob letter). Bible: already in SB §Part One — The Narrow (the tannery culvert, through which the Wearer calls Pim). Tannery Row is new.
- **The Flume crossing, the Flume bridge and the bridge-post reed** — Old Tam sits on his stone at the crossing at dusk. Tam and Joram Pellow kept the night watch on the Flume bridge. A reed on the bridge-post sang back to Joram's note "when the wind was right". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the bridge watch and the bridge-post reed). Source: P6 (§1 d_tam_*); P9 (Old Tam; Pellow quest). Bible: already in SB §Part One — The Narrow (Tam at the Flume crossing). The bridge-post reed is new.
- **The sluice-gate on the Flume** — Tobin sang Essa down "at the sluice-gate on Massday, there being nothing else to put in the ground". In 413 surveyors drove a stake with a glass bell into the bank above the sluice. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin pages 3 and 4). Bible: new.
- **The Carders' mill (idle, fleece uncollected)** — Grey fleece sits in open sacking, "carded and never collected … Nobody has carded it since the engine stopped". "Nell is not carding." In 413 surveyors walked the Flume bank from the mill. Status: **canon (delegated)** (R cites this Phase 9 text as a basis for its sheep ruling). Source: P9 (§The Carders' mill); P6 (Tobin page 4); R (§The Watch Downs become a region). Bible: already in SB §Part One / §Audit (Carders); the idle mill is new.
- **The Greenhall (game additions)** — The Greenhall ledger shelf, the notice board and the Gazette are there. Its clerk sells a house on Cooper's Row for sixty sovereigns ("The clerk bit every coin"). Its board pays the Writ of Clearing. Tobin's register vote ("I wrote 'yes' in the register") and the 414 lease reading took place there. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_house, d_bounty_board; Tobin pages 5 and 10). Bible: already in SB §Part One / §Audit (Greenhall). B1 says the orchard-cantors built it three hundred years ago as the reed-growers' guildhall. The bible is incomplete on that.
- **The empty bell-frame in the Chapel and its chalk** — "They rang in E," the chalk on the empty bell-frame says. Old women say it "like a grace, and [ask] who taught the Hedge to whistle." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_plinth). Bible: new. B1 has "the Chapel of the Pure Note with its empty belfry … a fine peal of six" and no chalk.
- **The watch-house (once a granary)** — Oakhaven's lock-up, "a very quiet cell". "Nobody [had] walked out of the watch-house cell … since it was a granary." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 c_jail, c_escape). Bible: new.
- **North Gate stables** — Horses are hired "out of the North Gate stables by the hour". "A horse. In Oakhaven." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 p_horse). Bible: new.
- **The north field** — The north-east corner of Oakhaven, inside the Hedge, past the Carders' mill. Its last stand of grain is the Last Sheaf. The rest is stubble, "cut weeks ago". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§Invented facts; §The north field). Bible: already in V7b §2 The Last Sheaf (the "north field" is named but not located). The bible is incomplete.
- **The east square market** — It sits in the east square. On Massday it fills with stalls, "awnings the colour of old wine and old moss". The stalls come up at dawn and are gone by dark. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Market day). Bible: new.
- **The Governor's orchard, the Chapel garden and Bettany's yard (the skeps)** — The three places where Bettany's skeps stand. The bees forage "the orchard and the chapel lime". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§Telling the Bees). Bible: new.
- **The Tarrows' house (north-west corner of Oakhaven)** — A shut house in the lane by the north-west corner. It has a front room with a pitch-board, a kitchen, a parlour, Nan's back room, a nursery and Gil's workroom. Since the family left it has "kept" noises: a kettle that never boils and a child counting to seven. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13 (§P13 HOUSE). Bible: new.
- **Hedge-gate (as festival board)** — The Last Sheaf is announced by a sheaf hung on the Hedge-gate. In 419 a Church notice is nailed under it, and after the secret cut a plaited figure hangs beside it until spring. Status: **canon (book)** (V7b §1–2); the notice and second sheaf are game staging. Source: P12 (§The Last Sheaf). Bible: already in V7b §1, §2.

##### Outside the Hedge (the Reach)

- **The clearing, the hound-bed and the iron-bough** — An ordinary oak at the clearing's north-east end, "a stone's throw from where the hounds sleep", is an iron-bough. Tomas has chalked it. Kaelen says the hound-bed outside his hut is the one place where the glass goes quiet. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the location; the quiet in the glass). Source: P9 (Tomas quest; Kaelen "What he hears"). Bible: iron-bough timber is already in V1 §4.4 and §6.5, and ringing timber in V2 §Hazard Table — The Reach. Hound beds are in SB §Part IV. The placement is new.
- **Reed beds by the Flume crossing and in the clearing** — Beds of green wind-reed by the Flume in Oakhaven and in the wet ground of the clearing. A bed regrows each day. The Flume beds "went under with the Hedge" at the Greening. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§Reed beds; §Journal). Bible: already in V5 §Other Reach Figures (Dorran Sedge "kept … the reed-beds"). This sits in tension with SB and B1 on the reeds dying (see Contradictions).
- **The Greenroot road** — The old cart-road leaves the clearing "by the east hedge" and runs three miles to the parish on the Flume, past the reed-beds. "The Church came up it." It is said nobody has gone down it since the summer the third Cantor sent her note. After the Greening the hedge has grown across it. Status: **canon (delegated)** (the location: R's route table records "Greenroot is a map … three miles east by the road"). Source: P9 (§2 Access); R (§The route). Bible: already in V5 §Status (road graves) and SB §Part One ch7 (the Church comes up it). Partly contradicted (see Contradictions).
- **Greenroot, "the third parish"** — A village of seven houses and thirty-one souls on the Flume. It has a chapel on the knoll to the east, a charnel house with a stair to the ossuary, a well and a village green. In 417 its people went quiet one by one. Every place is laid and every door shut, and no bodies are shown. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§The Third Parish, §2–3). Bible: already in SB §The Resonant Reach ("Greenroot Hamlet lies downriver"), V2 §Settlements (Greenroot Hamlet, 400 souls) and V5 §Frostmonth 419 at a glance. **Contradicted** (see Contradictions).
- **Greenroot's chapel spire (on Oakhaven's north-east skyline)** — It is painted on the skyline from Oakhaven, and sleeves ruled the panorama reachable. Greenroot is listed as "already reachable". Status: **canon (ruled)**. Source: R (§The panorama becomes reachable; §The route table). Bible: new.
- **The Chapel of Greenroot (interior)** — Barred from inside by a bar on a wall lever. Whoever barred it "went down the stair after, and did not come back up". Inside: a long table laid for thirty-one, a pew with a folded shawl, the lectern with the register and a reed across it, the Cantor's kettle and cloak, and a rack of tuning-reeds with "one slot for each house … Every slot is full." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§4 The Chapel of Greenroot). Bible: new. V2 gives Greenroot "a chapel with a silent reed" only.
- **The Greenroot Undercroft and ossuary** — The charnel stair goes down to the ossuary, "where Greenroot keeps its old dead". There are carved verses, the Pell family's passing-bells, a grooved reed-stone that opens to the Root note, a reed gate with a bronze counterweight, a sluice valve "green with a century of damp", reed-hung bone niches, and three chairs facing a fourth where a family held the Last Note. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§6). Bible: new.

##### Whisper-Grove, the Deep Woods and the quarry

- **The Sunken Court (Whisper-Grove)** — The court of lit water where "thirty voices" were singing when the Silencing came. Its water rises once a year for the Drowned Lanterns. A Light-glass shard stands there. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the name). Source: P6 (light-glass echo sh_wg; dream "Evening in the Court"); P12 (Drowned Lanterns); P14. Bible: new as a name. SB §Part Three ch14 has "a plaza under a foot of lit water", and V5/V6 have the Plaza of the Willows. The bible should say whether the Sunken Court and the Plaza are the same place.
- **The Weeping Canopy (Canopy candles)** — On the first Memoryday of Ashfall "someone has set a few small candles at the foot of the Weeping Canopy anyway, facing in". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Canopy). Bible: already in V1 §III.7, V2 and V5 (Weeping Canopy), and V7b §3 (window candles). The Grove candles are new.
- **The Cloister of the Lost Choir (north-west windows)** — The Cloister has tall windows on its north-west side, where sun shafts fall by day. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§P11 AIR, invented world facts). Bible: already in SB §The Spectral Woods (the Cloister); the windows are new.
- **The Whisper Catacombs (mirror rows)** — Brass mirror-lanterns steer a beam row by row ("Beam north, row one point five"). "A mirror forgets what it was for." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (echoes sh_cat, lp_cat). Bible: already in SB §The Spectral Woods (Whisper Catacombs of the Phantom Weaver); the mirrors are new.
- **The Malting (intake and Tuning Room)** — An intake desk ("Next, please … The name, as it is currently held … There is no cost to you"), and a press at the back that Bettis Renn keeps her back to. Under the Tuning Room floor "something a very long way down was keeping count". Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part Three ch18–19; V6 §4.1); the wording is game text. Source: P6 (echo sh_mal; Bettis letter); P12 (rod journal). Bible: already in SB §Part Three; V6.
- **The Mausoleum of Chords (bells rung from the highest)** — Bells are rung "from the highest, then. Down". "The last is not cast in stone." It lies "a mile north" of the Deep Woods camp. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the bell-ringing order and the distance). Source: P6 (echo sh_mau; epilogue lb_pending). Bible: already in SB §The Spectral Woods; V5 §The House of the Silent Veil.
- **The Deep Woods shrine lamp and gift lamp** — An old keeper tends a lamp in the Deep Woods "for them. Somebody always comes by." Another lamp holds a child singing loud and out of tune: "Mama says the loud one is the one that gets heard." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (echoes lp_shr, lp_gift). Bible: new. SB §The Tongueless ("children who sing in the woods as hush-gifts") is close.
- **The Office cutter wreck, iron rings in old oak, and the line cache** — In the Deep Woods, iron rings "set deep in old oak, bright on the inside where a line has run" let a grapnel haul you across. A cache is wedged in rock "by someone who came here by line". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§RIGGING). Bible: the cutter is already in SB §Part Four ch21; the rings and cache are new.
- **The Oakhaven quarry (headframe fence, third landing)** — In 414 the headframe was fenced, and "even the fence hums". "Below the third landing the walls begin to be alive", green-gold and veined. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (these details). Source: P6 (Tobin pages 5 and 9). Bible: already in SB §Part One ch5 (headframe, green-lit shaft).
- **The Root-vault Casing** — Kaelen's resonance rod "stopped dead. The Root does not answer. It listens." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the wording; the place is canon). Source: P12 (rod journal). Bible: already in SB §Part Two.

##### Beyond the Reach

- **Registry Square and its coffee stall** — Grey buildings, brass plates, and "a coffee stall that has been there sixty years". The Clerk of Intake's memo says the stall "remains sixty years old and unaffected". Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (§1 x_organum_office; bookshelf o_memo; dream "Registry Square"; map notes). Bible: already in V4 §The city and §Hooks, and V6 §3.6 (Ambrose's stall).
- **The Hall of Carriage** — It "does not appear on the public plan and should not be asked after at the counter". Sable places WG-41005's record on its third floor: "I know the cabinet." Status: **canon (book)** (the Hall); the cabinet line is minor **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (o_memo); P9 (Sable "The boy in the Cloister"). Bible: already in V3b §Naming and places and V6 §3.3. There is a minor conflict (see Contradictions).
- **The Organum (the city plays the hour)** — The city plays the hour every hour, audible thirty miles off. On the day the Edict of Symmetry was proclaimed it played for nine hours. Visitors from the west may find their reeds do not answer it, and those whose reeds do answer must report to a window. "A seat, not a realm"; the Keynote lies beneath it. Status: **canon (book)** (V6 §3.1; SB §The Organum). The reed-answering rule is **[PROPOSED]** detail. Source: P6 (rumours x_organum_*; o_notice; Codex "The Organum (hearsay)"). Bible: already in SB §The Organum; V4; V6 §3.1. The reed detail is new.
- **Broadsheet Row, Port Cantabile** — "Prints four papers, all libellous, and one of them is occasionally correct." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (§1 x_cantabile). Bible: already in V2 §Settlements (Tempest Archipelago).
- **Bastion Fortis, the Titan's Spine and the Lodestone Vaults** — A fortress cut into the Titan's Spine above the Lodestone Vaults. Every skyship's lodestone core is Fortis-cut, and the Bastion keeps "the longest unbroken watch-rota in history". Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (Codex "Bastion Fortis (hearsay)"; rumours x_fortis, x_lodestone). Bible: already in SB §The Iron Peaks; V1 §III.4; V2 §Realm IV.
- **The Spine wall and its bells; the mist that takes buckles** — Brannoch keeps "the third bell on the Spine wall … the cold one". The Spine's mist "pulls my buckle sideways" ("Leave your buckles at the foot. The mist takes metal sideways"). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (p_letter; map note "peaks"). Bible: new. SB has "lodestone pits under magnetic mist".
- **Null-Watch, Rampart Two and the Rift winch-house** — "Rampart Two, lead battery." A winch-house lowers baskets into the Rift. "A dawn gun" fires every morning. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Rampart Two, the winch-house). Source: P6 (r_line, r_baskets; map note "rift"). Bible: Null-Watch, its batteries and the dawn Refusal are already in SB, V1 §III.8 and V6 §1. "Rampart Two" is new terminology; V6 uses numbered towers and batteries.
- **The Sandglass Bazaar** — "They sell you an hour and you owe it back." Status: **canon (book)** (SB: "hour-merchants buy and sell hours of life"). Source: P6 (§1 x_sandglass). Bible: already in SB §The Shifting Dunes; V1 §III.5.
- **Cinderhold's furnaces** — "The furnaces at Cinderhold have not gone cold since before the Fracture." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 x_cinderhold). Bible: Cinderhold is already in SB §The Blighted Caldera; this detail is new. (SB's "Cold Burn freezes fires" sits uneasily beside it.)
- **The Wick House (mirror)** — Halder Wick's house holds a tall mirror: "The glass is clear, which is more than anything else in the house is." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P14 (§PROPS). Bible: new.

#### From Phases 15–25 (B)

##### The Watch Downs region (Phase 20, promoted by the Rulings)

- **The Watch Downs (region)** — A chalk-downland map west of the clearing, outside the Hedge, reached by a hurdle in the thorn ("A hurdle in the thorn, and a sheep-track up onto the Watch Downs"). It was ruled up from a one-map walk into the first map of a new region. That first map keeps danger 0 as "the quiet way in", and the region's later maps may have people, items, enemies and quests. French name: *Les Hauts du Guet*. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Two rulings that grow the game › The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 1; §2 Map name…). Bible: new. The bibles never describe the country west of the clearing.
- **The Long Down and its beacon cairn** — Chalk and flint piled round an iron fire-basket. In Phase 20 the ash in it is "years old". In Phase 22 it becomes the northern of the three fold-fires, laid with dry furze. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Beacon cairn); Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`cairnHint`, `absalom.topic`). Bible: new.
- **The beacon's view** — From the Long Down: the Grey Teeth to the north; to the south-east, over the Hedge, the roofs of Oakhaven and "the Chapel's bell-tower, its belfry open to the weather". It was found that Medium graphics did not draw the belfry. By delegated decision the far town is now drawn at least 84 units out (`FAR_MIN`, PR #45), and the text is unchanged. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 #2; §Found while checking: the beacon's view on Medium, Decided (delegated)). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 2). Bible: new.
- **The Grey Teeth** — Mountains visible north from the Long Down. Phase 20 says `js/maps.js` already places them beyond the Hedge. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 #2). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 2). Bible: new.
- **Hedge Knoll** — From the knoll the Hedge is "a dark line along the bottom of the world". Oakhaven shows as roofs behind it: the Greenhall dome, the mooring mast and the Chapel's tower. "Nothing hangs in the tower. You can see daylight through the frame." The owner asked whether the line needs a condition. Claude measured the view in the browser and found it correct before and after the Rite: the lamp shows only on the tower's south face, and the knoll sees the north and west faces. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region; §Checked: the Hedge Knoll line). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Hedge Knoll). Bible: new. The Greenhall is in Series Bible (canon).md §Part One — The Narrow; its dome is new.
- **The Scar** — A sheer chalk crag with flint "like knuckles", no way up, "nothing up there to want". Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 The Scar). Bible: new.
- **The Downs sheepfold** — Dry stone with the gate gone and thistles inside: "Somebody kept it mended for a long time, and then stopped." The delegated sheep ruling explains why. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Sheepfold). Bible: new.
- **The dew-pond** — Clay-lined and "older than the Hedge". Rain still fills it, and "nothing drinks from it that you can see". In Phase 29 it gives a drink once a day (out of scope). Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Dew-pond). Bible: new.
- **The boundary stones** — Three leaning stones. "Whatever parish it marked the edge of, the lettering is gone under the lichen." Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Boundary stones). Bible: new.
- **The hawthorns of the Downs** — "A hawthorn, combed flat by the wind." Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Hawthorns). Bible: new.
- **The Downs finger-post (THE WATCH DOWNS / SHEEP-WALK)** — One arm left, reading THE WATCH DOWNS, with SHEEP-WALK cut small beneath. The first text: "There are no sheep. The turf is short anyway, as if it remembers them." Once Tamar's flock is home, the line changes to "There are Farrant ewes on it again, and the turf is short because of them." The changed line is Phase 22's and is **[PROPOSED]**. Status: **canon (ruled)** (the post and its first line: Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Finger-post); Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`downsSign`). Bible: new.
- **Unused Downs dry-stone wall line** — "A dry-stone wall across the slope, with a gap for a stile." This was `TX.wall`, which was never shown. Status: **cut or changed**. `TX.wall` was deleted by delegated housekeeping (Rulings §Housekeeping (Decided, delegated)). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Unused). Bible: new.
- **The Hedge as a story boundary, and the route rules** — The Hedge stays a story boundary, and everything beyond it is reached from outside it, never from Oakhaven. There are four rules:
  1. Nothing new joins Oakhaven.
  2. A new place joins only the map it is seen from, or a new map beside it, and only one that is already reachable.
  3. Every new map passes the no-softlock check and the byte-identical-when-off check.
  4. A painted landmark stands at its real bearing.

  Status: **canon (delegated)** (Rulings §The route (Decided, delegated)). Source: Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md. Bible: new as a rule. The Hedge itself is in Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach.

##### Panorama landmarks made reachable (Rulings)

- **Farmsteads, barns and orchards round Oakhaven** — Painted on Oakhaven's skyline, and ruled to become the Watch Downs region's country (order 1). Phase 22 built them as Wethercombe. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The panorama becomes reachable; §The route table). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 6). Bible: new.
- **The snapped iron pylon** — On the clearing's skyline at bearing 2.07 (south-south-west), reached from the Downs region going south (order 1). Its existence is ruled canon, and "what the pylon … was is up to whoever builds" it. For Phase 22's identity for it, see *Pylon Hill*. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The panorama becomes reachable: "the pylon and the stone ring are canon"). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 6). Bible: new.
- **The standing-stone ring** — On the clearing's skyline to the east (bearing 0.11), to be reached off the Greenroot road (order 2). It is not built yet, and what it was is undecided. Release Readiness lists it as the sixth new area to build. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The panorama becomes reachable). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 6); Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§3 item 6). Bible: new.
- **The broken tower stump in the Deep Woods** — On the Deep Woods' skyline, reachable only from the Deep Woods (order 3). What it was is undecided. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The panorama becomes reachable). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 6). Bible: new.
- **Greenroot's chapel spire** — On Oakhaven's skyline to the north-east. It is "already reachable": Greenroot is a map three miles east by the road (`js/parish.js`). The look from afar is invented. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The route table, "done"). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 6). Bible: Greenroot Hamlet is already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Settlements ("a mill, a chapel with a silent reed") and Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach. The spire is new, and the bible says nothing on its look.
- **The broken colonnade and marble spires round Whisper-Grove** — On Whisper-Grove's skyline, reachable only from Whisper-Grove (order 3). Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §The panorama becomes reachable). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 6). Bible: new. The bible has Whisper-Grove's marble plazas (Vol I §III.7) but no colonnade or spires.

##### Wethercombe and the Sheep-Walk (Phase 22a)

- **Wethercombe** — A chalk combe south of the Watch Downs and west of Oakhaven, reached only by a new stile in the Downs' south hedge, down a drove-road. Tamar calls it "the sheep-walk's door: the fold, the fair, the Bellwether for the drovers". Everyone went inside the Hedge when it went up, and Tamar is the first back. It holds the farmsteads ruled onto Oakhaven's skyline. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new; `combe`, `stileIn`, `tamar.combe`). Bible: new.
- **The Wethercombe finger-post** — It reads WETHERCOMBE at the foot of the drove-road. "The other arm points south, and someone has painted over its word. Under the paint you can still read PYLON." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`sign`). Bible: new.
- **Pylon Hill and the pylon (WESTERN TALLY LINE — STATION 6)** — Iron lattice snapped two-thirds of the way up, with a single wire running down to an iron box. The box's brass plate reads CONSORTIUM OF BORES — WESTERN TALLY LINE — STATION 6. The wire carried the bore's pitch and still hums, and the hum draws hounds as a "lodestone". The line went dead at both ends in 417. Phase 22 Question 2 asks whether to keep the tally-line identity, and it was never answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the identity; the pylon's existence is **canon (ruled)**). Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new; `pylonHum`, `pylon.body`, `pylon.done`, `items.tally.desc`). Bible: new. The Consortium of Bores is in Series Bible (canon).md §The Iron Synod.
- **The Bellwether (drovers' inn)** — The drovers' house on the sheep-walk: "Eight hundred sheep a fair-day went past that door." Gideon Thrale had it from his uncle, shut it the night the Hedge closed, and reopened it the week Tamar came back. Its board shows a ram with a bell at his throat, in new paint on an old board. It has a mutton-fat fire, rooms at a sovereign, cider, and an orchard. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`inn`, `innDoor`, `innSign`, `gideon.topic`, `gideon.normal`). Bible: new.
- **The Farrants' farm and fold** — A door with AT THE FOLD pinned to it, and a fold with a hurdle. Nearby are a barn barred from inside ("hay to the roof beams, and the smell of a winter that never came"), a shut shepherd's cottage with a hole in the thatch, and a woodpile of furze faggots cut for the beacons. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`farmDoor`, `barnDoor`, `cotDoor`, `woodpile`). Bible: new.
- **Wethercombe's dressing (well, skeps, wool-cart)** — Things to look at in the combe:
  - The combe well, its bucket scrubbed and hung back.
  - Straw bee-skeps: "The bees came back before the people did."
  - A haystack roped against the wind.
  - A wool-cart with one wheel off, "the bed still grey with lanolin".
  - Harebell, scabious and wild thyme.
  - A bench on the Knap with a view of the fold, the orchard, the brook and the Ram.

  Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`well`, `skep`, `hay`, `cart`, `flowers`, `bench`). Bible: new.
- **The Wether brook, the Wether spring and the ford** — A cold chalk brook with every stone on its bed visible. The spring "comes out of the chalk as clear as glass" over a lip of flint. Stepping stones cross the brook. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`brook`, `spring`, `ford`). Bible: new.
- **The Wethercombe orchard** — Apple trees with propped branches and lanterns on stakes. The red Wethercombe apple ("cold and sharp") comes "off the Bellwether's orchard". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`tree`, `treePick`, `lantern`, `items.apple`). Bible: new.
- **Hob's Knap** — The eastern fold-fire hill, above the orchard. All three fires can be seen from it. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`absalom.intro`, `absalom.topic`). Bible: new. Do not confuse it with Hob Wendle.
- **The shepherd's hut and the surveyor's tent** — A shepherd's hut on iron wheels holds a bed-shelf, a broken lantern, and "a tally-stick with notches up to forty and then no more". A surveyor's tent's canvas is stamped with a Consortium serial; its stakes are rusted in, and a stool lies "where somebody got up in a hurry". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`hut`, `tent`). Bible: new.
- **The Chalk Ram (hill figure)** — A ram cut in Wethercombe's west scarp, grown over for ten years and marked by six survey stakes: nose, horn, chest, back, haunch and tail. Scoured, it shows white "the way he was cut before anyone wrote anything down", visible from the whole valley. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new; `kit.intro`, `scour.*`, `ramDone`). Bible: new.
- **Ewe Barrow (exterior)** — "A long mound older than any name for it", on the west ridge and old mound. Its door is a grey sarsen slab "set in the chalk and not meant to move", and someone has laid a sprig of thyme on the sill. It is also the western fold-fire. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`barrow`, `journal.fires`, `absalom.topic`). Bible: new.

##### The Hanger and Ewe Barrow (Phase 22b)

- **The Hanger** — A beech wood on the steep ground behind Wethercombe, reached by a gap in the combe's south hedge. It has fog, a charcoal-burners' ring cold for years (a turf hut fallen in, a rake with no teeth), and a pool "black as tea" where "something long and faintly lit turns over". It also has the wool-tree, a hollow beech stump, and the Woolwight's nest. The ROADMAP says about 600 trees. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§22b What is new; `hanger`, `gapIn`, `ring`, `pool`, `stump`). Bible: new. Vol I's "Hanger" match is unrelated.
- **The wool-tree** — "A beech hung all over with tufts of grey wool, as if a flock had been dragged through it. There is no flock." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`woolTree`). Bible: new.
- **The Woolwight's nest** — "A nest the size of a cart, of grey wool: fleece after fleece … snagged with Hedge-thorn. It is not sheep's wool off a sheep. It is wool that has been in the ground." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`nest`, `nestEmpty`). Bible: new.
- **Ewe Barrow (interior)** — The sarsen moves once the Chalk Ram is white and the three fold-fires burn. Inside are:
  - traps: a barbed tripwire, a snare net and alarm-bell wire;
  - lantern-bells as the only light;
  - an antechamber with the barrow shears;
  - grave goods: a torc, amber beads and a lunula;
  - the barrow helm and the leaf-blade;
  - in the last chamber, the Old Fleece, with Kit Farrant behind it.

  Absalom: "The shepherds buried somebody in there before there were shepherds." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§22b What is new; `sarsenOpen`, `absalom.barrow`, `foundJournal`); `js/game.js` TRAP_TYPES. Bible: new.

##### The Flume, Stillwater and the Low Rings (Phase 16)

- **The sluice-arch gate (Oakhaven's Hedge door along the Flume)** — "The way along the Flume." After the Greening: "The Hedge has grown across the sluice-arch … Whatever lay along the Flume is under the green now." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§P16 FLUME › Oakhaven gate). Bible: new.
- **The Flume Road** — Outside the Hedge, east of the Flume crossing, the Flume climbs uphill in a channel of grown wood, "humming one low note". There is a path beside it, with grown-wood stakes that ring like bells and draw hounds, and plum-sized beads of green sap-light that hum "very slightly flat". The Flume goes on over the ridge toward the quarry. A Timber Compact sign reads: THE WASH. WHEN THE NOTE CLIMBS, GET IN A POCKET. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (as a map). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Flume Road). Bible: the Flume as a living, uphill-pumped aqueduct is already in Vol I - The Expanded World Bible.md §III.1 and Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach; the road is new. Release Readiness and Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §Other Reach Figures place the canon beekeeper Ennis Crowle on the Flume road.
- **The Wash** — A cutting on the Flume Road where the Flume "over-pumps": "A wall of green comes down the Wash" and walks travellers back down the road without hurting them. There are pockets in the cutting to shelter in. The third pier is hollow: a knot in its south face turns, and a tunnel runs under the surge, with a smuggler's strongbox in the middle. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Wash surge; `pellam.tip`). Bible: new. The over-pumping Flume is canon in Vol II §Settlements (the Low Rings entry).
- **Stillwater Landing** — A smugglers' wharf of grown planking on a basin of green water at the Flume's lower lock. Reach timber leaves for Fortis and Cantabile broadsheets come in. The lockkeeper sells the same news to six parties. Game details:
  - the board "LOCK DUES PAID IN ADVANCE … NO QUESTIONS, PLEASE, ABOUT THE BOARD";
  - the lockkeeper's house, where he trades on the porch;
  - a cooperage with new hoops;
  - a lock-wheel chained to its post, with a grown-timber sluice;
  - a raft of forty iron-bough logs chalked for Fortis.

  Status: **canon (book)**. Phase 16 wrongly marks it [PROPOSED]; the wharf details are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Stillwater Landing). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Settlements (STILLWATER LANDING), and the game matches it. The bible has no lock-wheel, raft or cooperage, so it is incomplete there.
- **The Low Rings** — "Oakhaven's lower town, flooded when the Flume began to over-pump, and left." Roofs and upper floors stand out of knee-deep green water, with boards laid roof to roof and Mire-drones in the lanes. Its rooms:
  - a chandler's loft of unlit wicks;
  - a counting room with ledger-desks bolted down;
  - a clerk's desk pencilled LEAVE THE WATER ALONE;
  - a loft with a warm stove, four bowls and a child's reed that still sounds.

  Status: **canon (book)**. Phase 16 wrongly marks it [PROPOSED]; the rooms are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Low Rings). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Settlements, as one of Oakhaven's four quarters ("flooded lower city, abandoned since the Flume began over-pumping"). The game reaches it from outside the Hedge; see Contradictions.
- **The hidden household of the Low Rings** — Someone "who never left" lives out of sight in a dry loft, with a warm stove, four bowls, a table too low for a grown man, and a child's reed tuned "to someone small" that sounds in a room with no wind. The player can leave food on the stove. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (`rings.loft`, `rings.reed`, `rings.stove*`, `rings.gift`, `rings.table`). Bible: new.

##### Under Whisper-Grove (Phase 16 DEEP)

- **The Lower Plazas** — The drowned offices of the Synod's Restorative Administration, under the deep pool of the Sunken Court. A stair goes on down "where the pool should end", with a painted hand on the wall reading LOWER PLAZAS. RESTORATIVE ADMINISTRATION. Air is caught under some vaults, and lilac weed grows lit from inside. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Phase 16 DEEP). Bible: new as a place. "Restorative administration" is canon in Vol I - The Expanded World Bible.md §III.7 and Vol II §Settlements (The Cordon).
- **Well Three and the station rooms** — The rooms of Whisper-Grove Station:
  - WELL THREE ("STAFF ONLY");
  - a hall of notices whose Office ink has not run;
  - the filing hall, with drawers labelled LANTERN ROW, CANDLE STREET, THE STEPS and HOUSEHOLDS, BY NUMBER;
  - the clerks' room, with four coats buttoned on four chairs;
  - a stair past a painted line, WATER, 409;
  - the depot of loaded handcarts and crates stencilled WHISPER-GROVE STATION;
  - a small office with a stove;
  - RETURNS. UNCLAIMED: shoes in pairs tied by the laces, a child's coat, spectacles on blank slips.

  Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Rooms; §Things to look at). Bible: new.
- **Lantern Row, Candle Street and the Steps** — Streets of the Whisper-Grove "Loop Quarters", filed by household number. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Things to look at; §Notices). Bible: new. The term "Loop Quarters" is also new; the loops themselves are canon in Vol I §III.7 and Vol V §The Shortening of the Loops.

##### Oakhaven, the Chapel and the quarry (Phase 16 HEIST)

- **The Chapel of the Carillon's vestry and undercroft** — The Church's house in Oakhaven. Phase 16 established it as the Synod's only house there, in place of the brief's "cathedral". The undercroft is reached from the vestry door, which has a brass tumbler lock. It holds:
  - a robing room (grey-and-white choir robes, a deacon's stole);
  - a back stair from the robing room, with no lamp;
  - west, middle and east aisles of pigeonholes;
  - a vestry ledger headed HELD;
  - the sexton's cot;
  - a coal-hatch to the lane, barred from inside;
  - hush-lamps;
  - the Held Cabinet at the far end.

  Phase 16 asked the author whether the Chapel could stand in for the cathedral, and no ruling was given. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (Questions 3; §Phase 16 HEIST; §The vestry and undercroft). Bible: new. The bible names no "Chapel of the Carillon", though the Chapel's empty bell-frame is game canon.
- **Depot Three: the Factor's counting room** — A plain door at the south-east corner of the quarry depot, sealed DEPOT THREE IS BEING RECONCILED. STAFF ONLY. Beyond it:
  - a passage and stair down to a counting floor of desks in rows;
  - a ledger room with THE UNNUMBERED chalked on the wall;
  - a strongroom, with Vosk's brass safe in its dark west half;
  - a bore gallery and the bore's iron shaft ("not tuned to anything you could name");
  - pumps;
  - a service hatch.

  The shift board reads DAY SHIFT OFF AT THE TENTH HOUR … NIGHT BELL AT THE TENTH. BORE BITES: EIGHT BREATHS IN TWENTY-TWO … MORNING BELL AT THE SIXTH. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the counting room). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Ines Tarn, the shift board…; §The Factor's safe…). Bible: Depot Three is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part One — The Narrow; the interior is new.
- **The Quarry Gate** — The Synod-held gate where Sergeant Vause counts heads ("Quarry Administration, Quarry Gate"). Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Depot Three: … Sergeant Vause). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Settlements ("Quarry Gate (Synod-occupied, barracked, hated)").
- **The Greenhall (moot seat)** — Reeve Mossbrook is found "at the Greenhall". A refugee says three parishes were called to the Greenhall, two came, and "the third sent a note saying they had stopped being able to send". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the uses); the Greenhall is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Factor's safe… "the Reeve at the Greenhall"); Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Families on the road, shared 2). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part One — The Narrow (ch. 2 Greenhall scene); the parish summons is new.
- **Ketter's Ford** — A refugee: "The reeds at Ketter's Ford stopped sounding on Memoryday. Not one of them." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Families on the road, shared 1). Bible: new.
- **The south road out of the Reach** — Families from the outlying farms leave by the south road, before the Greening. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Families on the road, journal). Bible: new.

##### The Drift-Kestrel (Phase 16 KESTREL; Phase 20)

- **The Drift-Kestrel walked** — A deck, hold, galley, bunk room and tank room. On deck are the wheel, polished "by somebody who denies it", a mast "stitched and re-stitched", and an awning over the map table. Below are wall charts of the Reach and the Woods with Mags's pencilled crosses, a stash chest, a chandler's locker, a repair list in Vane's hand, and twenty-one cups on hooks in the mess. She is boarded by a hundred-foot mast-ladder (Sunken Court) or a cargo hatch (Deep Woods camp). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Phase 16 KESTREL). Bible: the ship is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Vane and §Part I — Canon Ledger, and Vol I §PART VIII; the walkable layout is new. The 21 cups echo the 21 survivors of Series Bible (canon).md §Part Two — The Root, ch. 13.
- **The Kestrel's three lift-tanks** — Number one is "the oldest … and the steadiest". Number two is "closed with weaverling silk and an Office valve", hums a little flat, and is the one whose hum you must never stop hearing. The new tank is built up from the Office cutter's and lets the ship make the forty-mile hop between the Deep Woods and the Sunken Court. A gauge "has said forty since Oakhaven". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Props; §Upgrades; §Pennick). Bible: lift-tanks are in Series Bible (canon).md (§Antagonists and Key Figures; §Part Four — Coda, ch. 21 "A holed lift-tank"); the tank numbering is new. See Contradictions: Book One holes number three.
- **The Drift-Kestrel in flight (look)** — Two red pennants at the mastheads, lit stern windows, and a brass lift-tank slung along her side. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 #5). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 5). Bible: new as a look.

##### Port Cantabile (Phase 17 extra, set 7 Ashfall 419)

- **The Long Mole, the derrick and the Mole-bell** — Granite blocks with iron rings, "the whole arm hums faintly". The derrick at the Mole-head is where the Articles are enforced. The Mole-bell is rung once for each soul that goes into the water, "then nobody speaks, so the harbour can count". Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§A trip to Port Cantabile, strings 12–13, 34, 124). Bible: already in Vol IV - The Hush, the Organum, the Rust Coast.md §The town as written and Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §Customs (Rust Coast).
- **The Salt Stair and its landings** — Six wet flights of forty-one steps up the cliff, with a gutter carrying the cistern's overflow, an iron-stanchion rail (the drop runs from 30 feet on the first flight to 190 on the sixth), and lamps lit one landing at a time from the bottom at dusk. The landings:
  1. Rope and tar, shuttered: "BACK AT THE TURN".
  2. A shuttered bottle shop with a chalk mark rubbed at.
  3. Fenwick Sloe's chandlery, with "a diving-helm … new in his grandfather's time".
  4. Marrin Tace's door.
  5. A net-loft and "the best view on the Stair".
  6. The top: wet flags, the cistern of green slate, and the counting-house.

  Status: **canon (book)** for the Stair, the 41-step flights and landings one to three (Book Two ch. 3); **[PROPOSED]** for landings four to six, the cistern and the bench. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 9, 25–29, 36–49). Bible: already in Vol IV §Errata against Volume II and §The town as written, and Series Bible (canon).md §Part One · The Rust Coast (ch. 3). The landing-by-landing shops are only in the Book Two manuscript (Part One Ch1-3), so the bible is incomplete there.
- **The counting-house** — Square stone, slate roof and iron shutters, with a brass sun-disc and the Church's flame over the door. The game calls it "the oldest building in Port Cantabile, built by the Synod's tax-farmers to count what the harbour owed, and given to the Church", held by the Flambeaux "since the spring". It stays shut by rule, because the Book Two murder is not staged. Status: **canon (book)** for the building and the Flambeau tenancy; **[PROPOSED]** for its history. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 53–55, 319). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Canon added in Book Two, Part One ("a new Flambeau counting-house … which pays for talk") and Vol IV §The town as written ("the counting-house (the Organum's)"). See Contradictions.
- **The Lantern Yard and Hesper's four houses** — The lower town. The houses are lime-washed and salt-streaked, and Hesper's are marked by rows of small boots. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 21–22, 253). Bible: already in Vol IV §The town as written ("Hesper's four houses. Where the first vox-bottle was opened").
- **The Harbour office** — At the foot of the Long Mole. It holds a pine box whose key "stays with the Harbour-Queen" and "has been used four times in nineteen years", a rack of gaffs and hooks for boarding ships under no hand, and the chalk board of the reef's keys and mooring dues. A sign outside: THE HARBOUR OFFICE. Dues, bench and bell. THE ARTICLES ARE READ AT NOON. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 23, 73–82). Bible: new as a building. Reading at noon is new; Vol IV has the Articles "read aloud in a flat voice".
- **The mast-tower** — Sixty feet of wet iron rungs, the Kestrel moored at its head. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 10–11, 282). Bible: already in Vol IV §The town as written.
- **Driftwood Cay and its burying-ground** — "A long low island of grey rock and grey scrub", out past the harbour mouth. On its spine the Coast buries its children in earth, "because the town is cut into rock". Each grave has a small bell on an iron hook, and some hooks are bare, "for the ones nobody claimed". Hesper's orphan-house, a jetty and the high rock are there. Status: **canon (book)** (the Cay, the child burials and the bells); **[PROPOSED]** (the orphan-house layout and the jetty). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 96–109, 255). Bible: already in Vol IV §The town as written; Vol V §Customs ("Children's graves are hung with bells"); Vol V §Status (the Cay orphan-house, 20 Frostmonth).

##### The River Gate (Phase 17 prologue, 19 Frostmonth 419)

- **The Greenroot road in snow** — Two days' walk from Greenroot Hamlet, with milestones II–V, bare grey iron-bark oaks that no longer ring, and graves all along it. About 40 graves are finished, and from the fifth milestone on, 10 are bare. Status: **canon (book)** for the road, the snow and the graves (Book Four, Part One, ch. 4); **[PROPOSED]** for the map layout and counts. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§P17 PROLOGUE › Invented facts). Bible: the Greenroot road graves are already in Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §Reach Customs (expanded).
- **The North Gate (sealed)** — "A great iron grille in an arch of stone, rusted shut, with the thorn grown through it." It has been sealed since the Greening, and it is where Ada Pellow went in. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Every player-visible string). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part Two — The Root.
- **The river gate** — Round to the east of the North Gate, where the river goes in under the Hedge. Dorran Sedge kept it for twenty years. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §Other Reach Figures.
- **Oakhaven as a wood ("the Grove")** — Houses wedged 40–80 feet up in the forks of grey trunks "as thick as towers". There is a shop-front reading HALE & DAUGHTER, CORDWAINERS, an iron bed in a fork, and leaves a foot deep that fell "Nullday night … at the dead hour. All at once". The people at Reedside have called it "the Grove" since Ashfall. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Vol V §Status (Reach) and §The Grove of Oakhaven; Vol VII - The Calendar of Aethelgard.md §7 (18 Frostmonth leaf-fall).

##### The Long Night (Phase 17 extra)

- **The Conservatory Orchard and the old stair** — Snow on the iron-bark. A fallen oak, "still faintly ringing", lies across the old orchard wall. Under its roots a slab with an iron ring, "older than the Conservatory", has been lifted, and the narrow cut stair beyond has a rotted hemp rail. There is a hollow reed-orchard tree that Cantors hum into, and a tally-board of nights kept. Status: **canon (book)** for the stair, the slab, the ring and the humming custom; **[PROPOSED]** for the fallen oak and the tally-board. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Phase 17 LONGNIGHT › Orchard props). Bible: already in Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §The Old Stair and the Gallery and §The Cantors' Secret.
- **The Gallery above the Root-vault** — A waist-high stone rail worn smooth, and below it the Root, "a hundred thousand roots twisted into one like a rope, grey, with green only in the deepest places … the coldest place in the Reach". Also there: "the Synod's fill, where the bore came through", and an oil niche by the east lamp. Status: **canon (book)** for the gallery and the Root's look (wording follows Vol V); **[PROPOSED]** for the niche and lamps. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Gallery props). Bible: already in Vol V §The Old Stair and the Gallery.
- **The Keeper's Stone** — "Five notches in it, worn by knees." The verse is sung there. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Gallery props). Bible: new.

##### Generated and other places

- **Daily-challenge dungeon floors** — Generic floor names: the Undercroft, the Old Cellars, the Ossuary, the Drowned Chapel, the Glass Cistern and the Old Cuttings. They are dressed with "a lamp of green-gold glass, burning without oil" and "a broken statue. Its face has been worn away by hands." Status: **[PROPOSED]** ("generic places, no story names"). Source: Phase 18 - New In-Game Text.md (§Phase 18 DAILY). Bible: new.
- **Release Readiness candidate areas (not built at the time)** — Best first:
  1. The Hollow through the tannery.
  2. The Carders' house and root-cellar.
  3. The Moot-house in Whisper-Grove (Sable's gallery).
  4. The Bell Yard and Ashlane (Halder Wick's and Ivo Renn's homes).
  5. The Governor's house (Kaelen's birthplace, the "aired room").
  6. The standing-stone ring.
  7. The Reedside Conservatory and reed orchard, with the old stair kept sealed for Book Four.
  8. The Deep Grove.

  The ROADMAP shows some were later built (Phase 29, out of scope). Status: **canon (book)** for the Hollow, Reedside and the Deep Grove (bible places); **[PROPOSED]** for the Bell Yard and Ashlane as a map. Source: Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§3). Bible: the Hollow is in Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach; the Deep Grove in Vol II §Settlements ("No path leads in that is the same way twice"); Reedside in Vol II §Settlements. The Bell Yard and Ashlane are new.

---

#### From Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

- **The Hollow** — Oakhaven's burl-sick ward: the lanes behind the tannery, reached through the tannery's back door. Thirty-one households and eighty-four souls live behind barred doors that hum, most chalked with the sap-mark, around a well-head where Rooke's ward-book hangs on a chain. At the Greening the whole Hollow goes under bramble. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 326, "the lanes behind the tannery, where the town kept its burl-sick"; ch. 7, l. 2397, "Thirty-one households... Eighty-four souls, by Surgeon-Grafter Rooke's census"; the bramble follows ch. 11, l. 3462). Source: js/hollow30.js, p30-hollow.tsv; commit 9a9cd1a; PLAYTEST "R5a". Bible: already in Series Bible §The Resonant Reach and §Part One — The Narrow, by name only. The bible has no household count, layout or well-head. The canon pass also fixed Ignatius's warrant, which had said "the Hollow in the quarry" (p32-canon.tsv).
- **The tannery's back door** — A barred door from the tannery's vat room into the Hollow, with a slate on the nail: WARD CLOSED TO CALLERS. ROOKE. It was shut in R4 and opened in R5a. After the Greening there is nothing behind it but bramble. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the door itself; the tannery's position is **canon (book)**, ch. 1, l. 326). Source: p29-thin.tsv, p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Carders' house (the blue door) and its root-cellar** — Nell and Pim Carder's house. Inside: a pallet pulled into the middle of the floor away from the damp wall, bricks warming on the hearth-back, a hush-lamp Lyra left in the window, and her chalk note on the chimney-breast. Below is a siphoned root-cellar of soft apples, green-skinned lard and seed potatoes sprouting in the dark, with three hush-mites and a hush-lamp at the stair foot. Status: **canon (book)** (the blue door, Nell and Pim, Rooke's "Keep him out of the damp": MS ch. 3, ll. 913, 999); [the cellar is **[PROPOSED]**, the GDD's Hush-Mite tutorial (hollow30.js header); the site on the north row is **[PROPOSED]** and contradicts the book]. Source: js/hollow30.js, p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: the Carders are in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures; the house is new. **Contradiction:** MS ch. 6, l. 2031 puts the blue door in "the lane behind the tannery", and ch. 11, l. 3462 has it under bramble at the Greening, two doors from Orla's. The game puts it on the north row, between the Greenhall and the granary.
- **Tull's, the baker's** — Oakhaven's bakery on the north row near the North Gate. The loaves rise past their tins and shoulder the oven doors, and the dough in the trough always rises again after it is punched down. Status: **canon (book)** (the bakery and the baker's wife who punches the dough down every hour of the night: MS ch. 1, l. 308; ch. 11, l. 3420) [the name Tull is PROPOSED]. Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv; commit d3b92b1. Bible: the law-rot of "bread that rises until the oven splits" is in Series Bible §Part IV — The Rules of Dread. The bakery is new. Near-collision: Garret Tull (Series Bible §The Twelve).
- **Vettle's, the chandler's** — A chandlery east of the square: tallow dips in pairs, wax for the chapel priced in chalk, barrels of lamp-oil, and a box of tuned hush-whistle reeds on the counter "like sweets". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (thin29.js: "Book One has no Oakhaven chandler"). Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Drift-Kestrel's hold at the Oakhaven mast, and the mast-ladder** — The twelve crates of cores ride in the Kestrel's own hold at the mooring mast, east of the square. Hetty sends Vane's supper up the mast-ladder, and you listen at the crates from the ladder and the open hatch. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 4, ll. 1313, 1339; ch. 8, ll. 2564–2706, the crates stencilled OFFICE OF THE CATALOGUE, consignee "Oakhaven Works, Carillon garrison", thirty-six spheres in four rows of nine) [the supper up the ladder is PROPOSED]. Source: p33-crates.tsv; commit d9fc079. Bible: already in Series Bible §Vane — Corsair of the Rust Coast ("At night the crates say names in the hold") and §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **The Boar & Dulcimer's cellar** — The tavern cellar keeps its casks of small beer and, since R2, Garrow Finch's game of bones, so that the house board upstairs (NO DICE) stays true. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the dice cellar; the board's rules are **canon (book)**, MS ch. 4, l. 1373). Source: p27-r2.tsv, p33-crates.tsv. Bible: new. The Boar itself is in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures (Hetty Bram) and Vol II §Settlements.
- **The cairn by the old stile, under the western oaks** — A moss-grown cairn outside the Hedge, carved T. ASHGROVE, 417, where Oakhaven buried Tobin's lyre, "the only thing of him they found". Under a flat stone at its foot lies his bell-bronze Tuning-Blade, left there on his way up to the Pellow farm, with a slate. The Year 417 board now points to "the western oaks", not "near the culvert". Status: **canon (book)** for the lyre found by the stile (MS ch. 1, l. 464; ch. 6, l. 2135) [the cairn and the blade under it are PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r1.tsv, p27-r2.tsv, p27-canon.tsv. Bible: new. Release Readiness §2 noted "he carried no blade". The game reconciles this: he left the blade under the oaks before going up.
- **The Pellow farm** — The farm "out past where the Hedge runs now" where Tobin sang the cooling Pellow child back at full voice in the spring of 417, and walked out of the kitchen door singing so that the hounds would follow him and not the house. Status: **canon (ruled)** (owner's ruling on the library questions: "Spring 417, at the Pellow farm, before the Hedge (the manuscript)"); also **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, ll. 454–464). Source: p27-canon.tsv, p27-r2.tsv. Bible: Ada Pellow is in Series Bible §Part Two — The Root; the farm by name is new.
- **The Watch Downs** — Open turf west of the clearing, with danger kept at 0. A fold with Abel Sedge, a stack of turves cut square and stacked grass-side down, and a clay-lined dew-pond "older than anyone" where you may drink once a day. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the region is Phase 22's; Author Rulings 93–95 keep it safe). Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Wethercombe: the Hursts' farmhouse, the tithe barn and the shut-up cottage** — The farmhouse has its hearth and the note pinned to the door (AT THE FOLD; "Mother, the soup is on the hook"). The tithe barn holds two years' unsold clip, barred by Jem Cotter against the woolwight. The cottage's letter says its owner has gone to her sister at Fortis. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Bellwether's room and its slate of tabs** — A narrow room under the eaves for three sovereigns, with Gideon's lamp on the stair. The chalk slate reads: HURST 4 — COTTER 11 (WOOL) — TAMAR 2 — ABSALOM 0, HE NEVER DRINKS — THE WOOLWIGHT 1 EWE. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Flume Road and Ennis Crowle's skeps** — Straw skeps bound with bramble on stones along the road, humming "like a kettle thinking about it". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the road); Ennis is canon (see People). Source: js/thin29.js. Bible: Vol V §Other Reach Figures names Ennis Crowle "(beekeeper, Flume road)".
- **The Low Rings and Stillwater Landing** — Flooded streets worked roof to roof by a punt-poler, and a quay where timber goes down to Fortis and Cantabile broadsheets come up: a "smugglers' town". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the people and trade; the settlements themselves are bible canon). Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: Vol II §Settlements: STILLWATER LANDING ("Smugglers' town. Where Reach timber leaves for Fortis and where Cantabile broadsheets come in"). Vol II describes the Low Rings as Oakhaven's own flooded lower quarter, but the game places them by Stillwater.
- **The Moot-house gallery and muniment store (Whisper-Grove)** — A stair from the Moot-house hall climbs to a gallery of three windows over the plaza, where on a Stasisday Sable watched the first proctor stand still for a full hour. At the east end is the room she made for herself in the muniment store. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, ll. 4153, 4195). Source: js/grove31.js, p31-grove.tsv; commit 865a07a. Bible: the Moot-house, its gallery and the muniment store are not in any volume (new). The plaza is the Weeping Plaza in the MS; the game calls it the Sunken Court.
- **The Moot-house cellar** — Barrels of the Moot's stores, and Corwin Aye's barrel. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 16, l. 4556) [the cellar as a room is game staging]. Source: js/grove31.js. Bible: new.
- **The Bell Yard and Ashlane** — The joiners' and coopers' streets off the Sunken Court's west side. The streets are swept, the shutters latched and nothing has fallen down, "the first wrong thing". There is a window-box of dead flowers, three summers deep; lamps the lamplighter lights with a taper though they have had no oil since 416; and carts in the carters' yard with their shafts up, the horses long gone. Proctors walk it. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, ll. 4097, 4105, 4153–4157; ch. 15, l. 4235, the swept streets and dead flowers) [the dry lamps and carts are **[PROPOSED]** detail]. Source: js/grove31.js, p31-grove.tsv. Bible: new (Series Bible gives Halder "of the Bell Yard" only implicitly; the streets are unnamed in the volumes).
- **Halder Wick's house (Bell Yard)** — A joiner's house: a table laid for four under three years of dust with one plate missing (the blue one, which he carries to the plaza), a half-made chair-back in the vice, a pot gone to dust on the hook. Since the canon pass it also has a bare joiner's dresser, four chairs, two beds (one small) and a child's chair mended twice, and nothing in it has a name on it. Status: **canon (book)** (Halder, joiner, of the Bell Yard, taken on the second day in the first hour because the works began at the end of his street: MS ch. 14, l. 4097; the table for four: ch. 15, l. 4435) [the furnishings are PROPOSED]. Source: p31-grove.tsv, p37-halder.tsv. Bible: Halder is in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures and Vol V §The Chorister's List. The house is new.
- **Ivo Renn's house (Ashlane)** — A cooper's house: two bowls and two spoons set for a supper never eaten, a crock of salt between them, oak staves in a rack, an adze, a croze, a sun-plane, and a leather apron with I.R. burned into the strap. Status: **canon (book)** (Ivo Renn, forty-one, cooper, of Ashlane, and Bettis, taken on the fourth day: MS ch. 14, ll. 4105–4109) [the house contents are PROPOSED]. Source: p31-grove.tsv (bowls text revised in f4db2f7). Bible: Ivo is new to the bible; Bettis is in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **The Sunken Court fountain** — Where Sable is now first met, as in ch. 14. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, l. 4089: she comes down the Moot-house steps into the plaza's water) [the game says "fountain"; the MS has a "shallow ornamental basin"]. Source: p40-umber.tsv; commit 3c9b261. Bible: new; the "Sunken Court" is the game's name for the MS's Weeping Plaza.
- **The clearing by the Mausoleum, and the ring of lamps** — North of the Deep Woods camp fire, between silver willows at the edge of the trees, the camp's hush-lamps are set in a ring for the coda. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 23, ll. 6022–6126; Lyra walks out past the last lamp at the second hour, l. 6040). Source: p39-lamps.tsv, js/coda.js; commit 90e2680. Bible: Series Bible §Part Four — Coda mentions the coda; the ring of lamps is not described in any volume.
- **The Mausoleum choir (the Requiem)** — The choir of the House of the Silent Veil, where "something older" than the Wearer sings every funeral office ever sung there in a hundred voices. It is now fought only after the Wearer is dead, as the Requiem. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the game's own branch, kept from the old coda; brainstorm §5 item 8: "Keep the choir for the Requiem branch"). Source: p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Lyra's house: the Church's protection** — From Dawnday sunrise to the Rite, two Legionnaires stand inside Lyra's door. A sister in grey warms honey at the hearth and says "You are very fortunate", and Lyra answers only on her slate. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 7, l. 2317; ch. 9: "They did not let her speak for the last four days"; Series Bible §Book Two Part Two ch. 11 repeats "two kind women, honey and egg, you are very fortunate") [two Legionnaires inside the door is game staging]. Source: js/days35.js, p36-days.tsv. Bible: the rubric is in Series Bible (Book One ch. 9 summary).

### People

#### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

##### Lyra, Kaelen, Vane, Sable

- **Lyra Ashgrove: learning the reeds from Tobin (412–415)** — Tobin's journal has Lyra wanting the reeds in spring 412, learning "the long cane first" by summer, and tuning the First Reed over their door "standing on the chair with her tongue between her teeth". In 415 she holds his blade and calls it "too heavy for a first fifth". Tobin calls her "lark". Status: **[PROPOSED]** ("lark" itself is **canon (book)**, B1). Source: P6 (Tobin pages 1, 2, 6); P9 (Lyra "The reeds", "Tobin"). Bible: already in SB §Lyra Ashgrove. **Contradicted** on age and training (see Contradictions).
- **Lyra: details from her companion talk** — She taught children for years. Ilse Verrow sat behind her at the Conservatory. "Waste is a reed nobody cut." Her rude song is The Miller's Daughter, both verses. She will not answer a man in the dark who calls "lark": "it is not him". The thing wearing Tobin "practised him", and she will not name it "until it gives him back". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (Lyra topics; §[PROPOSED] items). Bible: already in SB §Lyra Ashgrove (base); the details are new.
- **Lyra: her father's falling fifth, the Hedge, "I know"** — She kept her hand on the wool the night the Hedge was planted. "It's listening," she said. Tobin said it was packed wool, and she said "I know". The Hedge "tried my father's fifth on me first." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 11); P9 (Lyra "The Hedge"). Bible: new.
- **Lyra after the coda (Book One epilogues)** — In the current text, singing the coda against the Requiem "cost her what was left of her voice. She talks in chalk now, in capitals". In other variants she rations speech "like salt" or hums the response to Sable's count. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (applied as a review fix in Phase 6). Source: P6 (header note; epilogue "Not Yet" lyra). Bible: already in SB §Part Four ch23–24. **Contradicted** (see Contradictions).
- **Kaelen Vance: facts the game takes from the books** — The Foundry floor on the morning of the Fracture, 412 ("Hold," he said). He took the Synod's money at seventeen. He read to "line ninety-four" and looked away. He keeps a chalked list of names on a beam, "one name a day". Lyra said she "would have forgiven [him] on Rootday, and cannot now" and took the glass hand. He hums the Root note Lyra taught him. Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Kaelen Vance; §Part Two ch13; §Part Three ch19; §Part Four ch23–24). Phase 9 had marked several of these **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (dream "The Foundry Floor"; epilogues); P9 (Kaelen topics). Bible: already in SB §Kaelen Vance and §Part XI.
- **Kaelen: invented details** — Lyra taught him the Root note "when we were nine". He measures the arm in fingers: "Four fingers from the shoulder". His glass hears each person as a note, and yours is "a little sharp when you are lying". The hound-bed is the one quiet place in the glass. "Nobody came to the hut in four years who did not want something broken." He says "It follows pain. The old hurts go first." He can Listen to what a cane has kept. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (Kaelen topics; Pellow quest; §[PROPOSED] items); P6 (epilogue k2_glass). Bible: already in SB §Kaelen Vance (base). The "four years" is contradicted (see Contradictions).
- **Kaelen's first watch and humming** — He takes the first watch, and the worst one. He hums "where she can hear it". He "sleeps on the near side of the fire now". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (epilogues kb_*); P9 (§Small permanent things). Bible: new.
- **Vane: the number, the ship, the axe** — "One thousand three hundred and forty-two. I say it every morning." Mags spliced the lines "for four to a rung. Children first." Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part Three ch16; §Part Two ch12). Source: P6 (Vane letter); P9 ("The crates"). Bible: already in SB §Vane and §Part XI.
- **Vane: invented details** — "I carried twelve crates for two years." He bought the Kestrel "with money I'd rather not discuss, from people I'd rather not name" (canon: Catalogue money). Mended, she flies in forty-mile hops: "up, down, cool the tank, up". He wants "somewhere with a horizon and no paperwork … Port Cantabile". He puts his axe down at the fire, and the cutter's 432 vox-bottles sang "one tune, four hundred ways". He has a tab at the Boar, and Hetty keeps a tally. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Vane letter); P9 (Vane topics; §[PROPOSED] items). Bible: already in SB §Vane (base). The "twelve crates" is contradicted (see Contradictions).
- **Sable: met outside the Hedge (Part One)** — In the game, Sable is met in Part One "out east past the Governor's son's hut, writing in a book by a hush-lamp. She asks strangers for three names.
