The grey men at the gate want her very badly." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_sable_met). Bible: already in SB §Sable (base). **Contradicted**: B1 first meets her in Whisper-Grove.
- **Sable reads a Hollow its name back: Hew Callan** — In one game branch Sable returns Hew Callan's name at the quarry pumps. She then has "my mother's hands in my hair, and a name. The smell of the scriptorium is gone." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (d_sable_returned; Sable letters). Bible: already in SB §Sable and V3b. **Contradicted** (see Contradictions).
- **Sable: the schedule, "in white", Quist's second cup** — "I wrote 'in white' on the Stage Nine form, so the Cantor would not be mistaken for staff. I was proud of the handwriting." "He poured the second cup." Status: **[PROPOSED]** ("in white" is not in B1; designing the schedule is **canon (book)**, SB §Sable). Source: P9 (Sable "The schedule"). Bible: already in SB §Sable (the schedule); the detail is new.
- **Sable: the Veil, eleven in, the stranger's page** — "The Veil records the death of civilisations. We do not hold it back. We do not hurry it." "I counted eleven in. I did not count them out." Status: **canon (book)** (V1 §5.3; SB §Part Four ch22). Source: P9 (Sable "The Veil"); P6 (dream "The Long Desk"). Bible: already in V1 §5.3; SB.
- **Sable: "the boy in the Cloister" (WG-41005)** — "A boy of the Cloister choir. A girl … would not let go of him at intake, and my hand shook, and I made his surname illegible." Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part Three ch17). The "girl in a red cloak" wording is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9. Bible: already in SB §Part Three. This conflicts with SB §The Twelve (see Contradictions).

##### Oakhaven townsfolk

- **Hetty Bram (game additions)** — Her house rule is "Singing is free in here and it will get you killed … It has been since Davin." She has "never lost a patron to a hound. Only to a song." She listens at the corsair's crates in her cellar "with a lamp and my fingers in my ears". She pays the stranger to make Vane hear the names, sends "one of Bettany's tonics", and in time stops charging Vane for small beer. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 Rumours; Hetty letter; Vane letter). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists and Key Figures (base).
- **Orla Hale (game additions)** — Bettany holds the pen for her letter. Davin's boots stand on the chair-rail ("Davin had big feet for a boy"). Lyra "has promised to sing me down proper, before the fire". Status: **canon (book)** (the boots and the promise: SB §Audit, ch3 and ch6). The letter details are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Orla letter; d_boots_done). Bible: already in SB.
- **Anselm Rooke (game additions)** — "This is not a thanks. I do not write those; they are an invitation to be owed." He stood on Orla's step and did not go in. He means to go on "not finding" the Inquisitor's list. He signs "Surgeon-Grafter" and sends linen "boiled twice". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the Inquisitor's list itself is **canon (book)**). Source: P6 (Rooke letter; d_boots). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists (Rooke).
- **Nell Carder (game additions)** — She held the honey jar "like a warm egg". She was up three nights and does not like being owed. She sang the work-song all night at the Hedge-planting in 417. She "is not carding" in 419. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Nell letters; Tobin page 11); P9. Bible: already in SB (base). The Hedge-planting detail was cited by R's delegated sheep ruling.
- **Pim Carder (game additions)** — The Hedge whistles "my whistle". He counts to forty twice and does not go. Roots go back under his skin "like worms from a lamp". Lyra's tending hum "goes up at the end" and the stranger's "goes down". He picks sweet reed from the Flume bank. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Pim letters). Bible: already in SB (base).
- **Old Tam (game additions)** — "Essa did the writing." He gave his second verse "to something on the first night, to see". He is trying a third verse "quietly". He kept the night watch with Joram Pellow for nine winters on the Flume bridge, and he sells an alder flute. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tam letters; rumours); P9 (Pellow quest); P12 (Alder Flute). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists (Tam the night-hummer). **Contradicted** on the nine winters (see Contradictions).
- **Essa, Tam's wife** — She died in the Cascade year, 412. Tobin sang her down at the sluice-gate on a Massday, and Tam asked for The Miller's Daughter, both verses. Her voice now comes up on the Flume water at dusk and "gets the second verse right". Her mother kept hush-whistles. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the name and dates; B1 has Tam's dead wife at the crossing, unnamed). Source: P6 (Tobin page 3; Tam letters; rumours); P9 (bees). Bible: new.
- **Hob Wendle (game additions)** — He signs "Wendle, tanner". He keeps the culvert key on a nail and a lamp in his window. "Leather is quieter than people." He sells a lute "strung with his own gut" and a Reed-Guard Dirk. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Hob letters; rumours); P12 (ARMS; SONGCRAFT). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists. **Contradicted** on his trade (see Contradictions).
- **Joss and Wyn, "the Tanner twins" (Joss Wendle, Wyn Wendle)** — Their coats lie under the Tannery culvert. Writing their "whole names" in your ledger is a deed. In Phase 12 they are named Joss Wendle and Wyn Wendle among the lantern names. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_twins_found, d_twins_named; Hob letter); P12 (§Invented facts). Bible: already in SB §Audit ("the Tanner twins", unnamed, dead before the book). **Contradicted** (see Contradictions).
- **Bettany (game additions)** — She sat on the Greenhall and was the only one to vote against Tobin's blade ("a pair of shears in a sermon"). She brought Tobin honey for his throat. She keeps three straw skeps (her mother's and her grandmother's) and has "forty-one lullabies in me". She makes tonics and sells an iron tuning fork "from a Cantor's widow". She asks that the damper jar not be called the Hush. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin pages 1 and 6); P9 (bees); P12 (KIT; SONGCRAFT). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists (Bettany the midwife); V5.
- **Old Harrow (game additions)** — He keeps the gate key and has hung the Last Sheaf "eleven years running". He organises the secret cut. In 413 Tobin asked him to walk a night-round of the mill. He says hounds "wait for the evening bell". He swears the Hedge said "I'm here" in a voice he came to know. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin pages 4, 10, 11; d_hedge_answered); P12 (§Invented facts; side quest). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists ("old Harrow the gate-warden"). The "eleven years" is contradicted.
- **Governor Aldric Vance (game additions)** — He read the lease aloud in the Greenhall "standing, in his good coat". To Tobin he said "Say it, Tobin" and had "said it every night for a month". Both his hands blistered planting the Hedge. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (that he sold the Root to feed the town is **canon (book)**, SB). Source: P6 (Tobin pages 5 and 11). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists.
- **Tobiah Marsh (game additions)** — Revision Seven was "under my mattress" for three years. Gazette No. 33 commends "Junior Engineer Marsh … for diligence. Commendation is revocable on notice." "If Engineer Vance asks, tell him I kept the margin." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the Vance Sequence at fourteen and the charts are **canon (book)**). Source: P6 (Tobiah letter; Gazette). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists.
- **Hew Callan, pump-man, Depot Two** — He was a Hollow at the quarry pumps for three years ("Hew Callan has been told the year"). He says his own name "a dozen times a shift". He finishes his rounds before going home to the Dawnday supper "three years late", owes his wife four sovereigns, and still owes the scribe a name. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Hew letters; d_sable_returned). Bible: new.
- **Mags Harker, Hew Callan's wife** — Hew sends her word, and she meets him at the quarry gate on Dawnday. "She did not shout," and she takes her four sovereigns "out in being obeyed". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Hew letters). Bible: new. Her name collides with Mags Tennant, the Kestrel's deaf bosun (SB).
- **Ada Pellow, widow of Cooper's Row** — She has lived "seventy years in one street" and walks with a hooked stick. She carried her husband's First Reed from his cradle to their door on their wedding day. It split in the frost "three nights ago", and she will hang only a reed that is his. When it is right she says "It was a comfort to have it wrong." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the details; Ada herself is **canon (book)**, B1 ch11). Source: P9 (§A Reed for Pellow). Bible: new (she is not in any bible volume). See Contradictions on the Pellow farm.
- **Joram Pellow** — Ada's husband. He died in 418 and went into the ground "with a stone on his tongue". He hummed one low note "when he was thinking, or the bread was proving", about 203 Hz, "a hair under the bridge-post reed". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§Invented facts; Pellow quest). Bible: new.
- **Reeds-Miner Tomas, timber-cutter** — He has a glove over a hand whose "fourth [finger] grew back wrong", a mare, a wagon and a sock for coin. He is one load short for the Synod's quota day. His timber goes "to shore the headframe". He has been "thinking about [it] since August". Status: **canon (book)** (V2 already has the name, the trade and the glove; Phase 9 marked him **[PROPOSED]**). The mare, the quota and the sock are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§The Ringing Wood). Bible: already in V2 §People You Can Use Today. The bible is incomplete on the quota and the headframe.
- **The Madders' girl** — "A girl to the Madders on Massday, with her grandmother's hands" is the birth told to the bees. In autumn 417 Tobin notes "The Madder child was warm this morning." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Massday, the grandmother's hands; the birth is **canon (book)**, B1 ch1 Ruth Madder). Source: P9 (bees); P6 (Tobin page 12). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists (Ruth and Coll Madder). The "Massday" is contradicted.
- **Brenna Loft, baker** — She keeps the harvest table on days 2–3: hearth bread, cider and moot-curd. "Come back with coin, or with a song, and I will take the song." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§Brenna Loft). Bible: new.
- **Dunna Sedge, sheaf-binder** — She leads the north-field crew at the dead hour and plaits the sheaf-figure. "This one stays cut." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§Dunna Sedge). Bible: new. She shares a surname with Dorran Sedge (V5).
- **Garrow Finch, quarry hauler** — Off shift at the Boar, he plays Bones for one to twenty sovereigns. "Nobody here ever got rich, and nobody has had to leave." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§TAVERN). Bible: new.
- **The Tarrow family: Gil, Maud, Linnet and Nan** — Gil cut reeds. Maud kept the carders' books ("Back by the first frost"). Linnet is eight and "counts everything she walks past". Nan, Gil's mother, was "the oldest voice in this lane", pitch about 188 Hz. Nan took her own First Reed down so nobody would hang a dead woman's note beside the children's. The family left on "the last cart before the gate was barred", and Nan died an hour later. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13 (§P13 HOUSE). Bible: new.
- **Wenna Marl, neighbour to the Tarrows** — She holds the key and sat with Nan as she died ("I am not blood. It will not take from me"). Afterwards she rehangs Nan's reed: "All four, now." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13. Bible: new. The name collides with Abbess Wenna of the Unrung Bell (SB §The Tongueless).
- **Herald of the Pure Note** — A herald in white at the crossroads: "The Rite is a feast. Attendance is a courtesy and will be noted." "There is bread after. There is always bread after." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§The Rite as a false festival). Bible: new as a game figure. SB ch7 and ch24 have Church heralds.
- **Market vendors** — A herb-seller, a provisioner ("Do not tell my wife what I charged"), a tinker ("If it is loud, I do not stock it"), an oddments-seller with pebbles, and a Flume fisher-wife ("Bless the Flume"). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Vendors). Bible: new.

##### Greenroot

- **Cantor Ilse Verrow of Greenroot** — She kept the parish register. She answered Tobin's summons with the one line the page would carry. She was the last of the parish to go, lighting a lamp in every window. Lyra says "We were girls in the same Conservatory, she sat behind me." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§5 Register; §8). Bible: new. The name collides with Ilse Brennan (V2, V5) and with the Sky Choir's Ilse (B1 prologue).
- **The Pell family of Greenroot** — Hesper Pell, 81, said the grace "and held it past the end". Joss Pell is 56, Ansa Pell 34 (her kettle boiled unheard) and Wick Pell 19. Their four passing-bells are rung "the eldest first, the youngest last". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§3, §5, §6). Bible: new. Name collisions: Hesper Sowerby (B1), Rowena and Cosimo Pell (V2), Joss Carder (B1).
- **"The Joss house" of Greenroot** — Its reed was the first in Greenroot to fall silent. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§5 Register, Driftday). Bible: new.

##### The Synod, the Office and the Church

- **Ignatius Crane (game additions)** — "Brother Ignatius has taken to preaching at the rafters." He keeps the Church's short file on Greenroot, which clerks mark "consolidated" and he has not signed. Given the register, he reads it standing and says "It will be filed". He sells the Bell-Bronze Glaive. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_ignatius); P9 (§8); P12 (ARMS). Bible: already in SB §The Twelve (base).
- **"Ignatius the smith"** — Sells the Resonance Rod, "ground at the forge to 174". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§SONGCRAFT). Bible: new as written; it collides with Ignatius Crane (see Open questions).
- **Factor Vosk's company store** — It sells the Quarry Great-Maul. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (ARMS). Bible: Vosk is already in SB §Antagonists; the store is new.
- **Registrar-General Quist (letters)** — "The matter of the hand-press is closed." It is "the first file in forty years to close without a remainder". "Hot water continues. The chair remains clean." "The desk across from mine is kept. It is dusted on Rootday." In another branch he fills Sable's second cup each morning, and it comes back unclaimed. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Quist letters). Bible: already in SB and V6 §3.6 (base; his Rootday address). The bible is incomplete on the desk and cup.
- **Inspector Mallow's round** — "The round of Inspector Mallow has been struck from the register. Eight lines, one rule through each … the only freedom this Office has issued." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Quist letter, round_struck). Bible: new.
- **Halder Wick (letter)** — "...four plates. The blue one is Ada's..." "The boys … are at choir. It is Thursday." He keeps a chair facing the door. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the table for four is **canon (book)**, SB ch14). Source: P6 (Halder letter). Bible: already in SB §Part Three; V5. Phase 9 renamed the wife (see next entry), and "Thursday" is a contradiction.
- **Halder Wick's wife "Ada" → "Bryony"** — Phase 9 renamed Halder's wife from Ada to Bryony, so that Ada Pellow is the only Ada. The Phase 6 letter text still says "Ada". Status: **cut or changed** ("Ada" is replaced by "Bryony"). Source: P9 (header); P6 (Halder letter). Bible: new (she is unnamed in the bible).
- **Bettis Renn (letter)** — "Name, please. That is how I began every day for three years." She writes the days down in order and has "reached the second winter". She does not forgive being refused, and is glad the stranger sat with her. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Bettis letter). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists and §Part Three. Phase 9's "a man on a bench" conflicts with B1, where Bettis is a woman.
- **Amos Fell, sexton, and Nan Fell** — He sits by a filled grave with his cap in his hands. He cut the tower rope himself. When the bell is struck three times, he cuts NAN FELL, WIFE OF THE SEXTON, and pays "the fee for a tolling" of nine sovereigns. Mourners lay lilies "all at once, in a row". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Amos Fell letters). Bible: new. The location is not stated in the doc.
- **Severin Ashe, High Patriarch (rumour)** — "They say he is kind. They say it the way you tell somebody the bridge is out." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the colour); the person is **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (x_patriarch). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists.
- **The Supreme Arbiter, 140 and listening through glass** — "A hundred and forty years old and listens through glass." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (x_arbiter). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists; V3b.
- **Cosimo Pell, broadsheet-man** — He sleeps over the press. The Office has sent him three polite letters, "he framed the first". He prints the Cantabile Candour, which is "found folded behind the notice". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (V4 keeps Pell himself as [PROPOSED]). Source: P6 (x_pell; Free-press counterpoint). Bible: already in V2 §People (Cosimo Pell) and V4 §Errata (kept as **[PROPOSED]**). The Candour is new.

##### The wider world

- **Brannoch of Bastion Fortis** — A Fortis soldier who writes home to "Mam". "Rota holds." He told muster he is "heavy enough, same as Da." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (p_letter). Bible: new.
- **Null-Watch patrol, "Tam" and "Orrin"** — A line-talk transcript of a four-man patrol: "she said the bees would know …", "I had a cat called … Mustard". "Four went out. The clerk has written four." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (r_line). Bible: new. It conflicts with the Null-Watch rota-number convention (see Contradictions).
- **The drover (Tobin, 416)** — He came up the south road saying Whisper-Grove "has stopped". "The Echo … used to catch them." On the old fields beyond the Grove "you can hear the dead humming … thinner every month." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 8). Bible: new. The Echo is canon (V1 §1.9).
- **The Synod surveyors (413)** — "Three of them in grey, with brass staves and a chain." They measured "gradients … the Synod's" and hung a small glass bell on a stake that hummed 174. "Whatever's there, Cantor." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 4). Bible: new.
- **The young man with a very clean voice (412)** — He took the bells for the Church: "the Pure Note had need of the bronze … the Carillon would be remembered." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 3). Bible: new. This is contradicted by B1 (see Contradictions).
- **The Whisper-Grove cat** — A grey cat, "wet to the elbows". Fed on three days, she follows you, hisses at "what you cannot see", and "does not do doors that echo". Suggested names: Thimble, Burr, Sorrel, Wick, Tansy, Nettle, Moth, Pip. "She is not a Hollow. She remembers all of it." Status: **canon (ruled)** (her look as a grey tabby: R §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 item 8). Her behaviour and names are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Whisper-Grove: the cat); R. Bible: new. B1 ch15 has "a cat that doesn't care".
- **The calmed Aegis** — It says DEFEND. THE. SINGERS. and chimes the alto line. A "how a boy says [a name]" phrase answers "Who taught you the alto line?" Given the red cloak, it chimes a chord that resolves "upward" for the first time in three years. Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part Three ch17, §Part Four ch22). The chimes are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§The calmed Aegis). Bible: already in SB §The Twelve. **Contradicted** on who is in the core (see Contradictions).
- **Wren (the in-game girl)** — In epilogue variants Wren "sits at [Lyra's] elbow and sings the skipping-rhyme too loud" (wren_saved). Otherwise she is "the girl in the north-west" who was taken (wren_taken). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (epilogue suffixes lb_s_wren, lb_s_taken; dream condition). Bible: already in SB §The Twelve as Wren Halloway, a different person. **Contradicted** (see Contradictions).
- **Annet Sowerby** — "Six. She asked the Carder boy his name through the side of a crate." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P9 (Vane "The crates"). Bible: already in SB §Antagonists and §Part Two ch8.
- **The Deacon and his clapperless bell** — Met in the Deep Woods after the Greening (js/wilds.js). Before the Greening the Unrung Bell is heard "from past the Hedge" and never attributed. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the implication; P13 asks the author to choose). Source: P13 (§Lore notes for the author). Bible: new. Related: SB §The Tongueless ("Abbess Wenna of the Unrung Bell").

---

#### From Phases 15–25 (B)

##### Phase 15 (Oakhaven, the roads, the ledger, the hearth)

- **The Deacon of the Pure Note** — An unnamed Church deacon at the Chapel of the Carillon, "sincere and kind" and "catastrophically wrong". He never shouts, waits for "the last of us" to stop talking, and will "not preach to an empty room". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The deacon). Bible: new.
- **Old Harrow (gate-warden), new facts** — "I have kept this gate eleven years on quiet feet. Heel first. Breathe with the Hedge, not against it." He teaches Stealth for coin "and I will not say what I do with the coin". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the eleven years and the teaching); Harrow is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Training: Old Harrow). Bible: Harrow is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures ("old Harrow the gate-warden"); the eleven years are new.
- **Kaelen's camp backstory** — "Salt on the edge, and then wait. A habit from Fortis. The woman who taught me weighed her salt." And: "I once cooked for forty engineers. Nobody died." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Companion camp lines). Bible: new. It is consistent with Fortis's culture of weight in Vol I §III.4.
- **Lyra's mother and the heel of the loaf** — "My mother toasted the heel of the loaf and gave it to whoever was crying. I was often at that table." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Companion camp lines). Bible: new. Book One ch. 9 mentions only her mother's shawl.
- **Sable's camp lines** — "Entry: a meal at a fire. I will not write down who ate what. Some things are better unfiled." And: "Hearth smoke has no register." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Companion camp lines). Bible: new.
- **Anselm Fenn, Consortium clerk** — He draws letters of credit at five in the hundred, "Quietly, please; the Factor is at the next desk." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Letters: new people). Bible: new. See Contradictions for the name collisions with Anselm Rooke and the canon Fenns.
- **Oswin Marl, Veil ledger-keeper** — "If it is paper, I will read it. If it is owed, I will write it down. Raised voices are written down too." He honours Consortium letters at fifteen in the hundred: "the counting-house that made it is under the oaks". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Letters: new people; agents' greetings). Bible: new.
- **Nessa Tolliver, ferrywoman off the Flume** — She deals Rounds at the Boar & Dulcimer's east-window table, "two to thirty sovereigns. I only cheat the river." She "never claims the double". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is [PROPOSED]; §Nessa Tolliver). Bible: new.
- **Tomas (the refugee's "only the wood")** — "The timber wagons don't creak any more. They hum. Tomas says it is only the wood." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Families on the road, shared 3). Bible: new. Vol II's Appendix C lists "Tomas" only as a sample Reach name.

##### Phase 16 FLUME

- **The Stillwater lockkeeper (Pellam Dree, later Tobit Dree)** — He sells news at twelve sovereigns and says he sold "the same this week" to six: the Church's man, the Compact's, a Cantabile woman "with a very small hat", the Reeve's clerk, a Bores factor and a boy. "It is not a secret if six people have it. It is a service." He keeps a drawer of keys and "only lie[s] to the ones who haggle". Status: **cut or changed** for the name. Pellam Dree was renamed **Tobit Dree** to avoid Pellam Quist (ROADMAP.md §R2 — Canon sweep, Near-collisions). The bribable lockkeeper who sells to six parties is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Pellam Dree). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Settlements (STILLWATER LANDING, unnamed); the name is new.
- **Madge Tallow, fence** — "Half of what a clean shop pays, and no questions." "Gone. / I never saw it. / That did not happen." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Madge Tallow). Bible: new.
- **Orrin Haft, Timber Compact cutter (later Barnaby Haft)** — His forty iron-bough logs are chained behind the lower lock, and he owes Compact wages by month's end. He says "Does it ring? No. Nothing rings on this wharf." The lock wheel "will take a pick, or the Root note sung at it" ("Grown wood remembers one note"). He pays 40 sovereigns and two jars of honey "because the Compact does not pay". Status: **cut or changed** for the name, now **Barnaby Haft** beside Orrin Pale (ROADMAP.md §R2, Near-collisions); the character and quest are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Orrin Haft and the quest). Bible: new.
- **The wharf hand and the raft-wife (Stillwater)** — The wharf hand: "The last man who did not [time the Wash] is still being walked back down." The raft-wife: "The Compact blames the lock, the lock blames the keeper, the keeper blames the weather … We are out of tea, patience and luck, in that order." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Wharf hand; §Raft-wife). Bible: new.

##### Phase 16 DEEP (Whisper-Grove Station)

- **Clerk Abelard** — A station clerk who "reports a sentence" on Memoryday, then "the same sentence" on Dawnday. By Nullday the desk is short two clerks: "Coats to stay on the chairs, in case the clerks wish to collect them." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Notices and documents, Daybook). Bible: new.
- **Hesk** — The station worker who tells M. Orly she has said a sentence twice. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§A relief worker's last page). Bible: new.
- **M. Orly, relief** — A relief worker who wrote the households' names into the register at night "so that they are somewhere. The Office has numbers." She left the register on the desk: "Whoever reads this: they are not numbers. Read them out." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§A relief worker's last page). Bible: new.
- **The Fell household (No. 11 Lantern Row)** — Halm and Bettrys Fell, two adults, and Wren, nine. Their loop is "the bread's for Thursday". Bettrys asks again and again where Wren's cloak is. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Ledger: Lantern Row, page four; §The Relief Register). Bible: new. Their names enter the player's Ledger.
- **Wren Fell** — Proposed as the Wren of the Hollow loop "Wren, the cloak". Phase 16 Question 4 asked for a ruling, and none was given. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (Questions 4; §DEEP canon notes). Bible: new. Possible collision with canon Wren Halloway; see Contradictions.
- **Aster Quill (No. 12 Lantern Row)** — One adult; loop "mind the water". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Ledger; §The Relief Register). Bible: new.
- **Lettice Marr and her boy Tamsin (No. 14)** — The mother's loop is "come in now". The child has no loop and does not speak. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Ledger; §The Relief Register). Bible: new.
- **Cobb Annory, Jory Mapp ("who kept the lamps") and Hewe Partridge** — More Lantern Row households in the register, under which is written "*More tomorrow.*". No. 13 is "Door open. Meal on the table. Nobody present. Not counted." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Relief Register; §Ledger names added). Bible: new.
- **Sable on the register** — "I had that household as a number. I had it as eleven." She will read the names "At the water, when it rises. One lantern each." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the lines); the Drowned Lanterns are **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Sable (companion-talk topic)). Bible: the Drowned Lanterns are already in Vol VII (b) - Addendum - Festivals of the Reach.md §The Drowned Lanterns.

##### Phase 16 HEIST

- **Dessa Quill, letter-writer** — She sits on the Chapel steps "where the Chapel can see me". She has written forty-one letters since the gate was barred, none answered, and has sat nine nights watching the vestry door. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dessa Quill and Orrin Pale). Bible: new. The surname collides with canon Jory Quill.
- **Imelda (Dessa's sister)** — Beyond the Hedge, she has written four times: "I am well. I am cold. The boy is walking." She folded in a lock of the boy's hair. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The six held letters). Bible: new.
- **Orrin Pale, sexton** — He keeps the vestry keys, has a daughter beyond the Hedge whose three letters the Chapel held, and will "sleep" for thirty sovereigns: "I did not sleep … I wish to be clear." Later (Phase 27, ROADMAP R2) he became "Oakhaven's living sexton (canon has one, unnamed)" and took over Ignatius's former shop stock and the dowsing rod. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dessa Quill and Orrin Pale). Bible: new.
- **Warden Hask** — A Proctor and the Chapel's warden. After the last hymn he walks the archive in a fixed round "a little over a minute", stops at the cabinet to "read the seal as if it might have changed", and says "The Chapel holds what is given to it." He later "asked to be moved". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dessa Quill…; §Heist beats; §Rumours). Bible: new.
- **The Compact steward (Steward Merrin Oake, Timber Compact)** — "We are the Timber Compact; we cut what the Consortium counts, and nobody counts us." He has eleven men in his books who were on the roster and then were "not anywhere", and he pays from the Compact's strike fund. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Names and labels; §Depot Three: the steward). Bible: new. The Timber Compacts are canon in Vol II §Local Powers.
- **Sergeant Vause, Quarry Gate** — She counts heads in and out. "For nine weeks the heads going out have been fewer than the heads coming in, and Reconciliation says that is acoustic echo artifacting … I have concluded that my superiors are lying." She was given a count of 311, and the roster holds 342. Status: **canon (book)**; the count-book and the numbers are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Depot Three: … Sergeant Vause). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §People You Can Use Today ("concluded her superiors are lying", female, 30s). The match is good.
- **Reeve Alder Mossbrook** — A "big, careful man with a pen he keeps turning". He burns the lease and pays sixty sovereigns: "Some things … are better unread." Status: **canon (book)** for the man. His Phase 16 role as the signer was changed to witness (see *The quarry lease* under Objects). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Depot Three: … Reeve Mossbrook). Bible: already in Vol II §People You Can Use Today (signed the 404 lease, hiding the documents). Vol II now conflicts with the game's Phase 27 change.
- **Ines Tarn, night clerk** — Owed four months, she takes Corporal Pike out for tea for forty sovereigns ("Seventy-five breaths"). Afterwards: "*gone to my sister's. Reconciled.*" Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Ines Tarn…). Bible: new.
- **Night-Reckoner Dray** — The Proctor who walks Depot Three's night round: "Persons without a roster number are not on the quarry." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Depot Three; §Ines Tarn…). Bible: new as a person. A canon Fennick Dray (15) is in Vol V §Other Reach Figures; see Contradictions.
- **Corporal Pike** — A Legionnaire who keeps the strongroom's east half, "three paces each way", and "has not been out of that room in a year". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Depot Three…). Bible: new.
- **Factor Vosk (as written in Phase 16)** — Keeps the roster in a brass three-tumbler safe plated VOSK AND CO. Status: **canon (book)** for the person (Factor Adela Vosk); **[PROPOSED]** for the safe. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Ines Tarn…; §The Factor's safe). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures. Phase 16 calls Vosk "he"; see Contradictions.
- **Wennet (Lyra's aunt)** — She writes from the Sandglass Bazaar ("The Bazaar keeps its clocks and I keep my ears"): "the reed you sent came back to me whole, so you are alive. I tuned it to your breastbone as Tobin taught me." Lyra: "She never wrote a word without a correction." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The six held letters). Bible: new.
- **Kaelen's mother ("Mam")** — Writes to "Kael": "your father asks nothing … I keep your chair and I do not dust it." Kaelen: "My mother has never dusted anything in her life." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The six held letters). Bible: new. This contradicts Book One ch. 5; see Contradictions.
- **Cob (Hetty's bargeman)** — "They will not let the barges through the Hedge lock … I have your share of the eel money in a sock under the loose board at the Landing. Keep the dulcimer tuned." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The six held letters). Bible: new.
- **Ness and baby Orla (Orla Hale's daughter-in-law and granddaughter)** — "Mother Orla, the baby has your chin and your temper and we have called her Orla … We are at the Landing." Orla: "Sap and splinters, the poor child." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The six held letters). Bible: new. Orla's canon son Davin was taken (Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures); whose wife Ness is goes unsaid.
- **Pim's father ("Da")** — "Pim, I am at the Landing. I tried the north road and the gate was barred … I count to eight every night so that you will have been counted." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The six held letters). Bible: new. This contradicts Book One ch. 1; see Contradictions.

##### Phase 16 KESTREL crew

- **Mags Tennant (as written in Phase 16 and 17)** — The game calls her "first mate". She signs and reads lips, and her lines show only when she can see the player's face. She buys a new lens for thirty sovereigns to stand lookout. Her lines: "I cannot hear her hum. I feel it in my teeth"; "If you see my back, step round. I am not rude. I am deaf"; "The children asked me for the sign for up." In the Cantabile extra she signs "Not aboard. Never aboard" about the bottle. Status: **canon (book)** for Mags; **[PROPOSED]** for the lines and the "first mate" title. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Upgrades … and Mags); Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 259–269). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures ("Mags Tennant, the deaf bosun"). See Contradictions.
- **Pennick (as written in Phase 16)** — "Engineer of the tanks", a pessimist: "It is going to fail. I am only deciding which morning." "Tobiah means well. That is the problem with Tobiah." He takes the tanks in hand at a fifth off all gold and builds the new lift-tank. Mags: "Tobiah talks to the tanks. Pennick talks to nobody and the tanks listen." Status: **canon (book)** for Pennick; **[PROPOSED]** for the characterization. Phase 16's doc calls him "proposed as the first engineer". Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Pennick). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures ("a young engineer who talks to the lift-tanks"). See Contradictions.
- **Nell Carder aboard** — "Nell Carder, of the mill", with grey flour "kept from the Hedge". She bakes a morning loaf once she has the galley stove: "It was a mill town … the wheel will have a tree through it by now." "The lad's hands are still clean. I check them every morning." "They sang the calling-song after us from four miles back. I knead to it." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the lines and the role); Nell is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Nell). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures; Vol V §5 (heir to Bettany).
- **Hob Wendle aboard (tanner)** — "Hob Wendle, tanner", on a sack of hides. "I have buried two apprentices this year." He puts an edge on weapons: "Leather does not care what it is holding." He put out the lamp in the twins' window before he climbed; "The twins went down the culvert after their dog." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Hob); Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Training: Hob Wendle). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures (named only). Book One ch. 3 makes him a field-hand; see Contradictions.
- **The Wendle twins (Joss, later Ned, and Wyn)** — Hob: "Joss would have asked what everything was for. Wyn would have asked who it belonged to." Status: **cut or changed**. Joss was renamed **Ned** off canon Joss Carder, and canon's Tanner boys got a rumour of their own (ROADMAP.md §R2, The Tanner twins). The current code reads "Ned would have asked…". Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Hob). Bible: new.
- **Captain Vane aboard** — "Wood, silk, three tanks and a debt." "I have a file at the Office that says reliable, does not ask questions. I would like it burned, and I would like to read it first." "Eleven hundred pounds over, and she still lifts." "If the Office comes, they will come by sound." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the lines). "Eleven hundred pounds" is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Vane). Bible: Vane is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Vane; the 1,100-pound overload is in §Part Four — Coda (ch. 21).
- **Captain Vane as trainer** — Teaches Blade, "Rust Coast style: short, mean, nothing a Synod fencing-master would sign … Everything I know cost me something." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Training: Captain Vane). Bible: new.

##### Phase 17 (the prologue, Port Cantabile, the Long Night)

- **Lyra in the prologue** — Voiceless, with a slate on a cord and chalk in her pocket. Her burned hand is bound in linen. She writes "in the square capitals you have been writing for two months". Her face is creation portrait 2 (red braids). Status: **canon (book)**; the portrait choice is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§P17 PROLOGUE). Bible: already in Vol V §5 and Vol VI §8 (no voice; slate).
- **Seraphina, Ignatius Crane, Solenne and the Aegis (prologue looks)** — The looks:
  - Seraphina wears the Legion's plate turned white and carries the Sun-Blade.
  - Ignatius wears the Flambeau's robes "with the fire out" and a woman's apron, and has a translucent hand.
  - Solenne is a tall woman with broken black-gold wings and copper at the throat.
  - The Aegis is nine feet of iron, DEFEND THE SINGERS on its chest and a rosy light in its helm.

  Status: **canon (book)** for the people (Book Four); **[PROPOSED]** for the looks. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Invented facts and looks). Bible: all four are already in Series Bible (canon).md §The Twelve; the looks are new.
- **Dorran Sedge** — He kept the river gate twenty years and the reed-beds at Reedside before that. He has a voice like a bullfrog, sang bass in the orchard choir every Longest Night before 417, and is married to Mabbie. "Nobody says it now, Cantor. Not since Nullday. Nobody's said true enough since the leaves came down." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §Other Reach Figures.
- **Ada Pellow** — Went into the Hedge at the North Gate on the day of the Greening after hearing Lyra's father's voice. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part Two — The Root.
- **Oona Salt, Harbour-Queen (new details)** — The new details:
  - She takes dues "in my own hand, on the Mole-head, at the slack".
  - She has stood at the bell "nineteen years of mornings".
  - She has told forty widows that "a bottle is not the dead".
  - Teague has asked her eleven times to take dues through him.
  - She rings the Mole-bell once for Marrin Tace's bottle, the first time a bell has been asked for a bottle.

  Status: **canon (book)** for Oona (her brother Merret, the pilot whose bottle was held back "because his sister would pay more", is in Vol IV); **[PROPOSED]** for the details above. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 115, 120, 129–158). Bible: already in Vol IV §The Free Captains and the Articles; Series Bible (canon).md §Part One · The Rust Coast.
- **Auld Bessa, the last Reef-Reader** — 84. She sits on a bollard at the Mole steps, eyes shut: "What's she singing?" "Seven years of winter." "You sing, I think. Do not do it near the water." "That is why the Harbour keeps a clock made of fish." Status: **canon (book)**; the lines are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 159–172). Bible: already in Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §Reef-Readers.
- **Teague, Harbour office** — Keeps the Harbour's book and reads the Articles "in the voice you would use for a tide-table". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 111–128). Bible: a Colm Teague is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part One · The Rust Coast and §Canon added in Book Two (dead, 4 Ashfall). See Contradictions.
- **Hollis Teel, Free Captain of the Long Odds** — "Salvage and nothing else, whatever you have heard." He sits on "the best bollard in the Yard" with a stone jar, and calls the Sixteenth Article "the whole of my religion". He sings the pilot's rhyme. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 173–183). Bible: new.
- **Fenwick Sloe, chandler (third landing)** — Sells brass that is "from Fortis … Mostly". "Haggling is a form of theft and the Harbour has a view." He won't sell to hulls on the Harbour's bad list "since the Twelfth got interesting", and sells bells for the Cay hooks. Status: **[PROPOSED]** for the name and lines; the third-landing chandler is **canon (book)** (Book Two ch. 3). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 184–199). Bible: new. The chandler is unnamed in Book Two and absent from the bible.
- **Marrin Tace, widow (fourth landing)** — Keeps her husband's vox-bottle unopened for three years: "If I do not open it, he is both and neither, and I can sweep the step." A grey-coated man offers six sovereigns monthly. She gives the player Jory's tin whistle and six sovereigns if the bell is rung. Phase 17's doc lists her as canon, but its Question 5 lists her as an invention. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. The unnamed widow is a bible hook. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is canon…; Questions 5; strings 200–220). Bible: new as a name. Vol IV §Hooks #3 has "A widow on the fourth landing with a bottle she has not opened in three years and will not sell."
- **Jory Tace, pilot** — "Lost off the Cay passage three years ago and not found." His bottle was bought for four sovereigns: J. TACE in chalk on the wax, "and a four". "He whistled the keys when he could not hear them." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 91, 204, 216). Bible: new.
- **Mr. Dunmore, Receiving Clerk** — Grey coat, a trestle desk and a brass-bound box that clinks. "Units are received here. Six sovereigns, stamped … it will be reconciled." "The offer does not expire. It is simply not renewed." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. The doc calls the six-sovereign offer canon, but no bible or book source was found. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 224–235). Bible: new.
- **Marlow Quain, pilot (Cay boat)** — "Cay run, a copper there and back. I go at the slack and I come back at the slack." "Do not sing in the boat." He whistles four notes all the way over. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 236–248). Bible: new. Vol VI's "Quain" match is unrelated.
- **Hesper, of the four houses** — "A big slow woman with a soft face and a voice like a foghorn", who has "buried … more children than most women on the Coast have borne". Nine children are at supper tonight. "Since the Screaming Year? More than I will say in the Yard." Status: **canon (book)** for Hesper and the wreck-orphans; **[PROPOSED]** for her description. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 249–258). Bible: already in Vol IV §The town as written and §Hooks #2.
- **Cantabile background people** — A net-wife on the fifth landing ("The lamplighter is on his fourth in three years"). A Yard deck-hand: "I was a thief nine years and never once took a nail from the Harbour." A child on the Cay: "That one is my brother's. He was three … it rings crooked because he was." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 270–280). Bible: new.
- **Marl Dunning, bass (Long Night keeper)** — "My mother hummed it into the hollow of the oldest tree every year and never said what for. I have hummed it forty." "Low and slow. That is all the old ones ever asked." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 17 Question 3, never answered). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§The three keepers). Bible: new.
- **Tansy Quill, apprentice (Long Night)** — "I keep losing my place on the turn … after the fifth. Don't tell Marl." "I have never been this far from the Conservatory in the dark." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Q3). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§The three keepers). Bible: new.
- **Jory Fenn, lamplighter (Long Night)** — "I have lit lamps in the Reach for twenty years. First time one has been lit for something." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Q3). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§The three keepers). Bible: new.

##### Phase 22 (the Sheep-Walk)

- **Tamar Farrant, shepherd** — "Seventh of the Farrants who had the sheep-walk" ("six generations"). She brought eleven Rust Coast ewes and a tup up from Cantabile in Vane's hold, "and [he] charged me for the smell". She tells the truth of the flocks: "I fed my children my father's ewes, one a month, and I would do it again. Nothing took them. We did." She knows Hedge-wool by her mother's mark. She gives her mother's mantle, her father's camp kit and line, and her grandfather's crook. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (as a person). The ruling suggested "a shepherd's family" as the speaker. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§Tamar Farrant; §22b People). Bible: new.
- **Kit Farrant** — Tamar's boy: "Mam says I'm to watch the fold and not the hill. I watch the hill." He asks the player to scour the Ram. He goes missing after a bleat and is found in Ewe Barrow's last chamber: "It was making a noise like a lamb. Like a lamb that needed me. And then it was big." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§Kit Farrant; §22b People). Bible: new.
- **Kit's gran (the Farrant grandmother)** — Source of the Ram folklore (scour it every seven years) and of the ram's-horn-button custom. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`kit.intro`, `kit.done`). Bible: new.
- **Gideon Thrale of the Bellwether** — "I opened an inn on a road nobody walks." He serves mutton "six ways", cider and apples. "I cannot give it away; I tried, and it went badly." He hears bleating at night by the Hanger. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§Gideon Thrale; `gideon.wood`). Bible: new.
- **Absalom Rudd, the old beacon-keeper** — "Three fires on three hills. I lit them forty back-ends running. Then the Hedge." He lends his brass tinder-box. His grandfather called the night-thing "the Woolwight", "a story for keeping boys in at night". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§Absalom Rudd; §22b People). Bible: new.
- **Brenna Loft (mutton seller)** — Named as one of the places selling Phase 22 mutton. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (as a seller). Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new, Mutton). Bible: new.

##### Phase 24 and Phase 25

- **Old Harrow's two gate lines** — "You came up the Greenroot road? Then you heard what's behind you, singing." And: "Keep your voice in your chest, friend. And welcome to what's left of Oakhaven." Phase 24 Question 2 asks keep, cut or reword, and it was never answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md (§New text). Bible: new. The phrase "Keep your voice in your chest" is the old game INTRO's.
- **The opening's cast and staging** — The opening flies over the Reach before a new game:
  - Ignatius Crane asks the Catechism and the congregation answers.
  - The Synod column comes through Greenroot: the Flambeau with his lantern, the Herald, and ten Proctors two abreast with censers of glass-fire, under the Pure Note at 528 Hz.
  - Ignatius says "One note. One law. One hand." over the column.
  - The narrator is "an older man, grave and unhurried", chosen by the owner from four auditions.

  Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the staging); **canon (book)** (the words). Source: Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new). Bible: Ignatius is in Series Bible (canon).md §The Iron Synod and §The Twelve; the column staging is new.
- **The 27 rigged people (Phase 25 looks)** — Each figure has a prop:

  | Person | Prop or pose |
  |---|---|
  | Hetty | tankard |
  | Rooke | scraping blade |
  | Sable | ledger |
  | Nell | folded arms |
  | the Flambeau | censer |
  | Vosk | ledger |
  | Harrow | lantern and staff |
  | Bettany | herbs |
  | Tam | on his cane |
  | Halder | tray |
  | the Sexton | on his shovel |
  | the Herald | proclamation |
  | Mags | rope and sling |
  | the Mourner | lilies |
  | Burlsick | twigs |
  | Orla | "enthroned among her roots" |
  | the Catalogue Proctor | staff raised overhead in both hands, then brought down |

  The other rigged figures are Kaelen, Lyra, the townsman, the townswoman, Hob, Vane, Pim, Tobiah, Aldric, Quist and Wren. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (visual). Source: Phase 25 - The pixel-3D set.md (§Painted side views…). Bible: new as looks. Most of these people are canon; "the Mourner" and "Burlsick" are game names.

##### The Game Design Document (prototype v0.1, from Series Bible v1.0 and the Book One draft to ch. 10)

- **Travellers and Path Actions** — Lyra (Cantor · Support, 174 Hz) uses **Tend**. Kaelen (Vanguard · Tank, 528 Hz Light-glass) uses **Listen**. Vane (Corsair · Skirmisher, 639 Hz) uses **Smuggle** ("Nobody looks up"). Solenne Aubade appears only in the Prologue, at 528 Hz. Future travellers: Sable (**Recite**), the Aegis Sentinel, Ignatius (**Purify**) and the Static Remnant, "and the rest of the sixteen". Status: **canon (book)** for the people and frequencies; **[PROPOSED]** for the Path Action names. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§3). Bible: the people are already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part VIII — The Cast; the Path Actions are new.
- **Vane's pistols, *Last Rites* and *Second Opinion*** — Glass-shot pistols. Release Readiness suggests putting them into his lines. Status: **canon (book)** (Book One). Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§3); Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§4 #21). Bible: new. They appear in the Book One manuscript but not in any bible volume.
- **The Tide-Weaver** — Narrates the Echoes between Parts: "I have watched you choose this four hundred and eleven times." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§2, §4.9). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §The Twelve and §Part Four — Coda (Echo I).
- **Release Readiness's canon people "written and unused"** — Each was checked against the bible and books:

  | Person | Release Readiness use | Where found |
  |---|---|---|
  | Ennis Crowle | the Flume-road beekeeper | Vol V §Other Reach Figures |
  | Lissa Merrow | eleven, hands taken by the Grafters | Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures |
  | Ilse Brennan | voiceless teacher | Vol V §Ilse Brennan (78) |
  | the Wetherby twins, the Hale girl and the miller's three | famine graves | Book One ch. 2 |
  | Hew Callan | "I was there. I counted every stroke." | not found in any bible volume or book manuscript |
  | Ivo Renn | Ashlane home | not checked further |

  Status: **canon (book)**, except Hew Callan, whose source is unverified. Source: Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§3, §4). Bible: as in the table. The famine graves are new to the bible, and Hew Callan is new.

#### From Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

##### The likeness cast (Phase 23) and their dossiers

- **Kaelen Vance (likeness)** — The Governor's son and Lyra's shadow since they were both nine. He left the Reach at seventeen for the Conservatory of Bores and came home with a glass arm. His dossier: born in the Governor's house above the square; trade "Calibration... for the Conservatory, then the Consortium"; he lives in a hut north of the North Gate and keeps a chalk list on a beam, paying it down a name a day; he carries a caliper and chalk. Says: "I don't build things anymore. I just know how they break. What do you want broken?" Status: **canon (book)** for the frame (Series Bible §Kaelen Vance; MS ch. 1, l. 480) [the dossier details are PROPOSED]. Source: p23-cast.tsv, js/data.js CREATION_FACES. Bible: already in Series Bible §Kaelen Vance. **Contradicted:** the dossier says "About thirty-six" (the bible: thirty-four, entering the Conservatory at seventeen in 402) and "A left arm of Aether-Glass" (the bible and MS ch. 7, l. 2253: cyan Light-glass, Dawning glass). The beam list is the Kestrel's deck-beam in MS ch. 13, not his hut's.
- **Lyra Ashgrove (likeness)** — Cantor of Oakhaven. She cuts the town's reeds every dusk, keeps its lamps, teaches every child their first note, and tuned the First Reed over the door standing on a chair. Her father set the long cane in her hands first. Born on Cooper's Row, "the house with the green door"; she keeps a slate, a fresh reed and lamp-oil. Says: "A martyr is just a musician who gave up before the coda. I would rather not be either." Status: **canon (book)** for the frame [the green door, the First Reed story and the dossier are PROPOSED]. Source: p23-cast.tsv, js/data.js. Bible: already in Series Bible §Lyra Ashgrove. **Contradicted:** "About thirty-six" against the bible's thirty-three (MS ch. 11, l. 3426: "thirty-three years of the Reach").
- **Captain Vane (likeness)** — Rust Coast corsair, master of the Drift-Kestrel. He went to sea out of Port Cantabile; in 414 the Wave-Cutter went into a tonal trench with her tow, and forty-eight men came home because of the axe at the hawser. He has run eleven Consortium consignments, twelve crates a run, and keeps a vox-bottle on a chain he has never unstopped and a manifest with two columns. Born on a fish stair below the mast-yard, Port Cantabile. Says: "Every sailor dies, friend. The trick is to be somewhere else when it happens." Status: **canon (book)** for the frame (Series Bible §Vane; MS ch. 4) [the birthplace and dossier are PROPOSED]. Source: p23-cast.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Vane — Corsair of the Rust Coast. **Contradicted:** "About forty-five" (the bible: forty-one), and "bought the Drift-Kestrel the year after [414]" (Series Bible §Part V — Timeline: 417).
- **Sable (likeness)** — Archivist of the Veil, "who wrote the forms the Office still files people under". The Veil took her young; she copied registers for eleven years and read them for four. When the Synod absorbed the order she walked out at the change of the watch with a satchel of names never entered, and she writes her own name nowhere. Ties: "Registrar-General Quist, who poured the second cup". Says: "I am a list, not a cure. Say what you came to say." Status: **canon (book)** for the frame (Series Bible §Sable) [the dossier is PROPOSED]. Source: p23-cast.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Sable — Archivist of the Veil. **Contradicted:** "About fifty" (the bible: "Age unknown; she has forgotten"). "When the Synod absorbed the order" cannot be: the Veil was absorbed c. 300 SR (Series Bible §Part V), and Vol III (b) has the Office swear Sable in in 416. In the book she stole the Bound Ledger and stayed in Whisper-Grove; she did not walk out with a satchel.
- **Anselm Rooke (likeness)** — Surgeon-Grafter. "The Synod sends the saws and you hold them, and you stop the moment the patient asks." The Inquisitor has asked twice for his list of sap-marks. He keeps a needle in his lapel and a record of every door he has chalked, and carries a bottle of Bettany's. Says: "I have cut more limbs than any butcher in the Reach and done every one with care. Sit. I will not chalk your door today." Status: **canon (book)** for the frame (MS ch. 3, l. 951; Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures) [the history and dossier are PROPOSED]. Source: p23-cast.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures. **Contradicted:** the likeness raises him "in a barber-surgeon's room off the tanners' lane" with "twenty years" of cutting, "Past sixty", and gives him "a mother behind the tannery". MS ch. 3, l. 951 makes him "fifty or near it", come to the Reach from the Organum four years ago.
- **Mags Tennant (likeness)** — Raised in a boat-shed below the mast-yard at Port Cantabile, the youngest of a stevedore's family. She learned the dock-crews' hand-speech before reading, spent twelve years on the quay, and keeps her own tally of the crates "in a book the captain does not look at". She signs when the wind is up, speaks when it drops, and reads lips. Says: "Slower. I read lips, not minds." Status: **canon (book)** for the frame (MS ch. 4, l. 1327: Margarethe Tennant, deaf since birth, lip-reader, Rust Coast hand-cant) [the history is PROPOSED]. Source: p23-cast.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("the deaf bosun no voice in the dark can lure"). **Contradicted:** the likeness and dossier call her "First mate of the Drift-Kestrel". The bible and MS ch. 4 make her the bosun, and in the bible "first mate" is Vesper's title on the Wave-Cutter.

##### Oakhaven and the Reach

- **Bettis Renn** — Thirty-eight when taken, Ivo Renn's wife, of Ashlane. She stands in the Malting in a Whisper-Grove apron three years filthy, a basket at her feet, making the gesture of a woman putting a coat over her arm; her tag reads WG-40,102. Sable seats the core and reads her entry aloud: "Taken on the fourth day of the harvest, in the sixth hour. She asked to go second". Bettis screams, remembering all three years, and begs "put it back". The player chooses whether Sable puts her back (one fragment) or she is carried to the Kestrel, to Ivo in the third stack. Her letter: "My name is Bettis Renn. I am writing it so that it is somewhere." Status: **canon (ruled)** ("Bettis Renn should be Ivo's wife just as in the book"); also **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, l. 4109; ch. 18, ll. 5020–5082). Source: p32-canon.tsv, p31-grove.tsv; commits ad72340, f4db2f7; test/canon32.test.js. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("entry 40,102, the first person ever given a name back, who remembers all three years of it"). The bible omits that she is Ivo's wife and that she asked to go second. The "put her back" branch is the game's alternative [PROPOSED]; in the book she is carried aboard.
- **Ivo Renn** — Cooper, of Ashlane, forty-one at the taking. He asked whether he would be permitted to keep his hands and was told he would. His core is in the Kestrel's third stack; the Bell Yard Roll lists him (RENN. COOPER. Barrels, tubs and casks; staves dressed while you wait). Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, l. 4105; ch. 16, l. 4510; ch. 18, l. 5076, "the third stack"). Source: p31-grove.tsv, p32-canon.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Hob Wendle** — A field-hand of forty whose roots came out through the soles of his feet in the summer and left white threads in the dust; three toes have gone to wood. He sits on his step in the Hollow, across from Lissa. On Rootday Rooke takes the toes "with the hush-saw and the sap-iron" while the Cantor holds his head, and he walks after, as promised: "Keep 'em warm and dry, she told me after. I keep 'em dry." Part Three opens with his death aboard: at the fourth hour his feet have gone into the Kestrel's deck; Bettany gives him the long drops; "I'm sorry about your ship, Captain"; they put him over the side with four planks and five feet of beam, and Vane says the words. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 3, ll. 971–975; ch. 13, ll. 3914–3960). Source: p30-hollow.tsv, p34-hob.tsv, js/hollow30.js; commit c9e83b8. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures (name only) and §Part Two — The Root. The bible lacks his trade, age and death.
- **Abner Fenning** — Oakhaven's tanner on Tannery Row: the game's old "Hob Wendle the tanner", renamed so that canon Hob can die. He sets a lamp in his twins' window and keeps the culvert key on a nail. He sells oiled leather "so it never creaks" and gut-strung instruments, and takes the bench in the Kestrel's hold to sharpen your weapon once a day. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (p34-hob.tsv header; PLAYTEST: "a name I chose; tell me if you'd like another"). Source: p34-hob.tsv; commit c9e83b8. Bible: new. His NPC id stays 'hob' for saves.
- **Ned and Wyn Fenning (the culvert twins)** — Abner's twins, who went down the tannery culvert after their dog "on Driftday last". Their coats are found folded in the Under-hedge, and writing their names in the ledger lets their father put out the lamp in their window. "Ned would have asked what everything was for. Wyn would have asked who it belonged to." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (renamed Joss and Wyn Tanner, then Ned and Wyn Wendle in R2, then Fenning in the canon pass). Source: p27-r2.tsv, p34-hob.tsv. Bible: new. The book's Tanner twins are different people (below).
- **The Tanner twins (the Tanner boys)** — Canon's pair, killed with Marten and Old Coyle when a bore head went on the platform, and sung into the ground side by side by the Cantor. R2 gave them a rumour of their own. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 512; ch. 5, l. 1747). Source: p27-r2.tsv ("The Cantor sang the Tanner boys into the ground side by side, after the bore head went on the platform. Marten and Old Coyle with them."). Bible: new (no volume names them).
- **Sim and Moll Fletch** — Two hands at the tannery vats. Sim: "Lime, then the beam, then the vats... The hides are thinner this year." Moll: "We hear them in there at night, humming. Eighty-four in the Hollow, Rooke says." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Lissa Merrow** — Eleven, on the step of the red-shutter house with her own tin cup ("if you drink out of a cup that has been in a house, you get what the house has. That is not true... I still keep my cup."). She warns on Rootday: "Do not look at them. If you look at them they look back, and then they write you down." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 384; ch. 3, l. 1079; ch. 9, l. 2938) [her lines are PROPOSED]. Source: p30-hollow.tsv, js/hollow30.js. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("eleven, whose hands the Grafters took") and §Part X (an heir). The game's lines ("It is only in my arms, Rooke says, up to here") do not say that her hands are gone; in MS ch. 3 her forearms end in bandages below the elbow.
- **The Pells and the Ashbys** — The two Hollow households Rooke has pencilled, not chalked: "early, could go back, if kept warm". The Pells are on the north row and the Ashbys on the south. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collisions: Vol VI's Fen, "Fenella Ashby", and Vol II's Alchemist Rowena Pell.
- **The burl-sick of the Hollow** — A man whose feet have gone into the cobbles to the ankle; a woman whose hands are knuckled root, humming four notes over and over; an old man with green through his collar "like a second beard" who lifts one finger in greeting. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (types drawn from MS ch. 3). Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Grafters (on their Rootday round)** — A felt-wheeled wagon with a brazier on its tail and the saws laid out under a sheet. Three Grafters in white felt masks with no mouth and aprons over Office grey go door to door with the book, while a Legionnaire stands off at the end of the lane and every house stops humming. Status: **canon (book)** for the round (MS ch. 3, ll. 939–945) [the masks, aprons and the weekly timing are **[PROPOSED]** and differ from the book]. Source: p30-hollow.tsv, js/hollow30.js. Bible: Grafters are in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures (Rooke's sap-mark). **Contradicted:** the book's wagon has felt-wrapped harness and leather-booted hooves; the Grafters wear white leather aprons over white smocks and leather masks with glass eyepieces and wire over the mouth; the Legionnaire walks at the tailboard. They come "on the second Rootday of every month" (ch. 1, l. 328), but the game sends them every Rootday morning.
- **Nell Carder** — Pim's mother. Her chest keeps her out of the root-cellar, and once Pim is safe she asks for the house to be made fit: "Rooke said to keep him out of the damp." Her last words on the quest: "Take this. It is not enough. Nothing is ever enough." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, ch. 3, l. 913) [the quest is PROPOSED]. Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures and §Deaths (she inherits the children in Book Two).
- **Pim Carder** — Seven. Lyra teaches him a whistle "only me and Lyra know", two notes down, on Hearthday afternoon on the Carders' step. On Massday at the Listening Hour the Hedge whistles it from the culvert and he goes, and Lyra brings him back singing aloud, her mouth bleeding after. In one thread he says instead: "I did not go this time. I counted to forty." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 6, l. 2013; the lure ch. 6) [the counting-to-forty thread is PROPOSED]. Source: p36-days.tsv, js/days35.js; commit e25b999. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures and §Part X (an heir).
- **Davin Hale** — Orla's son. Until Hearthday evening he is alive and sings for coppers at the Boar "when Hetty lets him, which is never", with his mother's harvest song, all four lines. He dies at the Boar & Dulcimer on Hearthday evening (game day 2, from 20:00). Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 4; Series Bible: "taken for singing her harvest song") [the coppers detail is PROPOSED]. Source: p36-days.tsv, js/days35.js. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **Orla Hale** — Rooted into her chair. Before Hearthday she asks you to send her Davin home before the Listening Hour; Rooke gives you Davin's boots for her from Massday morning. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 3; ch. 6, the boots "at the first hour"). Source: p36-days.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **Old Tam** — The night-hummer of the Flume crossing. His wife ("the missus"; the game calls her Essa) died of the pox "the year the old king went into the glass", forty years ago near enough, and called him Tammy. Something past the thorn now calls him Tammy and sang The Miller's Daughter up the Flume in her voice. Once you have told him the truth he teaches you the whole song, "Essa's verse and mine". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 6, l. 1999; ch. 1, l. 426) [the name Essa is PROPOSED]. Source: p32-canon.tsv, p28-narrow.tsv; commit ad72340. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("Tam the night-hummer, who walked into the water-meadow singing"). The bible has no wife or Tammy.
- **Essa (Old Tam's wife)** — Named in the game only; the canon pass changed her death from "went in at the sluice in the Cascade year" to the pox, forty years ago. Bees are told of her death "in the order of the years". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the name). Source: p32-canon.tsv, p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: new. A library book still has an "Essa Brindle, who went into the Flume at the sluice-gate" (see Contradictions).
- **Bettany** — The midwife. She teaches the tending breath for eighteen sovereigns: "Forty-one births through a clay jar. The tending note is mostly breath, love; the voice only rides on it... I hum births, not charity." Aboard the Kestrel she gives Hob the long drops. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1; ch. 13, l. 3956) [the lesson and its price are PROPOSED]. Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p34-hob.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **Harrow** — The gate-warden. He has a high-standing line ("People speak well of you inside the Hedge. I hear things at the gate. Go careful.") and a low one ("The Hedge talks, and it has been talking about you."). Status: **canon (book)** (the person) [the lines are PROPOSED]. Source: p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **Hetty Bram** — Landlady of the Boar & Dulcimer. Before Hearthday evening her house rule has no name on it: "We do not sing in here. House rule." She sends the corsair's supper up the mast-ladder, hears a name from his hold, and pays you twenty sovereigns to listen at the crates. With high standing she pours a cup on the house; aboard, she packs blankets round every crate during the cutter chase. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 4; ch. 21) [the supper and the standing lines are PROPOSED]. Source: p33-crates.tsv, p36-days.tsv, p28-narrow.tsv, p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures and §Part X (an heir).
- **Annet Sowerby** — Six years old, a core in one of the crates in the Kestrel's hold. Listening at the crates puts her name in your ledger. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 8, ll. 2674–2808; Series Bible "core WG-41,006"). Source: p33-crates.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures. Also on the Cloister's tapes, "A. SOWERBY".
- **Agnes Tull** — The baker's wife. She has not slept since the Hedge went up and punches the dough down every hour of the night: "I would offer you a chair but they are all full of bread." Loaves cost two and honey four. Status: **canon (book)** (the baker's wife, MS ch. 1, l. 308; ch. 2, l. 652; ch. 11, l. 3420) [the name is PROPOSED]. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new. In the MS she has a husband who pulls her inside (ch. 2, l. 652); the game has no baker.
- **Ambrose Vettle** — Chandler: "Candles, oil, reeds for the hush-lamps. If it keeps a light lit or a street humming, I have it." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (thin29.js). Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collision: Ambrose of the coffee stall in Registry Square, Vol VI §3.6 People of the Organum.
- **Orrin Pale** — Oakhaven's sexton. He salts the chapel paths and every new grave at dawn, "so that what is buried stays buried and says nothing", keeps the burial book, and from R2 sells the old watch's crypt stock, including the bell-bronze dowsing rod. Status: **canon (book)** for the office (MS ch. 1, l. 308: the sexton salts the graves by the barrow-load and binds the jaws of the dead; ch. 3, l. 1087) [the name is **[PROPOSED]**; ROADMAP R2: "canon has one, unnamed"]. Source: p27-r2.tsv, p29-thin.tsv, js/chapel27.js. Bible: new. He is also the author of the library chronicle "The Fires on the Ground" (413 SR). Near-collision: Orrin Hale (Vol III §B.4, Vol VI §3.6).
- **Godwin Pettigrew** — Oakhaven's lamplighter. At dusk he walks the lanes with his pole and taper and lights twenty-four lamps one by one: "Every lamp in Oakhaven by full dark, and not one of them quiet." Status: **canon (book)** for the office (MS ch. 1, l. 412: "the lamplighters came", plural) [the name is **[PROPOSED]**; it comes from the Phase 26 library journal "A Year of Nights"]. Source: p28-narrow.tsv, js/narrow28.js. Bible: new.
- **The night-hummers** — Old men and women on stools in shawls at Oakhaven's crossings, humming so that no street falls wholly silent ("Lower. I am humming for the whole street, not for you."). At the dead hour they change over with the murmur "all's well, all's quiet, keep to the Narrow". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 412) [three crossings and the dead-hour call are PROPOSED]. Source: p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part IV — The Rules of Dread ("Night-hummers sit at crossings until dawn").
- **Abel Sedge** — Turf-cutter by the fold on the Watch Downs, who cuts turf for the Bellwether's fire and says the sign's line aloud: "There are no sheep." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (thin29.js). Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collision: Dorran Sedge (Vol V §The Grove of Oakhaven).
- **Mother Hurst** — At the hearth of the Hursts' farmhouse in Wethercombe, minding the soup while the others are at the fold: "There are no sheep, mind. You will have noticed." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Jem Cotter** — Shearer in the tithe barn, which he bars from inside against the woolwight; he stacks two years of unsold clip "and waits for the road to come back". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Gideon (Thrale) of the Bellwether** — The innkeeper, who rents the room upstairs: "Three, I said." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 22's Gideon Thrale; R4 gives him the room). Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Tamar Farrant, Kit Farrant, Absalom Rudd** — Named in R1's closing lines for the Sheep-Walk quests: Tamar's ewes still out on the Downs, Kit Farrant brought out of Ewe Barrow (or not found), Absalom Rudd's fold-fires. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 22 people). Source: p27-r1.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Ennis Crowle** — Beekeeper on the Flume Road, author of "Telling the Bees". She tells her bees everything ("The bees do the work; I only tell them things"), teaches the three tellings for Bettany's skeps, and hears it in her own hives when the town remembers itself. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (her lines and placement; she herself is bible canon, Vol V, not in the manuscript). Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: already in Vol V §Other Reach Figures ("Ennis Crowle (beekeeper, Flume road), the first sung down"). In Vol V she is the first of the 187 sung down in the Felling, in Frostmonth 419, after the game's events.
- **Gil Wade** — Punt-poler in the Low Rings: "Roof to roof, a penny a pole. I know where the street still has a bottom." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new. In-game near-collision: Gil Tarrow (Phase 13).
- **Ysolde Marrow** — Salvager in the Low Rings, "out of the upstairs rooms, mostly". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collision: Chancellor-Magnate Ysolde Carrow (Series Bible), and "Marrow", one of the eight pump-men on Kaelen's beam (MS ch. 24, l. 6355).
- **Corvin Ash** — Smuggler on Stillwater's quay: "Tobit sells news; I sell what the news is about." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collisions: Corwin Aye (MS ch. 16) and High Patriarch Severin Ashe.
- **Prue Lettice** — Broadsheet-seller from Cantabile, keeper of the board: "SYNOD REASSURES, it says, every week. I put it up anyway." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Tobit Dree** — The lockkeeper at Stillwater, who sells the same news to six parties: the Church's man, the Compact's, a woman from Cantabile with a very small hat, the Reeve's clerk, a Bores factor and a boy. Renamed in R2 from Pellam Dree. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the name; the role is bible canon, Vol II §Settlements: "The lockkeeper is bribable and sells the same information to six parties"). Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: role already in Vol II; the name is new. Near-collision: Tobin, Tobiah, and the tape BEN DRAY.
- **Barnaby Haft** — A Timber Compact cutter at Stillwater with forty iron-bough logs chained behind the lower lock. Renamed in R2 from Orrin Haft. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Bethan Marl** — Neighbour to the shut Tarrow house in Oakhaven's north-west corner, whose kettle boils again when the house is quiet. Renamed in R2 from Wenna Marl, to avoid Abbess Wenna. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Nan Tarrow, Linnet Tarrow (the Tarrows)** — The shut house's family (Phase 13): Nan's First Reed with its tag, and "Linnet's counting-beads, which were on the cot", given by Bethan. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 13; house13.js "[PROPOSED, all of it]"). Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new. **In-game collision:** the canon pass named the hush-gift child Linnet too (below).
- **Aveline Sorrel, Pol Wick, Gerrit Hale, Ruth Marren (THE UNNUMBERED)** — Names chalked on the ledger room wall under THE UNNUMBERED, "names and no numbers, in a clerk's round hand". Aveline was renamed in R2 from Annet Sorrel. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collisions: Wick and Hale are canon surnames (Halder Wick, the Hales).
- **Garrow Finch** — A quarry hauler off shift who runs bones (dice) in the Boar's cellar, one to twenty sovereigns, and shakes his cup inside his coat so it will not rattle. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collision: Sounder-Captain Ulf Garrow (Vol V §The Sounders).
- **Mabyn Verrow** — The Greenroot Cantor (Phase 9), renamed from Ilse because Ilse Brennan is canon (Book Four). Status: **canon (ruled)** (the rename was applied under the owner's library rulings: "two Cantors called Ilse. Ilse Brennan is canon"); the person is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-canon.tsv; commit 052a6a6. Bible: new.
- **Tomas** — The quarry timber-cutter, who now says "Firstfruit", not "August". Status: **canon (ruled)** (library ruling 9). Source: p27-canon.tsv. Bible: already in Vol II §People You Can Use Today ("Reeds-Miner Tomas").
- **Alder Mossbrook (Reeve)** — Witness to the quarry lease, not its signer: "Witnessed: Alder Mossbrook, for the Reeve-Moot." He means to read it aloud at the moot. Status: **canon (book)** (the brainstorm's reading that Aldric signed the Consortium's paper; Release Readiness §2) [the witness role is PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: already in Vol II §People You Can Use Today, where he "signed the quarry lease in 404". **Contradicted** by the game's date (414) and signer (Aldric).
- **Aldric Vance** — The Governor. He signed the quarry lease "Aldric Vance, Governor, 414", and gives the purge countdown over the speaking-tube: "Sixty seconds on the reservoir gates. Anyone below, get to the cage... Get to the cage. I'm counting. Forty-one. Forty." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 10, ll. 3282–3298; Series Bible: he sold the Root). Source: p27-r2.tsv, p32-canon.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **The Synod herald** — He reads the warrant in the square on Dawnday, and at the end, the writ. "His hands shake the entire time, and he does not let it get into his reading." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 24, l. 6196; the warrant ch. 7, l. 2303). Source: p32-canon.tsv, p36-days.tsv. Bible: Series Bible §Part Four — Coda / ch. 24 summary.
