- **Ignatius Crane** — In the chapel from Dawnday with his Legionnaires. From R2 he gives three pellets of glass shot once and does not keep a shop: "The Church does not keep a shop. It gives, once." His warrant: "The Hollow behind the tannery is to be burned at the hour of the Rite. The Cantor is in the Church's protection, which is a phrase I chose myself... and it is a cell." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 7) [the pellets are PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv, p32-canon.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Ignatius Crane.
- **Brannoc Hewe and Sella Marr** — The two Legionnaires inside Lyra's door while she is in the Church's protection. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the names). Source: js/days35.js (GUARDS), p36-days.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Hal Ransome and Edda Quill** — Ignatius's two Legionnaires in the chapel once the Church has come. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: js/maps.js, p36-days.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collisions: Jory Quill (Series Bible) and House-Master Edda Varn (Vol II).
- **Gethin Coote, Osric Dacre, Linus Harte, Bryn Pryor, Elspet Rowe** — Invented names Legionnaires now die saying, replacing Joss Carder, Emory Wain, Wren Halloway, Annet Sowerby and Merret Salt, none of whom can be in a core. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-r2.tsv; ROADMAP R2. Bible: new.
- **Tamsin Reed** — The voice of the Depot Three Legionnaire. Only she murmurs "the blue jug, Mam. Not the white. The blue one"; every other bell now murmurs its own last things. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 2, l. 658; ch. 5, l. 1867). Source: p32-canon.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures and §Part One — The Narrow.
- **The Pump-mender bell** — A Hollow at the pumps says "{name}. I mend pumps. The good spanner is on the shelf by the door... the pump on the third level", replacing its old blue-jug line. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p32-canon.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Kaelen Vance (canon pass)** — "Nobody searches the supervising engineer's toolbag, they say, but the Factor watched me at Depot Three": the coil goes down in your bag as his choice, not as a rule. At the Cloister porch, the Wearer replays his whole confession in his voice: "I calibrated the bore at the Dawning Spire. I signed for the collars. Line ninety-four... Three seconds... That's what happened to the sky, lark." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 7, l. 2491; ch. 19–20). Source: p32-canon.tsv, p38-wearer.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Kaelen Vance (line ninety-four in §Part Three — The Forgetting).
- **Lyra Ashgrove (canon pass)** — In the Church's protection she answers on a slate: "YOU CAME. SIT. I CAN'T TALK. THEY SAY THE VOICE IS NOT TO BE WORN OUT." / "NOT HERE. THEY READ EVERYTHING. WRITE SMALL." After the porch she asks "WHAT DID IT SAY TO YOU?" and writes ASK HIM, then rubs it out. At the coda she plans the ring ("I'm not bait. Bait doesn't sing."), and afterwards says "Say something" to Kaelen, who says nothing. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 9; ch. 20; ch. 23, ll. 6028–6142). Source: p36-days.tsv, p38-wearer.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Lyra Ashgrove.
- **Linnet (the hush-gift child)** — The child the Deacon of the Unrung Bell would have given to the Quiet, saved in the Deep Woods: "Mam said if I sang nicely the Quiet would come and be kind to me." At the fire she sings too loud, and Lyra teaches her the skipping-rhyme and lets her. Status: **canon (book)** (the rename follows the brainstorm's §5 item 18 under the owner's ruling) [the child and the name are PROPOSED]. Source: p32-canon.tsv; js/data.js, js/wilds.js; PLAYTEST: "The hush-gift child is now Linnet (the Bible already has a Wren). Does the name sit right?" Bible: new. Renamed from Wren because Wren Halloway is canon (Series Bible §The Aegis Sentinel). **In-game collision** with Linnet Tarrow, eight (house13.js).
- **The Deacon of the Unrung Bell** — A Deep Woods figure. Answered in Mags's hand-cant, he gives you his clapperless bell and walks away; otherwise he "died without a sound, which is what he wanted". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the scene, from earlier phases; only the child's name changed here). Source: p32-canon.tsv (wording updated for Linnet). Bible: the Unrung Bell is in Series Bible §The Tongueless ("Abbess Wenna of the Unrung Bell").
- **Emory Wain** — Twenty-six, of Cooper's Rise in the Iron Peaks; his mother was Hanne and his sister Ivy. He is the proctor Sable stops in the Sunken Court ambush by reading his surrendered name: "an absolute hitch in a machine". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 15, ll. 4401–4413). Source: p32-canon.tsv; js/grove.js. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("a proctor who surrendered his name to the Office and got it back for one and a half seconds"). The bible lacks his hometown and family. Near-collision: "Hanne of Mirage Rest" (Vol II).
- **Ruth Madder's girl** — Born on Nullday, day 1 of the nine, "with her grandmother's hands"; the first of Bettany's three tellings to the bees. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, ll. 248–278). Source: p32-canon.tsv. Bible: Ruth and Coll Madder are in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **Joram Pellow** — Died in 418; his reed hangs level over his door, and his death is told to the bees. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (earlier game content; reworded in the canon pass). Source: p32-canon.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Ada Pellow** — The Pellow girl Tobin saved. The Hedge speaks in her voice ("Oh — it's easy, come on. It's easy from this side."), and it is one of the voices at the ring. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 11, l. 3556; ch. 23). Source: p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part Two — The Root.
- **Tobin Ashgrove** — "Tobin Ashgrove of the orchard rows. Son of Mara. Cantor of Oakhaven." He was fifty-six and afraid of ladders and nothing else. He went out into the Pellows' yard singing the calling-song so that the hounds would follow him and not the house. His last pages say "Tell Lyra she was right: a martyr is just a musician who gave up before the coda. I am not giving up. I am finishing the piece." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 464; ch. 23, ll. 6050, 6058) [his journal wording is PROPOSED]. Source: p27-canon.tsv, p27-r2.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible (§The Wearer and others). The bible does not name his mother, Mara, or give his age.
- **Hew Callan** — Pump-man of Depot Two, thirty-one, married to nobody, who owes Mags Harker four sovereigns and a Dawnday supper. The Hollow at the pumps whose leaf (nine thousand and six) Umber can read back at the cost of her scriptorium bell; he then comes up the rope behind the Carders saying his own name. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (earlier Sable quest, reassigned to Umber). Source: p40-umber.tsv. Bible: new. Mags Harker is also new (and close to Mags Tennant).
- **Sable (canon pass)** — She never leaves Whisper-Grove until the Kestrel comes. She is first met at the Sunken Court's fountain, always reads Emory Wain his name in the ambush, and spends one fragment, for Bettis ("Entry: one thing, for Bettis Renn, in the Malting. I could not name it afterwards."). At the Deep Woods camp she counts "One wreck, twenty-six souls, a press, and me." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14–18; ch. 21, l. 5494; ch. 16, l. 4476). Source: p40-umber.tsv, p32-canon.tsv; js/data.js sableFragments; commit 3c9b261. Bible: already in Series Bible §Sable.
- **Umber** — "A runaway of the Veil", Sable's junior. She carried the Veil's Loose Leaves (its day-pages) out under her coat when the Office came for the Bound Ledger. She hides in the mist outside Oakhaven's Hedge, hunted by two Catalogue proctors, and asks for three names. Her own three fragments are the taste of the Veil's bread, the scriptorium bell at matins, and her name. She does not come up the rope. "I copied the harvest schedule fair, fourteen times... Sable designed it. I only made it legible." "I am a day-book, not a cure." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (explicitly, in p40-umber.tsv and PLAYTEST). Source: p40-umber.tsv; commit 3c9b261. Bible: new. **Collision:** the Phase 26 library already has an older Veil scribe styled Umber ("What Is Written", 410 SR, fifty-one years in the designation; and the copyist of "The Rule of the House", c. 290 SR).
- **C. Linley** — The ninth name-tape in the Cloister's choir cloaks, replacing Tom Wick. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (p37-halder.tsv: "the book leaves it unread"). Source: p37-halder.tsv; commit 4c17766. Bible: new.
- **The Cloister choir children (the name-tapes)** — M. ORRY, BEN DRAY ("the choir-mistress's own"), A. SOWERBY, Halloran, E. and R. Veske ("probably sisters"), Marchant, Tipp; the ninth is now C. Linley, and the tenth collar has only the holes where its tape was. Ten cloaks hang on eleven pegs. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 20, ll. 5310–5318) [the initials E. and R. and C. Linley are PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv, p37-halder.tsv. Bible: new (no volume lists them). The choir dream's leaning child is now Ben ("Lean left, Ben. Left.").
- **Halder Wick** — A thin old man in a waistcoat, wet to the knee, setting a table for four in a foot of lit water for three years: "...the blue one goes there... they will be in directly... sit, sit, there's four..." The canon pass removed the family the game had given him. His logbook entry ends on Sable's line, and you may only sit in the fourth chair or tell him. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 15, l. 4435: "He set a table for four every evening for three years. I do not know who he was expecting. There is no one left to ask."). Source: p37-halder.tsv, p31-grove.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("entry 12,207, who set a table for four") and Vol V §The Chorister's List.
- **Corwin Aye** — Cooper of the Ashlane. He burned a notched ring and C. AYE into every barrel-head; his barrel has stood in the Moot-house cellar nineteen years without leaking. He came in the second consignment, sixteen crates, eleven days in a ship's hold. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 16, l. 4556). Source: p31-grove.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part Three — The Forgetting.
- **Mirren Salk** — Wheelwright of the Bell Yard; her board reads SALK. WHEELWRIGHT, with newer paint under it: "widow, and the work still taken". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 16, ll. 4510, 4560) [the board is PROPOSED]. Source: js/grove31.js, p31-grove.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part Three — The Forgetting.
- **Osmer Lowry** — Warden of the Lamps, Bell Yard: "Ring at any hour." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (from the Phase 26 library book of the lamp-makers' loft, flyleaf "Osmer Lowry, Kitto Lowry"). Source: js/grove31.js. Bible: new.
- **Edith and Rosamund Amory** — The Bell Yard household whose board carries a slate in a girl's hand: "Rosamund is at the basin." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (from the library's Grove feast-receipts book, flyleaf "Edith Amory, Rosamund Amory"). Source: js/grove31.js, p31-grove.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Grisel Imrie** — Midwife of the Ashlane: "Knock twice and wait; if I am out, I am at a birth." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (from the library's "Day-Book of a Midwife of the Ashlane"). Source: js/grove31.js. Bible: new.
- **The twice-taken man** — In Ashlane a Hollow sits against a wall with his legs out, doing nothing at all, because the Office pressed him a second time. His entry has Sable's struck-through margin note, "This was done because I spoke to him", and under it: "pressed a second time by the Office of the Catalogue on the fourteenth or fifteenth day of Leaffall, 419". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, ll. 4155–4166). Source: p31-grove.tsv. Bible: new (Series Bible §Part Three — The Forgetting mentions "a second time" only in passing).
- **The Bell Yard Hollows** — A Hollow with a broom ("...the step, then the next step... she likes the step done..."), one at a window ("the blue one, and the white one"), one at the shutters ("latched... latched..."). Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Hollows keep a task: **canon (book)**, MS ch. 14, l. 4157). Source: p31-grove.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Quist (Form 9)** — "Ninety seconds. Form 9: three questions and no more. Name. Age. Trade. Further speech is not recorded, as it lengthens the queue. Then eight seconds of seven forty-one, under the felt. Most of them look relieved at six." When Sable asks to put Bettis back: "No. I am sorry. We will hold you until it passes." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14 epigraph, l. 4075; ch. 18, ll. 4970–4981) [the refusal line is PROPOSED]. Source: p32-canon.tsv. Bible: Quist is in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures; Form 9 is new to the bible.
- **Bosun Teller** — The Office cutter's bosun. He counted the Kestrel's hold by ear at 432, scratched on the back of his hold-key tag (TELLER — HOLD), and calls it "Consignment 19, Whisper-Grove stock". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (an earlier game figure, reframed in the canon pass). Source: p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: new. Near-collision: "Maud Teller" (Vol VI §Customs).
- **Tobiah Marsh** — He weighs the cutter's evidence chest and says nothing about the lift, and the Wearer's burst knocks him off his feet at forty yards. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 23, l. 6102). Source: p35-cutter.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **Vane, Tobiah and Mags (setting the lamps)** — They carry the camp's lamps out to the clearing and set the ring. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the MS has Lyra set the ring). Source: p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: n/a.
- **Kaelen's coda lines** — At the coda Kaelen "says nothing at all, which was, for once, precisely right"; he and the Aegis strike once the Wearer is inside the ring. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 23). Source: p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Kaelen Vance.
- **Vane's coda line** — "'And when it comes?' says Vane." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 23). Source: p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: n/a (a line).

### Creatures

#### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

- **Hounds as a "congregation" (417)** — The first hounds came on a Massday night and sat in a row of eleven at the orchard edge, "heads on one side". "They did not howl. They listened." They wait for the evening bell, or anything like one: "the kettle, the cartwheel, the milkmaid's second verse." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 10). Bible: already in SB §Part IV (hounds come to music). The congregation is new.
- **Hounds that work your trail; hunters' moon-lore** — Hounds that lose sight of you follow your recent steps, nose down. Old hunters say the Hush "keeps closest when the sky is empty" and the hounds are bolder on dark nights. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13 (§HUNTERS); P11 (§Moon phases). Bible: new.
- **The thing at the Hedge (dead-hour encounter)** — At the dead hour the Hedge tries Lyra's voice, then a child's. "Answering tells it you are there." A listener under the voices hears "a great many people, far down, all talking at once". If killed, "everything it had eaten came out at once" and it leaves "a patch of ground … where no sound carries". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_hedge_*, d_wearer_dead; Gazette No. 34). Bible: already in SB §Part IV (mimic rule; the Wearer) and V5 §Status (RULING: the Hedge speaks in the voices of the dead). The killable Wearer in Part One conflicts with B1 (see Contradictions).
- **Mire-drone** — "A low, wallowing beast the size of a calf, the colour of the bank it lies in. It does not hunt. It hums, one note, under hearing, and everything near it gets a little heavier." It causes "despair" (the Options name). Status: **canon (book)** (V2 already has Mire-drones; Phase 9 marked the name **[PROPOSED]**). The calf size and heaviness are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§1; §7). Bible: already in V2 §Class I — Resolvables ("bovine-sized … 60 Hz drone … nausea and despair"). Minor mismatch.
- **Fret-crawler** — "Flat as a flagstone and the same grey, with a shell ridged like the neck of a lute." "What it bites it cuts: after it, no Song will hold its pitch." Status: **canon (book)** (V2). The lute-neck shell is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§1; §7). Bible: already in V2 §Class I ("sever intervals, not flesh").
- **The quiet that follows ("the keeping")** — Outside the Hedge a quiet "keeps a little behind me". The river, the leaves or the dripping go silent first, then your steps stop echoing. It takes the text of one logbook line and returns it after the next dawn, "not quite how I wrote it". It never follows into the Hedge, where "sound dies where it falls". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P10 (§DREAD). Bible: new. V4 §1.3 "The Keeping" uses the same word for the trench taking a person.
- **The Unrung Bell** — On some nights "a bell is struck somewhere past the Hedge. The air leans toward the sound, and there is none." The hounds are bolder after. It stops at the Greening. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13 (§The Unrung Bell). Bible: new as an event. The name is already in SB §The Tongueless.
- **The Hushwarden's grey wave** — A Hushwarden's muffling spreads as a grey ring, colour leaves and sound dips. "The felted bell comes down on you." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13 (§HUNTERS). Bible: new (the Hushwarden is not in any bible volume).
- **Carrion crows** — Crows gather at bodies by day and scatter noisily. "Crows have found what is left." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13. Bible: new.
- **Fire against the creatures (lore ruling)** — Fire burns beasts (hounds, swarms, weaverlings, fret-crawlers, Mire-drones) and people, and scares them. The Hollows (Drowned, Dirge) burn but are not afraid: "they have no will to be". Constructs (Steam Golem, Aegis, Listening-Horn, Chord-Warden) take a quarter damage. The Hush (a Quiet, Hush-Moths) is untouched: "The fire does not seem to reach it." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§KIT Lore decisions). Bible: new. SB §Part IV says quiets die to "iron, cold water, and patience". Weaverlings, the Chord-Warden and Hush-Moths are in no bible volume.
- **Cacophony Hound, "Static with appetite"** — Used as the sample Info line. Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part IV). Source: P10 (§Interaction modes). Bible: already in SB §Part IV.

---

#### From Phases 15–25 (B)

- **Briar-sprite** — "Spore-fauna, mutated out of the verge-briar: the size of a cat, all thorn and pale little lamps. It strips leather and bootlace in under a minute, and what it opens does not close quickly." Its bite causes BLEEDING, and its clinging makes legs heavy. Status: **canon (book)** for the creature. Phase 16 wrongly marks it [PROPOSED]; the size, the lamps and the bleed are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Briar-sprite). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Hazard Table — The Reach (#2: "Mutated spore-fauna. Strip leather and boots in under a minute."). The bible is less detailed.
- **Briar in the wrong month** — "Briar in full leaf in the wrong month. It is faintly warm, and something in it breathes." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (`road.briar`). Bible: new.
- **Mire-drones in the Low Rings** — "Something large lies in the shallows ahead, the colour of the bank." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (`rings.journal`, `rings.drone`). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §CLASS I — RESOLVABLES (MIRE-DRONES).
- **The Drowned Hollow underwater** — "Down here nothing comes out of it but silver." Its shriek is silent under water on this map only. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§DEEP canon notes; §Breath…). Bible: new. Hollows are canon.
- **Hounds and ringing timber** — Any blow on grown wood rings "a bell struck in a cupboard", and "a hound lifts its head". This follows the canon rule that since 417 any blow on wood in the Reach rings. Status: **canon (book)** for the rule (Book Four); **[PROPOSED]** for the mechanic. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Ringing timber); Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (prologue limb: "In the Reach, since 417, any blow on wood has rung"). Bible: Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach has "The oaks ring like bronze when the wind moves them". The bible lacks the explicit rule, so it is incomplete there.
- **The Pale Moth (Long Night)** — "A pale moth, drawn to the lamps. It drinks the light first." A lamp with a moth on it "burns three times as fast". Swatting it does not hurt it. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Foes…; §The three keepers, Jory). Bible: new. It is related to the game's Hush-Moths, and canon Quiets are "drawn to light" (Series Bible §Canon added in Book Two).
- **A Hollow (Long Night)** — "One of the Unstrung, walking toward a held voice. It does not hurry, and it does not know it is walking." Turned aside by lamplight, it "sits down where it stands, and is still". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the behaviour); Hollows and the Unstrung are **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Foes…; §In-run messages). Bible: the Unstrung are already in Vol I §III.7.
- **Cacophony Hound ("Static with appetite")** — "It has come for the singer." Status: **canon (book)** for the creature; **[PROPOSED]** for the line. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Foes…). Bible: already in Vol I §PART VII and Vol II §CLASS I.
- **The Woolwight** — "A heap of grey fleeces taller than a man, stitched with thorn, three sheep's heads set in it at the wrong heights. It bleats like a ewe that has lost her lamb." It comes only after dark on Wethercombe, the Downs and the Hanger, keeps out of firelight ("Nothing that wears wool likes fire"), makes decoy bleats, and is gone by dawn. It has been pulling Hedge-wool out from under the Hedge's roots and wearing it. When it attacks, the wool splits "and out of it, on a neck of raw meat, comes a bull's skull with the skin gone and the jaw hanging open". Killed, "it comes apart: wool, and wool, and Hedge-thorn, and nothing in the middle". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. The bull's-skull reveal is noted as the owner's request, and the ROADMAP records "a night hunter dressed as sheep" as the owner's request; neither is a ruling on the lore. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§22b What is new; `foes.woolwight`, `hunt.*`, `woolDone`); ROADMAP.md (Phase 22 entry). Bible: new.
- **Lambkin** — "A ball of wet wool on twig legs, the size of a lamb. It bleats, very small, and bites." It is one of the Woolwight's small ones. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`foes.lambkin`). Bible: new.
- **The Old Fleece** — "The oldest of them, the size of a cart, fleeces to the roof, a bell at its throat with the clapper wrapped in wool." It waits in Ewe Barrow's last chamber: "It has been under the barrow a long time." When it falls, "its bell rolls across the floor … and for the first time it rings". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`foes.oldfleece`, `boss`). Bible: new.
- **Tamar's Rust Coast ewes and the tup** — Eleven dark-faced ewes "in a fleece the colour of old cream", and a tup with a bell at his throat: "the bellwether. Where he goes, the flock goes." Strays carry the Farrants' ear-notch. "The ewes will not come indoors." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§Sheep). Bible: new. The bible has no sheep at all.
- **The glass-eel** — "An eel you can see through, lit faintly from inside, caught after dark." It restores voice; roasted, "your throat feels like a struck bell". It may be the "long and faintly lit" thing in the Hanger pool. Phase 22b Question 4 asks fantasy fish or plain eel, and it was never answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.glasseel*`; `pool`). Bible: new.
- **Chalk-stream fish** — Brown trout ("speckled red"), striped perch ("all spines"), and a drowned boot "full of water and a crayfish". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§Items). Bible: new.
- **Fantasy flora of the Sheep-Walk** — Bell-ash, glass-thorn, lantern-bells, whistle-grass and echo-poppies:
  - **Bell-ash:** "Its seed-keys are bronze, and in any wind at all the tree rings, very small, like a shop door a long way off."
  - **Glass-thorn:** "a hawthorn whose haws have set as beads of Light-glass. Each one holds a point of light, day or night."
  - **Lantern-bells:** a woodland flower that "after dark … give[s] off a pale blue light, enough to read a ledger by, if the ledger is small". It is also the only light in Ewe Barrow.
  - **Whistle-grass:** "hollow and silver. In a wind the slope hums with it."
  - **Echo-poppies:** red. "Shepherds say they remember the last thing said near them. Nobody says anything near them."

  These strings are in `js/sheepwalk22.js` and `tools/i18n/p22-sheepwalk.tsv` but **missing from the Phase 22 doc**. Status: **[PROPOSED]**; the ROADMAP records "lusher flora with fantasy species" as the owner's request. Source: tools/i18n/p22-sheepwalk.tsv; js/sheepwalk22.js (lines 91–95, 484–486); ROADMAP.md (Phase 22 Flora). Bible: new.
- **Ordinary flora of the Sheep-Walk** — Also missing from the doc: "A beech, smooth and grey as a seal"; "An ash, last into leaf and first out of it"; "A yew, older than the barrow it guards, or nearly"; hawthorn red with haws; elder black with berries; bracken; cow parsley; reedmace; foxgloves; harebells; nettles ("They know where the muck is"); thistle; vetch. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: tools/i18n/p22-sheepwalk.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Hush-Mite Swarm (GDD)** — Found in cellars and the meadow; it "raises Hush in battle". The Carders' root-cellar tutorial using it was never built (Release Readiness §3 #2). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§5 ch. 1; §6). Bible: new. Not in any book either.
- **Rootling (GDD)** — "Burl-sick overgrowth" on the quarry terraces: *Take Root* binds a party member. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§6). Bible: new.
- **Steam Golem (GDD)** — A Root-vault boss pair that grapples and vents. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§6). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §The Iron Synod and §Part Two — The Root.
- **The Wearer (GDD's "unkillable in Book One")** — It learns your voice. Status: **canon (book)** for the creature; **[PROPOSED]** for "unkillable". The game later added a killable Hedge fight; see Contradictions. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§6). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures; it bursts in §Part Four — Coda (ch. 23).
- **Hounds drawn clean (GDD presentation)** — Hounds "are never drawn clean": a heat-haze shader "with a row of mouths down the back, as the book describes them". Status: **canon (book)**, per the GDD's claim; not checked. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§7). Bible: new as a look.
- **Rear and side views of the 25 species; A Quiet as an absence** — Every back and side view is invented from the front painting. Pressman Mallow's hand-press on his back is canon. A Quiet is drawn "as an absence with smoke", so the Hush is never shown. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 #9). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 9). Bible: new.
- **The Whisper-Grove cat** — Walks as a grey tabby seen side-on. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 20 #8). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 8). Bible: new.
- **Death pictures and shared-picture mapping (PAINTER)** — These were cleared to merge on story grounds.
  - The Weaver family: a web across a ruined archway, with no spider shown.
  - The Mausoleum choir: a marble choirmaster with lilies.
  - The Hollows: a flooded street, far figures standing still.
  - Shared pictures: the Cutter-Man and Bosun Teller share the grapnel picture; Outriders, Hushwardens and the Outrider-Captain share the bell-helm; the Tongueless, the Deacon and Hush-Moths share the Quiet picture, an empty lit room; Mire-drones, Fret-crawlers, Briar-sprites and Long Night hounds share the beast picture; the Aegis shares the iron fist.

  Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 §3). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§3). Bible: new.

---

#### From Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

- **The Wearer (one Wearer)** — The Cacophony that ate Tobin Ashgrove. Book One has one Wearer, never fought until ch. 23, and the game now matches. At the Oakhaven mast it only speaks in your voice from the thorn ("Grab the line.") and sends three hounds; it "will not come into the light where the town is". At the Cloister porch it stands in the fog and will not come in. At the ring of lamps it circles in Tobin's voice, then Lyra's, then yours if it learned you. It "has no face. It has the place where a face was not, rippling", and one strike bursts it: "It had eaten a Cantor and four others and two years of a woman's nights." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 20; ch. 23, ll. 6084–6112). Source: p38-wearer.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv; commits f6e24bb, 90e2680. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("courts Lyra at the Hedge, and for two years it has also been listening outside Kaelen's hut") and §Part IV ("Wearers have eaten a person whole"). The bible says nothing of the mast scene or the "never comes where people are" rule.
- **Its rule: "It won't come where people are"** — "In two years it has never crossed the Hedge, or come through the culvert, or set foot in the Hollow, and at the Cloister it stood in the fog and would not come into the room." "I only know what people say out loud." It cannot read. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 20, l. 5406, "I can't read"; ch. 23). Source: p38-wearer.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: new as a stated rule. The Series Bible's mimic rule covers answering, not reading or crowds.
- **Hounds and hound packs** — Hounds near one another run as a pack behind a larger leader. A falling fifth that stops the leader breaks the pack "with a sound like tearing silk" for a few seconds. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the leader mechanic); hounds hunting in packs is in the bible. Source: js/narrow28.js, p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: Series Bible §Part IV ("Hounds hunt in packs and come to music").
- **A burst hound's dead patch** — Where a hound bursts the ground goes dead for a while: "Your footsteps stop arriving", and no Song carries. Status: **canon (book)** (the hound's bed, where "no sound carries over it for a day": MS ch. 2, l. 614; Series Bible §Part One — The Narrow) [the forty seconds is the game's]. Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p27-r2.tsv. Bible: the hound's bed is already in Series Bible §Part One — The Narrow.
- **The hound's bed** — "A patch of ground a little longer than a big dog, where the dew has not formed. No sound carries over it for a day. Your own footsteps arrive by bone." It lies twenty paces from Kaelen's door; it is not twenty paces across. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 2, l. 614). Source: p27-r2.tsv; commit 6e03ed0. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part One — The Narrow.
- **Quiets** — "They go to a lit lantern, the way moths would if moths ate sound." In the cutter's hold, unstoppering the twelve vox-bottles lets one through: "the air beside the hold stops carrying sound." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the lantern rule as a mechanic; the facts are bible canon). Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part IV and §Canon added in Book Two ("drawn to light; come aboard through unstoppered vox-bottles").
- **Hush-mites** — The humming in the Carders' siphoned root-cellar: "a Cacophony swarm, the hush-mites: the falling fifth stops them, and a blade finishes them". Clearing them lets the damp go back into the ground. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the GDD's Hush-Mite tutorial; hollow30.js). Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: "Swarms of mites and leeches strip a room of sound" (Series Bible §Part IV). The name is new and risky: Vol IV §1.7 rules that the Hush is not the Cacophony.
- **The woolwight** — The Downs' predator, still hunting when the Reach is left ("THE WOOLWIGHT 1 EWE" on the Bellwether's slate). Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 22). Source: p27-r1.tsv, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Jitters** — "Something has learned you. Out in the orchard, three jitters lift their heads." Status: **canon (book)** (jitter as the Cacophony's visible shimmer: MS ch. 1, l. 484; Series Bible §Part IV). Source: p38-wearer.tsv.
- **The Aegis Sentinel (porch and ring)** — Nine feet of bronze, DEFEND THE SINGERS stencilled across its chest. At the porch it comes off its mark toward a "singer" in the fog singing Hymn 9 in Lyra's voice. You hold the doorway (it stops eighteen inches from you, trying to hold two instructions at once) or step aside (it walks out after nothing, comes back, and kneels by the risers). At the ring it strikes "one second behind" Kaelen, "because DEFEND THE SINGERS is four words long". Without Sable it sits with you at the fire and chimes the alto line (R1). Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 17, ch. 20, ch. 23) [the hold-or-step-aside choice is PROPOSED]. Source: p38-wearer.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv, p27-r1.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §The Aegis Sentinel.
- **The Requiem (the older thing in the choir)** — "Not the Wearer. Something older. It has every office ever sung there", now including Tobin's. It bursts into "three hundred years of funeral offices", and the House is "only a building now". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: new. "Three hundred years" clashes with Vol V's eleven hundred years of the House.

### Institutions, law and customs

#### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

##### The Synod Gazette and Office dialect

- **The Synod Gazette** — A broadsheet "printed under the Pure Note" by the Oakhaven District Desk. It is pinned at the Greenhall front, the Chapel front and inside the Boar. Its front page follows the story, with one "Local matters" item. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§2; §3 UI). Bible: new.
- **Gazette No. 31, "The Pure Note is to be held over Oakhaven"** — It announces a Rite of Pure Unison for Hearthday, "Bread will continue", and the hands-and-feet inspection with chalk in Rootday week. "The district has no bells. Reports of ringing are to be treated as weather." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§2 No. 31). Bible: new. The Rite naming conflicts with the No. 36 fix (see Contradictions).
- **Gazette No. 32, "The Cantor is honoured"** — "Identified as Source for the purposes of Stage Nine. This is an honour. It has not been made optional." "Absence of long standing is not a disappearance." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6. Bible: new. Stage Nine itself is canon (SB ch2).
- **Gazette No. 33, "Quarry roster under routine reconciliation"** — "Persons without a roster number are not on the quarry." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6. Bible: new.
- **Gazette No. 34, "Acoustic echo artifacting at the Hedge"** — "The dead are not speaking. The dead are being echoed." "Saying nothing at the Hedge is a civic contribution." Status: **[PROPOSED]** ("acoustic echo artifacting" itself is **canon (book)**, V4 §Dialect). Source: P6. Bible: already in V4 §Dialect and V1 §Grades (the phrase only).
- **Gazette No. 35, "A coil is unaccounted for"** — "It is not missing. Missing is a grade the Consortium has not assigned." The Governor's son's presence "will be minuted as attendance". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6. Bible: new.
- **Gazette No. 36 retitled "The Hold over Oakhaven proceeds"** — The issue was first titled "THE RITE OF PURE UNISON PROCEEDS" (the body heading still reads so). Review renamed it because the Rite of Pure Unison is the earlier Sol-Luminary rite. Its text: Cantor "in the Church's protection for the duration of the hold, estimated at four hours … Protection is not detention." Status: **cut or changed** (the old title is replaced by "THE HOLD OVER OAKHAVEN PROCEEDS"). Source: P6 (header note; §2 No. 36). Bible: the four-hour hold is already in SB ch2. See Contradictions.
- **Gazette No. 37, "An agricultural irregularity"** — "The bore was retired by being broken. This is a scheduling matter." "Oaks that ring are to be treated as weather." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6. Bible: new.
- **Gazette No. 38, "Oakhaven: a classification update"** — "The district formerly styled Oakhaven is reclassified as woodland." "Departures by air … recorded as weather." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6. Bible: new. V5 confirms Oakhaven is a wood.
- **Gazette: local matters and the later Office lines** — "A resident has been found in a place other than the place to which they were assigned. They have been reconciled to that place." Ringing timber is "a property of the timber … not … of the Reach". Greenroot "is recorded as consolidated … Correspondence addressed to the parish will be answered by the file." The Last Sheaf is "agricultural". The Rite is "a district festival", where "absence will be recorded as a preference". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§2 Local matters); P9 (Gazette lines); P12 (Gazette items). Bible: new.
- **The Cantabile Candour** — Pell's free-press sheet, found "folded behind the notice, in a different hand". It questions the Office's words "components" and "replacement" for the corsair's cargo. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§2 Free-press counterpoint). Bible: new. Broadsheet Row is already in V2.
- **Office dialect ("reconciled", "a scheduling matter", "weather")** — The Office never uses a verb with a person as its object: "Reconciled, he said. We both drank to that." Status: **canon (book)** (V4 §Dialect). Source: P6 (x_office; Gazette throughout). Bible: already in V4 §Dialect.

##### Oakhaven law and trade

- **Oakhaven watch, bounty and warrant** — The constables "are considering, which is how paper starts". A bounty of 40 or more posts a warrant. "A slap is paperwork in Oakhaven. A bruise is a bounty." Setting fire in the street adds 35 to your bounty, and smashing an owned crate is "damage to goods". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 c_*); P12 (KIT); P14 (PROPS). Bible: new.
- **The Writ of Clearing (bounty board)** — The Greenhall board paid thirty-five sovereigns "for the culling of a pack on the north road". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§1 d_bounty_board). Bible: new.
- **The courier** — "A courier has found you with a letter," arriving at the next rest or arrival. Letters are kept in the logbook. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§Courier letters). Bible: new.
- **The quarry lease of 414** — The Consortium of Bores takes "the quarry, the shaft, and 'such resonances as lie beneath'". In return comes bread: "a cart a week, at a fixed price, for nine years." Tobin alone voted to refuse. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 5). Bible: new as terms. **Contradicted** on date and form (see Contradictions).
- **The Synod's timber quota** — "The Synod counts timber by the load." A man one load short on quota day loses his wagon and mare. The Consortium "likes timber that rings; they can hear the bore from the road". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (Tomas). Bible: new. V2 has the Timber Compacts.
- **The inspection of children's hands and feet** — Chalk is applied "where indicated. Applied chalk is not an accusation." It falls in Rootday week at the Greenhall. Status: **canon (book)** (B1 ch3, the inspection). The Gazette wording is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Gazette No. 31; rumours d_nell_wait). Bible: already in SB §Part One ch3 (inspection).
- **Proctors' seals on the Cantor's shutters** — "They call it the Church's protection. I call it a very tidy cell." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the protection is **canon (book)**, SB ch7). Source: P6 (d_lyra_taken; Gazette No. 36). Bible: already in SB ch7 (protection).
- **The Church's Greenroot file ("consolidated")** — "The clerks write consolidated against parishes they cannot reach." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§8 Ignatius). Bible: new.

##### Reach customs

- **The First Reed** — A reed is tuned to each child "before they can lift their head" and hung over the door. "In the silenced places not one of them sounds." Status: **canon (book)** (V1 §4.3; V7b §2 Reed-Hanging). Phase 9 had marked it **[PROPOSED]** as "inferred from the Ada Pellow material". Source: P6 (x_reed; dream "The First Reed"); P9 (items; Lyra); P13. Bible: already in V1 §4.3 and V7b §2.
- **The Last Note, counted in eights** — "The family holds their pitch until the body lets go, and counts it out in eights, one for each Anchor. On the eighth, the note is let go. We do it in silence now." At Greenroot, three chairs face a fourth. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the eight-count; the Last Note in silence is **canon (book)**, V1 §4.3). Source: P13 (Wenna; nursery); P9 (§6 chairs). Bible: already in V1 §4.3. The eight-count is new.
- **Stone on the tongue** — "I put the stone on his tongue when he let go." Status: **canon (book)** (SB §The Resonant Reach; V5 §Reach Customs). Source: P9 (Ada). Bible: already in SB and V5.
- **Singing the dead "down" / "out"** — "In the old days [the note] was sung out after. It cannot be now. The Hedge eats a note." "A Cantor is supposed to sing a place out." Orla is to be sung down "proper". Status: **canon (book)** (being sung down proper is in B1 and SB §Audit). The note-sung-out custom is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (Ada "The Hedge"); P6 (l2_plain; Orla). Bible: already in SB §Audit (Orla's promise).
- **Telling the bees** — "You tell the bees, in the Reach, every birth and every death, or they go." Since the Hedge it must be a murmur "close, at the straw". "Bees mind a shout worse than hounds do." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§Telling the Bees). Bible: new.
- **A household pitch-board** — Four notches cut in a plank by the door, each with a name in chalk (GIL, MAUD, LINNET, NAN), the lowest cut deepest. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13 (§Front room). Bible: new.
- **Tuning a reed against the breastbone** — A Cantor tunes each reed "against my own breastbone before I cut the slot". "A cane keeps the cut it was given, and the cut was made for one pitch." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 1); P9 (reedcraft; Pellow). Bible: new.
- **Tending custom: "sing nothing to a tree before the third week"** — "The old men say sing nothing to a tree before the third week, and then only the root note, a hand's width above the ground, with the mouth nearly closed." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 1). Bible: new. The tending notes are canon (SB).
- **Hush-lamps on every sill; night-hummers** — "Keep a hush-lamp lit on every sill. A room that goes quiet is a door left open." Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part IV, hush-lamps and night-hummers). The saying is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (a_lamps; w_night; Tobin page 7). Bible: already in SB §Part IV.
- **The Rota in Oakhaven (one duty a day)** — Each weekday one person does the god's duty quietly. Rootday: stand at a tree for the Root's hour. Hearthday: keep the hearth-stone warm. Massday: stand on the old floor of the square. Stasisday: wind the dial and name the hour once. Dawnday: open a shutter for an hour. Driftday: walk the Flume channel. Memoryday: say one name at the Hedge side. Nullday: keep the quiet end of town. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§The Rota). Bible: already in V6 §0.1 The Rota (itself marked [PROPOSED]) and V7 §4. The Oakhaven street practice is new.
- **Canopy in 419** — "The Canopy Feast would fall today. It has not been held in the open since 416." Candles stand at the backs of windows and on a doorstep "with its back to the road". "A candle is not a feast." Status: **canon (book)** (V7 §4; V7b §3 records the candles "in the game since Phase 11"). The wording is game text. Source: P11 (§Canopy). Bible: already in V7 §4 and V7b §3.
- **The Massday market** — There is a market every Massday in the east square. "Prices are fair on Massday. Everyone is watching everyone else's scales." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Market day). Bible: new.
- **Bones (tavern dice) and "a Quiet"** — Closest to fourteen wins, up to four dice. Exactly fourteen "pays double". Two ones on the first throw is "a Quiet: the cup is void". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Bones panel). Bible: new.
- **Birth signs (the seven modes)** — Ionian, the Open Door. Dorian, the Cloister Walk. Phrygian, the Hound's Step. Lydian, the Floating Light. Mixolydian, the Road-Song. Aeolian, the Mourner's Hour. Locrian, the Broken Fifth ("you notice what is missing"). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Birth signs). Bible: new.
- **Hedge-weavers and hush-cloth** — The alder flute comes "packed in hush-cloth by the Hedge-weavers". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§SONGCRAFT). Bible: new. Related: V1 lists "dampening felt (Null-Watch)".
- **A sexton's tolling fee** — "By custom a sexton pays the fee for a tolling. It is nine sovereigns." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Amos Fell letter). Bible: new.
- **Greenroot burial customs** — A grave has "a reed set in the ground at its head instead of a name". Bone niches each have a reed hung "for whoever it was kept for". The carved verses read "HOLD IT AS THE FAMILY HOLDS IT … FOUR BELLS TO A HOUSE. NOBODY RINGS ONE OUT OF TURN." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§3, §6). Bible: new.
- **The Bound Ledger's price** — "The Bound Ledger keeps a name only at a price, weight for weight. I carry it open so that I never forget it is a debt." Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Sable). Source: P9 (Sable "The Bound Ledger"). Bible: already in SB §Sable.

##### Festivals (Phase 12, on the approved Festivals addendum)

- **The Last Sheaf (festival)** — The last stand is cut together, one cut to a beat, singing. It is "the one sheaf that stays cut. Everything else grows back by morning and tastes of less." The figure hangs over the Hedge-gate until spring. Status: **canon (book)** (V7b §2; V1 §III.1 grain regrowth). Source: P12 (§The Last Sheaf). Bible: already in V7b §2.
- **The Last Sheaf in 419 (game staging)** — The sheaf is hung on day 2 and the Church posts a postponement notice on day 4: "Gatherings in a field are gatherings. No sheaf is to be cut before notice is given. Notice will be given." Bunting and small sheaves on lamp-posts tied with red cord. "My mother cut the last stand with eighty people." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§Invented facts; §The Last Sheaf). Bible: already in V7b §2 (postponed, never held in the open). The bible is incomplete on these details.
- **The secret cut at the dead hour** — Sixteen sickles on sixteen beats, nobody speaking. If a patrol passes, the line lies down in the stubble, and "Nobody is punished for a cut that did not get cut." In the morning "the Church will find the stubble and say nothing, which is its way of counting." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (V7b allows the option). Source: P12 (§Side quest; §The cut). Bible: already in V7b §2 and §5 (the option). In canon the sheaf is "still standing" (see Contradictions).
- **The Rite as a false festival** — White banners on every roof-peg and censers lit. Everyone must assemble in the square at noon. "Absence will be recorded as a preference." Bread is provided afterwards. Sermons are read: "One note, held. One town, held under it. This is the peace of the Pure Note." Status: **canon (book)** (V7b §3, §5). The sermons, notice and noon assembly are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§The Rite as a false festival). Bible: already in V7b §3 and §5.
- **The Eel-Run (game staging)** — The night of Driftday 12 Harvestmonth. "The Driftday walker went down the bank at dusk and lit the first lantern. That is how we know." Paper lanterns on stakes, wicker traps weighted with stones, a reed-line, "six eels … the rest of the Flume is for tomorrow's hunger". "It is like prayer, only it works." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the festival is **canon (book)**, V7b §2 Eel-Run). Source: P12 (§The Eel-Run). Bible: already in V7b §2 and §5. The bible is incomplete on these details.
- **The Drowned Lanterns (game staging)** — "The water in the Sunken Court has come up in the night, the way it does once a year." A few hundred paper lanterns are set out, and the last is always "For the ones not written down". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the festival is **canon (book)**, V7b §4). Source: P12 (§The Drowned Lanterns). Bible: already in V7b §4 and §5.

##### Beyond the Reach

- **Line-talk** — "The garrison at Null-Watch talks all night along the whole line, never stopping, because silence invites the Quiet." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (x_nullwatch; r_line; Codex). Bible: already in V1 §III.8 and §4.2; V2; V6 §1.
- **Order of Service for Those Lowered** — For those lowered into the Rift whom nobody could bury. The Cantor sings the response and the name is spoken. "The garrison returns the response from the lead ramparts at dawn, over the gun. If no answer comes, the name is spoken again." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (r_lowered). Bible: new. Compare V6 §1 The Discharge, which is the Watch's only funeral rite.
- **The Rift baskets (Basket 212)** — "The Rift opened in 413 and baskets have gone down for six years. We do not write what we send them for." Basket 212 came up holding "one boot, laced". Status: **canon (book)** (V3b §Timeline corrections; V6 §2). Basket 212 is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (r_baskets). Bible: already in V3b and V6 §2 The rope.
- **The Compact of Stone (Fortis)** — "A standing army that elects its own command … never been conquered and has never won anything." "Growth sounds at 174 and Mass at 396 … It calls it a fifth, and expects it to hold." Status: **canon (book)** (V1 §III.4 and §1.3 Bedrock Fifth). Source: P6 (p_compact; x_bedrock; x_fortis). Bible: already in V1 §III.4 and §1.3; V5 §1.
- **The Compact's harness order** — "Lodestone cores for three keels … to be collected from the Vaults by a man whose word has mass." "A promise weighs nothing until it has been kept." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (p_order). Bible: new.

---

#### From Phases 15–25 (B)

##### The Church and the Synod (Phase 15)

- **The Pure Note sermons** — Held at the Chapel of the Carillon on three days:
  - Dawnday at first light ("First light", 06:30–09:00).
  - Stasisday at dusk ("The hour", 17:30–19:30; the hour is named once and "the whole nave lets it lie").
  - Memoryday in the evening ("The names", 18:00–20:30).

  "Hearthday. Attendance is a courtesy. I would come. It will be a long note." They end at the Greening, because the Synod does not go to Whisper-Grove. Sitting one out raises standing with the Church; disturbing one ("The congregation stands as one and files out, eyes down") is "noted". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Lore notes; §Sermon…; §Sitting…; §Disturbing…). Bible: new. Vol VII (b) §3 has only the Rite's "compulsory sermon".
- **"The dead are being echoed" (Church doctrine)** — The Church's refrain: since the Grove went quiet the Echo no longer catches the dead of its own accord, so the Church "catches" them. It keeps "every name in one book, in one hand, kept dry", will "take the weight from you" at Unison, and asks names be said "in the Note's key, so that it is carried and not scattered". Phase 15 flagged the premise for lore review, but it matches canon. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the Church's claim); the premise is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (header; §Lore notes; §Sermon: the names). Bible: the premise is already in Vol I - The Expanded World Bible.md §1.9 ("Since the Silencing of Memory, the Echo no longer catches") and Vol V §The House of the Silent Veil. The Church's appropriation is new.
- **Saying a name at the Hedge on Memoryday** — "Some of you say a name at the Hedge on this day and tell no one whose. We do not forbid it." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Sermon: the names). Bible: new.
- **The Gazette notice on worship** — "The Chapel of the Carillon will receive the district for the Pure Note on Dawnday, Stasisday and Memoryday. Attendance is a courtesy. Disturbances of worship are an administrative matter." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Gazette notice). Bible: new.
- **The Synod's reach by story stage (map wash)** — In order: "the quarry roads and the eastern marches"; "the roads to the Hedge are Church-walked"; "the Note is held over Oakhaven"; "Oakhaven is struck from the roll, and the wash moves on". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Map legend). Bible: new.
- **The Synod checkpoint** — A trestle across the road, two Proctors "in grey oilcloth" who "regard the middle distance, as they are trained to", and a clerk at a folding desk with a brass RECONCILIATION plate. "Papers … For the record." A "voluntary declaration of property" is a search. The toll is eight sovereigns: "A toll is not a bribe. It is a toll." A grapnel gun stamped "Office issue. OC-742" is "a matter for the Catalogue". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Roadside event: a Synod checkpoint). Bible: new.

##### Reach customs on the roads and in the town (Phase 15)

- **The road's graves** — Graves along the verge after the Greening, of several kinds:
  - stones with a silent reed driven in beside each;
  - eleven small unnamed cairns, one with a child's laced boot;
  - "A wheel stood upright in the earth, rim to the sky, for a carter";
  - a cut reed on a post at every tenth grave "for the Last Note … held … in silence now, because the note does not carry";
  - a name scratched with a nail, and under it "Come back for it."

  Status: **[PROPOSED]** for the graves; **canon (book)** for the Last Note. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Roadside event: the road's graves). Bible: the Last Note is already in Vol I - The Expanded World Bible.md §4.3 Rites of the Life Cycle; the graves are new.
- **Burial by the Hedge, face to the road** — "We buried him this morning, by the Hedge, face to the road. Nobody sang. We have mostly stopped, in the lanes." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Lasting losses, next 1). Bible: new.
- **The reed over the door** — A burned-out family "took the reed from over the door. It had stopped sounding long ago, but they took it." Status: **canon (book)** for the custom; the line is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Houses that burn…, lines 3). Bible: already in Vol I §III.1 (Culture: "a tuned reed above the door, pitched to the child's Signature").
- **Fire, rebuilding and the Flume pump** — "We carried the pump up from the Flume by hand." "The roof went first. You could hear the tiles, ringing. I have not heard anything ring like that in two years." "It will be spring before it is a house again. If there is a spring." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Houses that burn and are rebuilt). Bible: new.
- **Refugees leaving before the Greening** — "Families from the outlying farms are walking out of the Reach … None of them looks back at the Hedge." "We are going where there are still Cantors." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Families on the road). Bible: new.
- **The congregation's habits** — "Sit at the back, say nothing, leave before the last word. That is how everyone does it." "I come for the warmth. And for the count at the door." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Townsfolk on a sermon day; §Congregation whispers). Bible: new.

##### Money, training and games (Phase 15)

- **Letters of credit** — Consortium letters payable to the bearer, at 25, 100 or 500 sovereigns. "Gold in, paper out." They weigh nothing. "Lose it and the promise is the finder's." Agents cash them for a fee: the Consortium clerk 5%, the Veil keeper 15%, and Mags's "captain who takes it a quarter off" 25%. "Do not unfold one near the lamp." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Phase 15 LEDGER; §Letters…). Bible: new. Sovereigns are canon (Vol I §4.4).
- **Paid training (who teaches what)** — Skill, cap, gate and line for each teacher:

  | Teacher | Skill | Cap | Gate or line |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Old Harrow | Stealth | 45 | |
  | Vane | Blade | 60 | |
  | Bettany | Physician | 55 | "Willow-bark, reed-pith, moon-moss: which steeps and which scalds"; "Past that is Rooke's trade" |
  | Hob Wendle | Mercantile | 50 | "Past that you are a Consortium factor" |
  | Lyra | Law-Song | 60 | only after Hearthday, by slate once voiceless: "I HAVE NO VOICE. I STILL HAVE THE SHAPES. SIT." |
  | Kaelen | Glasswork | 55 | "Past that it stops being work and starts being a diagnosis" |
  | Sable | Lore | 60 | "the gaps between them … The rest is in the Bound Ledger, and the Bound Ledger is not for lending" |
  | Mags | Climbing | 60 | "You climb like a Kestrel hand now" |

  Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Teachers; §Training…). Bible: new. The Bound Ledger is canon (Series Bible §Sable).
- **Rounds (card game)** — Twenty-four cards in four suits, Bells, Reeds, Glass and Roots, ranked 1, 2, 3, Deacon, Factor and Cantor from low to high. The turned card names trumps. Four tricks win the stake, six pay double, three apiece is a push. It is played at the Boar's east-window table. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Rounds panel and rules). Bible: new. Release Readiness flags dice at the Boar, not cards; see the Boar entry below.
- **Camp cooking and the warmths** — Cooking at camp fires, the Boar's hearth and your own house on Cooper's Row. A good meal gives a "warmth": Smoke-warmed, Hearth-fed or Easy throat. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dishes and warmths). Bible: new.

##### Stillwater, the Compact and the quarry (Phase 16)

- **The Timber Compact** — Cutters "of the Timber Compact" chalk iron-bough for Fortis and sign boards "in a Compact hand". The steward: "we cut what the Consortium counts". A strike fund pays out, and the Compact can refuse to cut: "The Consortium has called it an act of weather." Status: **canon (book)** for the institution (Vol II: "furious, one strike away from open revolt"); **[PROPOSED]** for the details. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Flume Road sign; §Orrin Haft; §Depot Three; §Rumours). Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Local Powers and §Scene Hooks #3.
- **Lock dues at Stillwater** — "LOCK DUES PAID IN ADVANCE. LOCK DUES NOT REFUNDED." The Gazette later reports: "The lock is recorded as having opened itself. The record is not disputed." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Stillwater Landing; §Gazette). Bible: new.
- **Grown wood remembers one note** — Singing the Root note low at grown timber opens it: "The chain slides off its post as if it had been waiting to be asked." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (`landing.songOpen`, `orrin.accept`). Bible: new.
- **Restorative Administration rules (Whisper-Grove Station)** — Three notices:
  - "Residents of the Loop Quarters are not to be addressed by name … Repetition is not conversation."
  - "Bread: one loaf per household per Memoryday. Households are counted by the places set at table. Where a place is set for an absent member, the place is not counted."
  - "Clerks who find they have said a sentence twice are to stop work, record the sentence, and report … This is not a disciplinary matter."

  The stores board, in three hands, reads CONVOY OF 409. FLOOD RELIEF. / Stores reassigned to Restorative Administration. / No further issue. Workers unable to continue. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Notices and documents). Bible: Vol I §III.7 has "Relief convoys stopped in 409 because the workers could not continue" and "under restorative administration"; the notices are new.
- **Held letters at the Chapel** — The Gazette (No. 32, earlier game) says messages "may be left at the Chapel, where they will be held". Held means held:
  - The pigeonholes are stamped HELD PENDING RECONCILIATION OF THE ADDRESSEE.
  - The letters are tied in fives with red tape, under the slip "To be released on instruction of the Office. No instruction received. No instruction expected."
  - Letters are resealed, never opened.
  - The sexton's strongbox is marked HOLDING FEES: "a sovereign a letter, to hold what should have been delivered for nothing".
  - Families wrote their own name cards "so the letters would be found".

  Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The vestry and undercroft). Bible: new.
- **Hush-lamps' reed-whistle** — "A hush-lamp. Its reed-whistle keeps the room from going silent." Dousing it makes the dark "a good deal thicker", which is used for stealth. Status: **canon (book)** for the lamp and its whistle (Book One ch. 1); **[PROPOSED]** for the dousing mechanic. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The vestry and undercroft; §The Factor's safe…); Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Gallery props). Bible: hush-lamps are already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part IV — The Rules of Dread and §Part XII.
- **The quarry's reconciliation practice** — Worksheets footed "carried as present, paid as absent", a CARRIED AS PRESENT stamp, and a chalk tally of bore-bites beside names whose strokes outnumber the names "by about a third". "Persons without a roster number are not on the quarry." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Factor's safe, the documents…). Bible: new. The Office voice "processed, assigned, graded, reconciled" is canon in Vol IV §Dialect (Organum).
- **"The Unnumbered"** — A chalk list headed THE UNNUMBERED: "names and no numbers, in a clerk's round hand". The names are Annet Sorrel (now Aveline Sorrel), Pol Wick, Gerrit Hale and Ruth Marren. "Some have been struck out, and written in again." Status: **[PROPOSED]**; the word is listed as invented. Annet Sorrel's name is **cut or changed** to **Aveline Sorrel** (ROADMAP.md §R2, Near-collisions). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§HEIST header; §The Factor's safe…). Bible: new.
- **Hymn hours and the Warden's custom** — The notice board: STASISDAY AT DUSK. MEMORYDAY IN THE EVENING. DAWNDAY AT FIRST LIGHT. "Warden to stand with the deacon. Vestry stair to be latched." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dessa Quill and Orrin Pale). Bible: new.

##### Port Cantabile law and custom (Phase 17)

- **The Mole-bell rule** — "One stroke for every soul that goes into the water. Then nobody speaks, so the harbour can count. That is the whole grammar." "Nobody rings it for anything else." "You do not ask whose." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 13, 124, 145). Bible: already in Vol IV §The town as written and Vol V §Customs ("The Coast does not ring it for traitors").
- **The reef's keys and the slack** — "THE REEF'S KEYS, WINTER, SINCE THE FRACTURE." The reef climbs six keys, E, F♯, G, A, B and C, one to each tide-hour, and drops back to E at the turning. The slack is "the key before the turning" (B), when "the boats hang on their ropes like washing". Status: **canon (book)** for the reefs singing and changing key with the tide and the winter key; **[PROPOSED]** for the six named keys, the hour-per-key and the slack rule. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 57–76, 118, 167, 170; balance table). Bible: already in Vol IV §Errata against Volume II and Vol V §Reef-Readers. The six keys are new.
- **Mooring dues at the slack** — "Six sovereigns a hull, a day, paid into the Harbour-Queen's own hand AT THE SLACK … NOT THROUGH THE OFFICE. NOT BEFORE. NOT AFTER." The receipt is chalk-stamped "DUES PAID, AT THE SLACK", and the chandler won't sell to a hull on the Harbour's "bad list". The doc calls slack-water dues canon, but no bible source was found. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 17 Question 5 calls it an invention). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 76, 141, 188, 301). Bible: new.
- **Article the Ninth (canon wording)** — "Any vessel making the harbour under no hand shall be boarded by the Harbour's people and no other, and what is found aboard her belongs to the Harbour." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 79). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Canon added in Book Two, Part One. Vol IV proposes a different Ninth; see Contradictions.
- **Article the Twelfth** — "The Harbour does not forbid theft; the Harbour forbids theft from the Harbour's own pocket, which is a different crime and carries the drop." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 79). Bible: already in Series Bible §Canon added in Book Two and Vol IV §The Free Captains and the Articles.
- **Article the Sixteenth (salvage)** — "What lies below the tide-mark belongs to whoever brings it up. The Harbour takes a fifth. The sea takes what it likes, and no court on this coast will hear a complaint against it." Status: **canon (book)**; the salvage-crate errand is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 30, 79, 182). Bible: already in Vol IV §The Free Captains and the Articles.
- **Article the Twenty-first** — "No bottle may be opened within the harbour." Phase 17 claims it as its own invention. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 79). Bible: already in Vol IV - The Hush, the Organum, the Rust Coast.md §The Free Captains and the Articles, as [PROPOSED] with the same wording, and §Hooks #4. The game now uses it.
- **The Articles read at noon** — Painted white on black board, "read aloud at noon, in a flat voice, and enforced from the derrick". Teague: "That is all of them that anyone is likely to ask about." Status: **canon (book)** for the flat voice, the derrick and the drop; **[PROPOSED]** for noon. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 23, 80, 122). Bible: already in Vol IV §Errata against Volume II.
- **Vox-bottles: never take the stopper out, never aboard** — "A bottle is a drowned man's breath with a stopper in it, or a thing that learned his name off the water, or nothing at all. Nobody can tell which until the wax is broken. The Coast has a rule for that: never take the stopper out." Also: never take one aboard a ship ("The Coast is very firm about it"). Status: **canon (book)** for the stopper rule and that quiets come aboard through unstoppered bottles; **[PROPOSED]** for "never aboard". Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 265, 285, 305–306, 317–318). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part IV and §Canon added in Book Two, and Vol IV §1.4 The Leak / §1.5 The three kinds of bottle.
- **The Office buys bottles ("reconciled")** — The Office's man buys bottles at six sovereigns, stamps them, and they go "into the box with a small sound, one of many". "A bottle went up the Yard in a grey man's ledger." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 208, 224–235, 158). Bible: new. It is consistent with the Catalogue's bottle trade (Series Bible §Canon added in Book Two).
- **Bells on the Cay's graves (price)** — "A bell costs a copper to me and a sovereign to you." "The Cay has more bare hooks than it has bells." Status: **canon (book)** for the bells on children's graves; **[PROPOSED]** for the price. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 99, 194, 255). Bible: already in Vol V §Customs (Rust Coast).
- **Harbour manners** — Tarred rope coils "left hand over right". "Nobody sleeps ashore at Port Cantabile." Lamps on the Stair are lit "from the bottom up". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 16, 39, 272). Bible: new. The Stair as "a zigzag of small gold points" is canon (Vol IV).
- **The Flambeaux pay for talk** — "The Flambeaux have had the counting-house since the spring … They pay for talk, and the Lantern Yard has plenty." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 319). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Canon added in Book Two, Part One.

##### The Reach after the Greening and the Long Tending (Phase 17)

- **Stone on the tongue, stone on the stone** — A grave "set the Reach way: a stone at its head, flat side up, with a smaller stone on top … the next person along the road will not dig". A grave with no second stone was dug in haste. The Aegis, then Seraphina, Ignatius and Solenne, set stones on bare graves. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§P17 PROLOGUE). Bible: already in Vol V §Reach Customs (expanded) and Series Bible §The Resonant Reach.
- **"Does it ring?" after the Leaf-Fall** — Dorran asks it, Lyra writes NO, and "Nobody's said true enough since the leaves came down." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Vol V §Reach Customs (expanded).
- **The Long Tending (as staged in the Long Night)** — Every Longest Night the Reach's Cantors went down the old stair and sang the Root its lullaby "all through, from the first note to the last, because a god that holds a law for ten thousand years forgets the tune". In the extra, three Cantors have come, the player is the fourth, the vigil runs ten hours to first light, lamps are kept, and keepers who stop humming are "warm" but not singing. Status: **canon (book)** for the custom; **[PROPOSED]** for the extra's vigil, the keepers and the mechanics. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Phase 17 LONGNIGHT). Bible: already in Vol V §The Cantors' Secret (Ilse Brennan's letter) and Vol VI §0.1 The Rota.

##### The Sheep-Walk (Phase 22) and the Downs flocks (Rulings)

- **The fate of the Downs flocks** — The Downs flocks were driven off the hills to be shorn for the Hedge, kept inside it, and eaten over the two shut years. "Nothing took them, neither the Hush nor anyone else." This is why the fold was "kept mended … and then stopped". Any later text must agree with it. Phase 22 gives it to Tamar: "We ate them. Ours too." It goes in the logbook. Status: **canon (delegated)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region › "Decided (delegated): the sheep went into the Hedge's wool…"). Source: Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md; Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`tamar.hedge`, `journal.truth`). Bible: new. The bible has no sheep, and Series Bible §The Resonant Reach has only "packed wool".
- **The Hedge's wool: every sheep in the Reach** — The Hedge was planted with fleece packed between the roots, "raw wool from every sheep in the Reach". "Thorn in the trench, a man's height of packed wool either side … The carders came out in their aprons and worked all night." Status: **canon (book)** (Book Four, Part One; and the Phase 6 game text the Rulings cite). Source: Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md (§The Watch Downs become a region). Bible: Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach has "three men deep of thorn and packed wool". It lacks the sheep and fleece and the carders' night, so the bible is incomplete. See Contradictions for the planting season.
- **The Carders' mill idle** — Fleece "carded and never collected" since its engine stopped. Status: **canon (book)**, from the Phase 9 game text as cited; not re-checked here. Source: Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md. Bible: new.
- **Salt mutton as the Reach's staple** — The Rulings cite Vol I and Phase 6. Phase 22 calls salt mutton "the Reach's staple". Status: **canon (book)** as cited, but the Vol I citation is wrong; see Contradictions. Source: Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md; Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.salt.desc`). Bible: Vol I §III.4 (Bastion Fortis) lists "salt mutton" as Fortis food; Vol I §III.1 (Reach) lists cider, hearth bread, river eel and moot-curd.
- **The fold-fires** — Three beacons: the Long Down (north), Hob's Knap (east) and Ewe Barrow (west). "Not war fires, whatever the Synod told you: shepherds' fires. At the back-end of the year you lit [them], and every flock on the hills came down to the light." They were not lit after the wool went into the Hedge. Once relit, "They will burn every night now." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new; `absalom.intro`, `absalom.done`, `journal.fires*`). Bible: new.
- **Scouring the Chalk Ram every seven years** — "Gran said he has to be scoured every seven years or he walks off the hill and takes the flock with him. It's been ten." Scouring a stake is "an hour on your knees with a spade and a stiff brush". Phase 22 Question 3 asks whether this is the right folklore for the Reach, and it was never answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`kit.intro`, `scour.body`). Bible: new.
- **The ram's-horn button for the scourer** — "Gran said you give the scourer a ram's-horn button for luck." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`kit.done`). Bible: new.
- **Shepherds' marks** — The Farrants' ear-notch, an old raddle mark in Hedge-wool, and Tamar's "mother's mark" on a hank packed in 417. "Shepherds will know the marks." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`sheep.stray`, `items.hank`, `tamar.wool`). Bible: new.
- **Wethercombe's fair and the drovers** — Wethercombe was where the fold and the fair were, and the Bellwether was "the drovers' house: eight hundred sheep a fair-day". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`tamar.combe`, `gideon.topic`). Bible: new.
- **Mutton on the menus** — Salt mutton, broth, chops, pie, hotpot and a roast leg, sold at:
  - the Boar & Dulcimer (Hetty's supper menu);
  - Brenna Loft's;
  - the market provisioner;
  - Vane's stores;
  - Sloe's chandlery;
  - the Bellwether;
  - the Farrants.

  "A flock that is not eaten is a flock nobody keeps." Status: **cut or changed** for Oakhaven. Release Readiness flagged mutton in a starving town. The R2 sweep made Oakhaven sell "only dear salt mutton off Vane's boat", and "Wethercombe and the Coast keep the rest" (ROADMAP.md §R2, Smaller fixes). The menus elsewhere are **[PROPOSED]**; ROADMAP records "sheep and mutton on every menu" as the owner's request. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new, Mutton; `tamar.reward`). Bible: new.
- **Camping and fishing anywhere** — The camp kit pitches a fire and bedroll on any open outdoor map, never in town, and the Woolwight will not come into its light. In the morning: "hoof-prints round the fire in a ring, and none inside it." A hand-line fishes any water within a cast. "Keep quiet." Phase 22b Question 3 asks whether dungeons and the Hedge should refuse camping, and it was never answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§22b What is new; §Camp; §Fishing). Bible: new.

##### Concordance and design canon (GDD, Release Readiness)

- **The Narrow meter** — "Too loud brings the hounds, too still brings the Hush." Noise brings hounds, Hush "unwrites" the party, and hush-lamps are safe points. In the GDD it runs only outside the Hedge. Release Readiness and the R2/R3 sweep later bring the Narrow inside the Hedge as law. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.1, §4.4); Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§4 #2). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part XII — Game-Layer Hooks.
- **Mimics: voices you cannot see** — Answering an off-screen voice triggers an ambush and "teaches the Wearer"; the count of learned voices carries forward. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.2). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part XII (Mimic encounters).
- **Kaelen's tideline in fingers** — Measured in fingers from the shoulder. It starts at 2.5, adds 0.1 per Drink and 0.5 for a large drink, and Heartglass is at 10 fingers. Status: **canon (book)** for fingers-from-the-shoulder; **[PROPOSED]** for the numbers. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.5). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part IV and §Running counts. Series Bible has 2½ on Rootday and 4½ at ch. 24; the 10-finger Heartglass threshold is new.
- **Lyra's Voice and being Torn ("Every mending borrows")** — When her Voice hits 0 she is Torn and sings at half power until rest. Status: **canon (book)** for the saying (Book One chs 1, 3 and 6); **[PROPOSED]** for the mechanic. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.6). Bible: new as a saying. It is in the Book One manuscript only.
- **The Ledger of names** — Every Legionnaire dies saying a name. The prototype has three: Tamsin Reed; Annet Sowerby (WG-41,006); and Davin Hale, "the only person who isn't a core". Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.7). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Antagonists and Key Figures and §Part XII (Names).
- **Echoes (branches shown and denied)** — For example, Vane cutting the mast-line in ch. 8. Status: **canon (book)** for Echo memory; **[PROPOSED]** for the specific Echoes. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.9). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part XII (Echo memory).
- **Concordance and the four endings** — Factions (Church penitents, Null-Watch, Free Captains, Deep Guilds, Catalogue defectors) unlock "the four endings in the bible (Accord, Crown, Void, Silence)". Release Readiness: standing should move only prices, a line from Harrow and from Hetty, and one ending line, with no numbers on screen. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§9); Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§4 #6). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Game layer: Concordance and §Open Decisions.
- **Campfires: who holds the line tonight** — Kaelen keeps the hounds off but never sleeps; Lyra hums the Hush off. Status: **canon (book)** for the hook; **[PROPOSED]** for the companion split. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§9 #4); Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§4 #8). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part XII (Campfires).
- **Hold the Pitch and the pitch minigames** — Hold the note at 528 on the 300th sunrise. Hum the baby into the world at 174 through a clay jar. The falling fifth on Pim's roots runs 174 → 116 → 174. Then the whistle duel with the Wearer, and "SING IT DOWN". Status: **canon (book)** for the scenes; **[PROPOSED]** for the minigame. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.8). Bible: the clay jar and the falling fifth are in Series Bible (canon).md §Part One — The Narrow.
- **The Boar & Dulcimer's rules** — Release Readiness: "Canon's board says NO SINGING. NO TOASTS. NO DICE", and Hetty says "We do not sing in here, not since Davin". The R2 sweep made the evening set a silent-applause Narrow performance and moved the dice to the cellar. Status: **canon (book)** as claimed, but the phrase was **not found** in the bible or the Book One manuscript, so the source is unverified; most likely earlier game text. Source: Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§2 Canon contradictions); ROADMAP.md §R2. Bible: new.
- **The Listening Hour at dusk** — Lamplighters light the hush-lamps, night-hummers take stools to the crossings, and the call is "all's well, all's quiet, keep to the Narrow". Status: **canon (book)** for the Listening Hour (Book One ch. 6 title); **[PROPOSED]** for the call wording. Source: Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§4 #11). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part One — The Narrow (ch. 1, ch. 6). The routine is new.
