- **The nine days' fixed visits** — The Grafters' inspection on Rootday, the Church on Dawnday. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (design). Source: Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md (§4 #10). Bible: Series Bible §Timeline (Book One) has Rootday = the Greenhall and the Grafters.

---

#### From Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

- **The Narrow as law inside the Hedge** — Loud noise in a witness's sight (sprinting, swinging, singing) gets the ward-sign and "Keep to the Narrow". A second breach within two hours is "a breach of the Narrow", fined ten sovereigns through the watch: "Twice now, above the Narrow. Somebody has gone for the watch." The Boar keeps its own rules, and nothing is fined during a chase. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the law and fine; Book One's Narrow is custom, not statute). Source: js/narrow28.js, p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: the Narrow is in Series Bible §Part IV. A legal fine is new.
- **The ward-sign** — "Two fingers to the lips, then to the ear. Mouth shut, ears open." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 442; ch. 2, l. 652). Source: p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: new to the bible.
- **The Listening Hour** — Dusk in Oakhaven: "with lamplight, and the sound of people closing doors softly" (Book One's own sentence). The lamps are dark by day; new games start at dusk. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 408). Source: p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part One — The Narrow.
- **The dead hour** — The deep night, when the Hedge talks ("Mam says the Hedge talks at the dead hour, and you don't listen"), the night-hummers change over, and Pim is lured. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, ch. 6). Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p36-days.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Lyra Ashgrove, Vol VII §5 HOURS.
- **Hush-lamps** — Lamps whose thin reed-whistle "keeps the stillness off you". The game: a lit lamp within about two cells stops the Hush timer; Lyra left one in the Carders' window "so the house would not go still while it stood empty". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, ll. 254, 412; ch. 4, l. 1371, "Hush-lamps whistled from every beam") [the radius is the game's]. Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part IV.
- **Learning songs from people** — Songs are taught in person through Hold the Pitch, from a "Learn a song…" button: Lyra (the Root note, free, once met), Old Tam (The Miller's Daughter, free, once told the truth), Bettany (the tending breath, eighteen sovereigns), Tobin's reed-pipes (the falling fifth, after notebook page two). Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the mechanism). Source: js/narrow28.js. Bible: new (Series Bible §Part XII — Game-Layer Hooks is general).
- **The Concordance (faction standing)** — Standing with the Hedge, the Office, the Synod and the Captains is fed once each by choices already made: Tam told, sat with or left; Pim safe or tended; Sable kept, accused or joined; the bottles freed or kept; the heists. It moves shop prices, a line from Harrow and Hetty, and one line of the ending, with no numbers on screen. A new game already stands at 10–25 with the Hedge. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (game layer). Source: js/narrow28.js. Bible: already in Series Bible §Game layer: Concordance and §Part XII. Name clash: Vol III (b) uses "the Concordance" for the Hall of Carriage's second floor.
- **The two ending lines of the Concordance** — High: "Inside the Hedge they will tell it for years: a stranger who kept to the Narrow when it mattered, and broke it only when it mattered more." Low: "In Oakhaven, afterward, nobody said your name above a murmur, and nobody said it kindly." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Boar & Dulcimer's rules** — The board says NO SINGING. NO TOASTS. NO DICE. NO WEEPING ABOVE A HAND. The evening set is now played the Narrow way, "a hand's width loud", and applauded without a sound: two fingers to the table, one-finger tapping, or hands shaken in the air. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 4, l. 1373) [the silent applause forms are PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv; commit d73de90. Bible: the board is not in any volume (new). Vol II's Boar (peat, cider, a hearth never let out) is older.
- **The sap-mark and the pencil circle** — The sap-mark is a green circle chalked at the height of a man's heart with a line struck through it: "Past cutting." Rooke chalks it and the Grafters read it. A pencilled circle with no line means "Early... could still go back, if kept warm and kept still"; the Grafters read the chalk, not the pencil. Status: **canon (book)** for the sap-mark (MS ch. 3, l. 1067; Series Bible) [the pencil circle is PROPOSED]. Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures ("his green sap-mark means past cutting, and now it also means the Inquisitor's list").
- **The hum of the Hollow** — "Every house at once, low, behind barred doors, the sound of a town keeping very still so as not to be seen growing." "Everybody in the Hollow hums." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (as written; humming to keep still is **canon (book)**, MS ch. 3). Source: p30-hollow.tsv.
- **Keeping your own cup** — A Hollow belief, which Lissa knows is untrue: "if you drink out of a cup that has been in a house, you get what the house has." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Telling the bees** — "In the Reach you tell the bees every birth and every death, close and low, or they go out to the lime and do not come home." For Bettany's three skeps: births to the one by her house; the dead to the Governor's orchard skep in order of the years; the stranger to the chapel skep, your own name last. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 9 and the library's "Telling the Bees"; R4 and the canon pass re-dated it). Source: p29-thin.tsv, p32-canon.tsv. Bible: new (Vol V §Reach Customs (expanded) covers the tending breath, not bees).
- **Grave-salt and bound jaws** — The sexton salts every new grave and binds the jaws of the dead, "so that what is buried stays buried and says nothing". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 308). Source: p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new to the bible.
- **Oakhaven by the clock (R4 street life)** — Status: **[PROPOSED]** (each item below is **[PROPOSED]** unless marked CANON). Source: js/thin29.js, p29-thin.tsv. Bible: new, except as marked.
  - The iron-oaks ring in the wind, "like a spoon touched to a bronze bowl", and hounds love a chime. **canon (book)**: the oaks ring like bronze, MS ch. 4, l. 1532.
  - A sapling comes up through the cobbles on the corner by Cooper's Row every morning, as thick as your wrist, with a hand-saw left beside it. Saw it and it weeps sap and returns. **canon (book)**: MS ch. 1, l. 304, though the book's sapling is a knee-high young oak by the well, "the third this week".
  - The baker's wife's thumping can be heard at night.
  - The tanker-wagon: green glass sloshing behind iron grilles, a Legionnaire with a bell-shaped helm at each end, and "nobody in the street looks at it, very carefully". **[PROPOSED]**; the MS's tankers are the Foundry's.
  - Dogs howl at noon, a quarter-tone wrong, "since the Cascade". CANON: Series Bible §Part IV, "The World Went Flat... Dogs howl at noon".
- **The Church's protection (the rubric)** — Lyra may not speak until the Rite: "She cannot speak; it is in the rubric." She has honey, and the sisters' "You are very fortunate". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 9). Source: p36-days.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible (Book One ch. 9 summary: "Four days of the Church's rubric: no speech, honey and raw egg, oiled throat, and two kind women who tell her she is fortunate").
- **The warrant of Dawnday** — The herald reads it in the square: the Pure Note held over Oakhaven day and night until the Rite; the Hollow given to the Pure Flame when the Rite begins; the Cantor, Lyra Ashgrove, kept in the Church's protection until then. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 7, ll. 2303–2317). Source: p36-days.tsv. Bible: Series Bible ch. 7 summary.
- **Form 9** — The Office's intake form: "Name. Age. Trade." Ninety seconds; further speech not recorded; eight seconds of 741 Hz under the felt. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14 epigraph; ch. 18). Source: p32-canon.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Office does not like to break a pair** — Bettis's core sat in the long-term rack because her husband's was requisitioned "and the Office does not like to break a pair". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 18, l. 5022). Source: p32-canon.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The Veil's three rules** — "Write what happened. Write who it happened to. Do not comfort yourself in the margin." Sable, in the game: "The third is the hardest. I keep it anyway." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, l. 4166) [her last two sentences are PROPOSED]. Source: p31-grove.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Veil designations** — "Umber is a Veil designation, as Sable is." Scribes take an ink or pigment name from the ink-cupboard; the Mausoleum's four tombs are Scribes Verdigris, Lampblack, Vermilion and Sepia, and the library has Ochre, Gall and Bistre. Status: **canon (ruled)** (the pattern: library ruling 8, "The ink and pigment designations stand [PROPOSED]"); the names are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p40-umber.tsv, p27-r2.tsv. Bible: Series Bible §Sable ("'Sable' is only a Veil designation").
- **The House of the Silent Veil (the Mausoleum's identity)** — "The Synod's maps call it a mausoleum. The scribes who lived there called it the House." There the Veil's scribes kept the names of the dead for eleven hundred years; over the door, "What is written is not lost". Its own scribes lie in stone tuned to the note each kept, and are rung from the highest to the lowest. Status: **canon (ruled)** (R2 applied Vol V under the owner's rule "a volume against an in-game book: the volume stands") [the tuned tombs and the four scribes are PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: already in Vol V §The House of the Silent Veil (the "Mausoleum of Chords"), eleven hundred years and the motto.
- **The Folding of the Silent Veil (c. 300 SR)** — A private Office memorandum. The Order is folded into the Synod as the Office of the Catalogue; the House stays open under the Office's seal and its scribes keep their desks. The Registrar-General finds the order's ways of recording a name "have uses beyond the keeping of the dead". Objecting scribes go to field ledgers in the Woods and "are not to be told what the ledgers are for". Status: **canon (ruled)** ("Softened at your call", Library Notes; dated by the Series Bible). Source: p27-r1.tsv, p27-canon.tsv, js/upgrades.js bk_veil. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part V — Timeline ("c. 300 SR | The Silent Veil is absorbed as the Office of the Catalogue").
- **Eight Anchors and their notes** — "Eight Anchors pin the continent to the world": Growth in the Reach (174 Hz), Heat in the Caldera (285), Mass in the Iron Peaks (396), Time in the Shifting Dunes (417), Light at the Dawning Heights (528), Velocity on the Tempest Coast (639), Memory in the Spectral Woods (741), and Decay at the Gate (852), "which keeps the rest between the notes". Under each Anchor is a Chorister. Status: **canon (ruled)** (R2 corrected "On the Eight Anchors" to the Series Bible under the owner's rule that a volume stands over an in-game book). Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part VI — The Eight Realms. The bible says "Tempest Archipelago", not "Tempest Coast".
- **The Hall of Carriage** — It is at the Organum, not Port Cantabile: "the Hall of Carriage keeps a clerk on the third floor. I know his handwriting." The cutter's seized vox-bottles are tagged FOR THE HALL OF CARRIAGE. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 22, l. 5866; Series Bible). Source: p27-r2.tsv, p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible, Vol III, Vol III (b) §Naming and places, Vol VI §3.3.
- **The Office seizes vox-bottles off Rust Coast boats** — Black-market last words are seized "for the Hall of Carriage". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (brainstorm §5 item 4, "a few seized vox-bottles [PROPOSED]"). Source: p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: vox-bottles as Rust Coast black-market goods are in Series Bible §Part IV and §The Free Captains. The Office's seizure of them is new.
- **The Office courier-cutter** — "Four hard little tanks and no envelope, too light to carry anything but paper and proctors", with a brass listening-horn on the bow; OC-742 on the hull. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 21, ll. 5518–5524) [OC-742 is PROPOSED]. Source: p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: listening-horns are in Vol VI §6.3 ("a hold full of cores saying their names at two miles").
- **The Free Captains (lore panel)** — "Air-crews of the Rust Coast, bound by shares and no flag... A captain's word is good until the weather turns; after that, it is a story." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (wording, on a bible-canon base). Source: p23-creation.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §The Free Captains of the Rust Coast.
- **The Iron Synod (lore panel)** — The Choir-Church of the Pure Note (unison as holiness, every second note a sin); its Inquisition the Flambeaux; the Consortium factors; the Office of the Catalogue; the Glass Court of nine dead monarchs with glass hearts. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (wording, on a bible-canon base). Source: p23-creation.tsv. Bible: matches Series Bible §The Iron Synod and §The Glass Court.
- **The Office of the Catalogue (lore panel)** — "Registrar-General Pellam Quist runs the harvest-houses, where people are Unnamed and their voices pressed into glass. The Office's clerks believe every name is a unit. Some of them are right, and some of them are sorry." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (wording, on a bible-canon base). Source: p23-creation.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §The Iron Synod.
- **The callings (Phase 23)** — The six callings' new biographies: the Dissonant Blade on the Dawning floor at the bulkhead wheel when the Light let go, given a medal and the word "consecrated"; the Cantor singing the old fourth line at burials "as a private heresy"; the Corsair "signed on with seven crews"; the Archivist who keeps a second Ledger and pays for each entry with a piece of her name; the Flambeau Penitent with a child's shoe and a jar of ash; the Quarry-Hand who left one man below. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p23-creation.tsv, js/data.js. Bible: new.
- **The retired likeness archetypes (Phase 23)** — The Hooded Return, The Braided Vow, The Old Salt, The Veil-Sworn, The Iron-Collared and The Frost-Marked: invented backstories (a press-gang in Cinderhold, a widow's bees, the ships Fennel, Grey Margit and Tally, a Veil novice at nine, the quarry's fifth gallery, a glass flood on the Flume). Status: **cut or changed** (replaced on 25 Oct by the six Book One characters; "kept as the record" in p23-dossier.tsv and p23-likeness.tsv). Source: p23-dossier.tsv, p23-likeness.tsv; commit 13e6220. Bible: new; nothing to add.

### Objects, songs and lore items

#### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

##### Tobin's journal and blade

- **Tobin's Journal (twelve pages, spring 412 – autumn 417)** — Loose pages in Cantor Tobin Ashgrove's hand, found around Oakhaven and its surroundings. Bound, they earn the title "Keeper of the Pages". "It is one hand … and it never once asks to be pitied." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§TOBIN). Bible: new. The contents are dated under Events.
- **Tobin's tuning-blade** — In 415 the Greenhall voted to give Tobin a long plain blade of bell-bronze ("from which bell, I do not ask"), "ground until it rings at one-seven-four when struck". It is meant to "cut a tuning where a voice cannot reach". He kept it behind the door with the garden hooks. In the end he left it "under the oaks", and Lyra lays her palm on it on the Kestrel. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin pages 6 and 12; epilogue l2_blade, lb_s_blade; dream "Three Deep"). Bible: new. B1 has "a lyre by a stile", not a blade.

##### Reeds, wool, timber and tools (Phase 9)

- **Wind-reed (properties)** — Wind-reed "holds any pitch you give it, and it is dead by this time tomorrow". Dry, it "will burn". Status: **canon (book)** (V1 §6.5: "holds any pitch, dies in a day"). The item name "Wind-Reed" is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§Items). Bible: already in V1 §6.5 and §4.4.
- **Root-Note Reed and Lure Reed** — A reed tuned to 174 "sounded against your breastbone" steadies you "in the Narrow" and slows nearby Hollows. A high lure reed, thrown, "sounds one note a long while". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§Items; §Using the products). Bible: new.
- **Blank First Reed and Joram's Split First Reed** — A blank reed is "cut and slotted, and not yet tuned". Joram's reed is "split down the slot by the frost". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9. Bible: new.
- **Bundle of Carded Wool and the Iron-bough Log** — Wool bound round a blade deadens a ringing strike. An iron-bough log is "heavy as a bell and nearly as willing to ring. Carry it; do not run with it." "A struck bough remembers the hour." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§The clearing; §Items). Bible: already in V1 §6.5 and V2 §Hazard Table (iron-bough rings); the items are new.
- **The Greenroot Register** — Ilse Verrow's slim strapped book of the parish. "Seven houses and thirty-one souls." It ends mid-sentence, "the way you listen to a person who is about to". Given to Lyra, it rests with Tobin's pages; given to Ignatius, it is "filed". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§5, §8). Bible: new.
- **Verrow's Tuning-Reed Case** — Spare reeds "each tuned against a Cantor's own breastbone and long dead". Carried, every Song costs about a quarter less voice. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§1). Bible: new.

##### Echoes, dreams and books

- **Light-glass echoes (glass keeps what it heard)** — Shards, walls, lamps and cores of Light-glass replay what they heard. "Glass keeps the path. Nobody told it to keep the reason." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§Light-glass echoes). Bible: SB §Part IV (glass is a "party line"; cores keep what soldiers hear). The echo mechanic is new.
  - *The count-in* (Sunken Court): a choirmaster, "Lean left, Wat", thirty voices, then "one long note … not in the hymn". **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Nose up* (Deep Woods): "Tank two is going." A woman says "Everyone stays on the line." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Three deep* (Oakhaven): "Pack the wool into the thorn until you cannot hear yourself." "It keeps the Narrow. That is all we ask of it." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Beside the reed* (clearing): "Don't sing at the reed, love. Sing beside it." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Hymn nine* (Cloister): "Hymn nine. Altos only. Whoever has the red cloak, you are late, and you are forgiven." **[PROPOSED]** (Hymn 9 is **canon (book)**).
  - *Where the beam goes* (Catacombs): "Mark every row. A mirror forgets what it was for." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Shift change* (quarry): "Don't hum down here. It hums back." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Roots do not* (Root-vault): "Is it in pain?" "It is a root, sir. Roots don't." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Intake* (Malting): "There is no cost to you," and someone "decides to say their name anyway." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *The low note* (Mausoleum): "From the highest, then. Down … the last is not cast in stone." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *The night of the bore* (quarry wall): "It's singing, foreman." "It's the drill. Seat it. Log it as resonance." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *A held note* (Root-vault wall): a man hums one low note to keep "a door from swinging". "Easy. I'm here." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *The emitter* (Catacombs lamp): "Light never forgives a mirror." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Loud* (Deep Woods gift lamp): a child singing loud is cut off mid-word. **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *The keeper* (Deep Woods shrine lamp): "I keep it for them … I'll leave it burning." **[PROPOSED]**.
  - *Core voices* (quarry, Malting, anywhere): "I mend pumps. The blue jug is on the shelf by the door"; "Assigned. Graded. Reconciled … Please. It is {first}"; "That's mine." **canon (book)** base (SB §Part IV: every Synod soldier dies saying a name); the lines are **[PROPOSED]**.
- **Rest dreams (fourteen)** — Each plays once when its condition holds. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (each). Source: P6 (§P6 DREAMS). Bible: new.
  - *The First Reed*: the reed over the door does not sound; "Does it ring?"
  - *Eight Lamps*: eight humming lamps in a ring, "not closed … a gap, as wide as a doorway". This echoes the Ninth (V1).
  - *The Foundry Floor*: "It is 412." A tall young man at a gauge says "Hold."
  - *Three Deep*: inside the Hedge at its planting, "It is spring". This conflicts on season (see Contradictions).
  - *The Long Rope*: lowered into the shaft. "Sorry. We needed the bread."
  - *The Reading*: a voice reads "Halder Wick" and the list loops to the first page.
  - *Evening in the Court*: the Silencing as "one long note … They only stop being about to do things."
  - *The Long Desk*: a scribe records "The end of a city … I do not hurry it. I do not hold it back."
  - *Clear*: glassing as clearness; "It keeps what it hears."
  - *Registry Square*: "Your name is being reconciled … You take the coffee. It is good. That is the worst of it."
  - *The Wind-Reed Orchard*: a woman with "a cord of knots" walks the reeds. "The ground has an opinion."
  - *Ten Heartbeats*: the hawser cut. Canon base: SB §Vane.
  - *A Good Conversation*: the Hush as a pleasant fire that makes no sound. "It feels like being excused."
  - *The Missing Bell*: eight bells and no frame for a ninth. "The ninth is not stone."
- **Bookshelf volumes (nine)** — Status: **[PROPOSED]** (each), with **canon (book)** bases noted. Source: P6 (§Bookshelf volumes). Bible: noted per item.
  - *Memorandum: Registry Square, Revised Hours* — from the Clerk of Intake to the Under-Registrar (Consignments). The windows have been "reglazed twice". Complaints about the hour "are processed under Acoustic Echo Artifacting". Bible: new as a document.
  - *Notice to Visitors: The Hour* — nine hours for the Edict, thirty miles, reeds that answer. Base: V6 §3.1.
  - *A Torn Leaf from the Conservatory Table* — a Conservatory copy of "the Long Octave", a pitch table that "stops at 852". Below it: "Eight is not the octave. Count again." "The ninth is not stone" has been scraped at. Base: V1 §The Doctrine of the Ninth Anchor. See Contradictions on the name "Long Octave".
  - *Letter Home from Bastion Fortis* (Brannoch): see People.
  - *Harness Order for the Compact of Stone*: see Institutions.
  - *The Compact of Stone: A Plain Account*: see Institutions.
  - *Line-talk, Null-Watch, Third Dog Watch*: see People and Contradictions.
  - *Basket Log, the Rift Winch-house*: see Institutions.
  - *Order of Service for Those Lowered*: see Institutions.
- **Lore Codex stubs (Organum, Bastion Fortis, Null-Watch and the Rift)** — Hearsay summaries: "a seat, not a realm"; the Keynote "where the eight roots meet"; "Talking is how you keep each other." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (§Lore Codex stubs). Bible: already in SB §The Organum, V1 §III.4 and §III.8, and V6 §1.
- **World map margin notes** — "Do not be taken in by the stall." "Longest watch-rota in history. Nobody remembers who set it." "Keep talking." A dawn-gun tally runs "off the edge of the page". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§World map margin notes). Bible: new.

##### Songs

- **The Miller's Daughter (two verses)** — Tobin sang both verses at Essa's burial. Tam gave the second verse "to something", is trying a third, and the water now sings the second verse. It is Lyra's "rude one … It is not a hymn. It is true." Status: **canon (book)** (the song: B1 ch6, "Oh, the miller's daughter, she was fair …"). The two-verse and third-verse details are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 3; Tam letters); P9 (Lyra; §[PROPOSED] items). Bible: already in SB §Part One ch6.
- **The Sheaf Song** — A sixteen-beat F-major pentatonic tune: "Cut it low, and cut it slow, the last of the year is let to go." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the lyric and tune; the song's existence is **canon (book)**, V7b §2). Source: P12 (§Invented facts; Boar line). Bible: already in V7b §2 (named, no words). Note: B1's harvest song "The Last Sheaf" ("Oh, the last sheaf's in and the barley's high …") is a different song.
- **The Boar's Reel** — The tavern tune for "playing for the room". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§TAVERN). Bible: new.
- **The carders' work-song** — Nell sang it all night at the Hedge-planting. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 11). Bible: new.
- **The skipping-rhyme** — Wren and Lyra hum it. Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part IV: "too loud brings the hounds, too still brings the Hush"). Source: P6 (epilogues). Bible: already in SB §Part IV.
- **The Root's five-note lullaby** — "Five notes, over and over … each time it reaches the fifth it loses the tune." Status: **canon (book)** (SB ch9; V5 §The Cantors' Secret). Source: P6 (dream "The Long Rope"; echoes; Tobin page 9); P9 (Kaelen). Bible: already in SB §Part Two and V5.
- **The falling fifth and the tending note** — Both are used throughout (Lyra against the bore; the tending note over a wound). Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6; P9. Bible: already in SB §Part One / Two and V5 §The Felling-Song.

##### Foods and festival items (Phase 12)

- **Hearth Bread** — "A round loaf from the new flour … It tastes of nearly what bread should." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the item; hearth bread is **canon (book)**, V1 §III.1 and V7b). Source: P12 (§Items). Bible: already in V1 §III.1 and V7b §2.
- **Last-Apple Cider** — "Cloudy, sharp, in a stoppered clay jug." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: already in V1 §III.1 and V7b (cider).
- **Moot-Curd** — "Soft salted curd in a dock-leaf; what is eaten at the moot." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: already in V1 §III.1 and V7b §2. **Contradicted** by V1 (see Contradictions).
- **Run-Eel** — "A green Flume eel taken alive and quiet in the Eel-Run … the Boar pays well for run-eels." Flume eels are "green as a bruise … It is only the water." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12; P11 (fisher-wife). Bible: already in V1 §III.1 (river eel) and V7b.
- **Plaited Sheaf-Figure** — "Plaited from the last stand of the north field, cut in the dead hour … It belongs over the Hedge-gate until spring." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the item; the figure is **canon (book)**, V7b §2). Source: P12. Bible: already in V7b §2.

##### Weapons, kit and instruments (Phase 12)

- **Quarry Great-Maul** — A lead-weighted quarry sledge on an ash haft. It "cracks bell-plate and golems". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (ARMS). Bible: new.
- **Long Glass-Edged Blade** — "A corsair's two-handed blade, the edge ground out of salvaged Aether-Glass. It rings through the Cacophony." Sold by Vane. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: new. SB says the Cacophony avoids Aether-Glass.
- **Bell-Bronze Glaive** — "Struck from a chapel bell the Synod did not take." Sold by Ignatius. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: new. It conflicts with B1: "every bell in the Reach" was requisitioned.
- **Reed-Guard Dirk** — A basket-hilted off-hand dirk. "Rust Coast duellists call it the second word." Sold by Hob. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: new.
- **Oil Flask, Damper Jar and Weighted Net** — The damper jar is "wool-fat, wax and powdered lead-salt … Apothecaries use it on sickrooms … It is not the Hush." The net is "a corsair's boarding net" of reed-cord and fired clay. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (KIT). Bible: new.
- **Office Grapnel OC-742** — "A cutter-man's grapnel gun, Office issue … OC-742 stencilled on the stock." It is taken off the Office cutter in the Deep Woods. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (RIGGING). Bible: new. SB ch21 has Vane taking "a grapnel" from the cutter.
- **Instruments: Lute, Alder Flute, Hurdy-Gurdy** — Hob Wendle's lute is strung with his own gut. Old Tam's alder flute is packed in hush-cloth. Vane's "wheel-fiddle with a drone string that never stops" calls hounds. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (SONGCRAFT). Bible: new.
- **Iron Tuning Fork and Resonance Rod** — The fork, from "a Cantor's widow" via Bettany, hums near hidden things: resonant stone, strongboxes, Light-glass, loose pages. The bell-bronze rod at 174 "dips toward what is rooted below. Do not stand on what it finds." Over a root-vein it finds "a small cousin of something much larger". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (SONGCRAFT; journal lines). Bible: new.

##### Tarrows' house items (Phase 13)

- **Nan Tarrow's reed** — "A reed, long and old … a tag on the cord … NAN TARROW … It is the only thing in the house that is sounding." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13. Bible: new.
- **Linnet's Counting-Beads** — "Eight beads on a cord, the eighth worn smooth." Counted slowly, they steady you against the Narrow. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13. Bible: new.
- **Gil's tuning fork, bench and tagged reeds; his stopped dulcimer** — Cut reeds in bundles, "each tagged with a name. Hung, not sounded. A trade in people's breath." His dulcimer strings are "stopped with strips of cloth, the way you do when a house is to be left". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13. Bible: new.

##### Gifts and small lore items (Phase 6 letters)

- **Sweet reed, reed-pith and the hush-whistle** — Sweet reed from the Flume bank is "for your voice. Do not eat it near the water." Reed-pith is "for a voice. Do not sing it." The hush-whistle comes "out of Essa's mother's stock. Walk soft in it." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Pim and Tam letters). Bible: new.
- **Vox-bottles in felt sleeves (the cutter's 432)** — "Nobody tells you a voice in a bottle keeps its tone. Some of them were singing." Status: **canon (book)** (432 is in SB ch21 and V3b). The singing is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (Vane "The cutter's hold"). Bible: already in SB §Part Four and V3b.
- **Kaelen's chalk list on the beam** — See Kaelen. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P9. Bible: already in SB ch13 and ch24.

##### Sky and glass (Phase 14)

- **The Octave lights (aurora)** — Slow coloured curtains in the night sky on certain nights, reaching straight overhead. Status: **canon (ruled)** (the look: R §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 21 item 3). Source: P14 (§SKIES); R. Bible: new.
- **The Octave lights' meaning and colours** — "The province's Signatures (the eight Anchor notes) made visible when the Relay is strained." One hue per note: Growth 174 green, Heat 285 ember, Mass 396 slate, Time 417 amber, Light 528 cyan, Velocity 639 blue, Memory 741 violet, Decay 852 grey-mauve. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (not ruled; the Phase 21 ruling covered only the look). Source: P14. Bible: new. The Relay is canon (V1 §2.3).
- **Light-glass growth and colour** — Light-glass spreads from the bore in three steps (the Rite, the coil seated, the bit snapping). It is cyan, "faintly warm", with "a glassy chime built on 528 Hz". In Whisper-Grove it grows around the Sunken Court's shard. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P14 (§GREENING visual inventions). Bible: already in SB (Light-glass is cyan, Kaelen's arm); the spreading is new.
- **Root veins** — Green-gold for the Growth root, lilac-white for the Memory root under the Malting's Tuning Room. They pulse at 1.2 s, "visual 174 Hz". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P14. Bible: new.
- **The Greening stage ladder** — Moss and flowers in the paving once Kaelen is met, then vines on shuttered houses, vines to the eaves, roots breaking the square, then the full overgrown town. The Vol V winter after-images are deliberately not shown. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P14 (§GREENING). Bible: V5 §Status has the aftermath; the stages are new.
- **The Drift-Kestrel in flight** — Two red pennants, lit stern windows, a brass lift-tank. "Under the lift-tanks the whole ship hums one note, and holds it." Status: **canon (ruled)** (the look: R §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 item 5). The scene text is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P14 (§Kestrel flight scene); R. Bible: already in SB §Vane and V1 §PART VIII (the Kestrel); the look is new.

##### Titles

- **Titles earned** — "Keeper of the Pages" (Tobin's journal), "Load-Bearing" (Kaelen), "Beside the Reed" (Lyra), "Entered in the Ledger" (Sable). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§Completion); P9 (§Small permanent things). Bible: new.

---

#### From Phases 15–25 (B)

##### Phase 15 objects

- **Smoked Run-Eel** — "A Run-Eel split, salted and turned over a fire until the skin crackles … It tastes of river and woodsmoke." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dishes and warmths). Bible: the Eel-Run is already in Vol VII (b) §The Eel-Run, and river eel in Vol I §III.1 (Food). The dish is new.
- **Toasted Round** — "Hearth bread toasted over the coals with moot-curd melted into the crumb … You eat it standing, the way the moot does." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dishes and warmths). Bible: hearth bread and moot-curd are already in Vol I §III.1 (Food); the dish is new.
- **Soothing Draught** — "Honey and willow-bark steeped hot in a tin cup." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Dishes and warmths). Bible: new.
- **Letter of Credit, 25 / 100 / 500** — "A Consortium letter of credit … payable to the bearer. It weighs nothing. Any agent will cash it for a fee, and so will whoever finds it." Icons have one, two or three seals: 25 blue, 100 green, 500 red with a gilt edge. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the items); **canon (ruled)** (the icon look, Rulings Phase 20 #7). Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Letters: the desk and the items); Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 7). Bible: new.
- **The Borrowed Collar (trophy)** — "A collar of knotted cord, cut from the thing that wore the villagers' voices at the Hedge. It is only cord now." It is granted for the Hedge Wearer fight (`wearer_dead` without `wearer_coda_dead`). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies); js/hearth15.js. Bible: new. See Contradictions.
- **The Split Funeral-Reed (trophy)** — "A mourning-reed split down its length, from the choir of the Mausoleum. It sounds nothing now, and it is the right nothing." When hung, "you think you hear someone count to eight". It is granted for the coda Wearer. Its icon is "nearly black" (Rulings Phase 20 #7). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: new.
- **The Requiem's Chord-Plate (trophy)** — "A bronze plate engraved with a chord that has one voice too many, taken from the Requiem in the Mausoleum of Chords." Its icon shows staff lines with one extra note. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: new. The Mausoleum is canon.
- **The Aegis's Cheek-Plate (trophy)** — "A brass plate from the Aegis of the Cloister, still scratched with a chalk hymn-number." "It has earned crooked." It is granted if the Aegis is destroyed. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: new.
- **The Hymn-Nine Slate (trophy)** — "A slate board from the Cloister, chalked HYMN 9. The Aegis chimed its alto line when you touched it." It is granted if the Aegis is calmed. Its icon is a chalk "9". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: HYMN 9 on the board is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part Three — The Forgetting (ch. 17), and Hymn 9 in Vol V §The Choir-That-Eats; the trophy is new.
- **The Weaver's Shuttle (trophy)** — "A bobbin-shuttle of pale wood from the loom under Whisper-Grove. A little shroud-thread is still wound on it, in no colour." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: new. The Phantom Weaver is canon (Series Bible §The Spectral Woods).
- **The Pressman's Plate (trophy)** — "A brass numbering-plate off Pressman Mallow's press-frame. The number is worn smooth." Its icon shows three worn digit cells. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: new. Pressman Mallow is a game boss.
- **The Weaverling Spindle (trophy)** — "A spindle of lift-silk wound by the Brood-Mother's get, light as breath. Whatever the silk was carrying has been let go." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: new.
- **Teller's Lanyard (trophy)** — "A leather lanyard with a brass tag: TELLER, HOLD, 432. The hold is open now." It is granted for Bosun Teller. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. 432 is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: the cutter's hold of "four hundred and thirty-two voices" is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part Four — Coda (ch. 21); Teller is new.
- **The Horn-Lens (trophy)** — "A thick glass lens from the Office cutter's listening-horn. Hold it up and the room looks back a little late." It stands in for the brief's "Sentinel's lens". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§What is [PROPOSED]; §The twelve trophies). Bible: the listening horn is already in Series Bible §Part Four — Coda and Vol VI §6.3 Office listening-horns.
- **The Felted Clapper (trophy)** — "The clapper of the Unrung Bell, wrapped in grey felt so that it would never be heard." It is granted for the Deacon of the Quiet Faith. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies); js/hearth15.js (`deacon`). Bible: the Unrung Bell (Abbess Wenna's order) is already in Series Bible (canon).md §The Tongueless; the clapper is new.
- **The Captain's Gorget (trophy)** — "A dented steel gorget stamped with Outrider-Captain Brennick's rank. Whoever wore it held his line." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The twelve trophies). Bible: new.

##### Phase 16 objects

- **The Lock-Wheel Key** — "A heavy brass key, still warm. It fits the padlock on the lower lock's chain at Stillwater, and nothing else." It sold for 25 sovereigns; "The key went to a factor from the quarry in the spring." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Item; `pellam.keyText`). Bible: new.
- **The iron-bough raft for Fortis** — Forty iron-bough logs cut and chalked for Fortis, every lashing oiled so it will not creak. "The logs did not ring once." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the raft). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (`landing.raft`, `orrin.after`). Bible: iron-bough timber is already in Vol I §III.1 and §6.5, and timber for Fortis in Vol II §Settlements (Stillwater).
- **The Relief Register** — "A relief register, swollen with water and still legible: the households of the Loop Quarters, in pencil, in a hand that is not the Office's. Not numbers. Names." "It is heavier than paper should be." Taking it adds nine names to the Ledger. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The register). Bible: new.
- **The Lantern Row ledger page and the station daybook** — The ledger reads "Carry the two. Balance forward." It echoes the earlier game loops "the bread's for Thursday", "mind the water" and "come in now". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Notices and documents). Bible: new.
- **The Held Cabinet** — "Oak, iron-cornered, with a seal across the doors." It holds "letters nobody was allowed to read: some for the Cantor, some from her". After a theft the Chapel refills it with blank sheets and a second seal. Rumour: "six slips of red tape on the floor". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The vestry and undercroft; §Rumours). Bible: new.
- **The six held letters (and their wax)** — Letters for Lyra (from Wennet), Kaelen (from Mam), Hetty (from Cob), Orla Hale (from Ness), Pim (from Da) and Dessa Quill (from Imelda). Their wax and ties are **confirmed by the owner** ("they match"):
  - Lyra: green.
  - Kaelen: blue-grey with black string.
  - Hetty: red.
  - Orla: brown with twine.
  - Pim: grey with a wool tie.
  - Dessa: violet with a blue ribbon.

  Status: **[PROPOSED]** for the letters' contents (two contradict canon; see Contradictions); **canon (ruled)** for the colours (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 #7). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The six held letters); Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 7). Bible: new.
- **The Depot Three roster ledger** — "A tall ledger bound in grey cloth … eleven columns of names against which the Consortium has entered no number. The last column is headed RECONCILED. It is blank." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Factor's safe…). Bible: new.
- **The quarry lease (copy)** — As written in Phase 16: "the Reach grants the Consortium of Bores the Root for twenty years", signed "Alder Mossbrook, for the Reeve-Moot, 404". Status: **cut or changed**. The R2 sweep made it Aldric's signature: the lease now reads "the Consortium of Bores takes the quarry, the shaft, and such resonances as lie beneath, for bread at a fixed price, for nine years … Aldric Vance, Governor, 414. Under it, witnessed: Alder Mossbrook, for the Reeve-Moot." The ROADMAP records that "Mossbrook witnessed it, so his reasons to burn it stand" (ROADMAP.md §R2; js/heist16.js:309, 401). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Factor's safe…). Bible: Vol I §III.1 and Vol II §People You Can Use Today still have the Moot or Mossbrook selling the quarry rights in 404, so they are now contradicted; see Contradictions.
- **The Kestrel's Light-glass lanterns and sextant** — Lanterns with "one shard apiece of what the Grove leaves lying about" that "hum at five twenty-eight, very faintly, and [are] warm". "A brass sextant with a Light-glass sight. It points, unhelpfully, at the Grove." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Props; §Upgrades). Bible: Light-glass at 528 is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Kaelen and Vol I §III.2; these objects are new.
- **The envelope patch, weaverling silk and lift-silk** — "Office mesh and silk in long laps." The number-two tank is "closed with weaverling silk". The Weaverling Spindle is "lift-silk". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Props; §Upgrades); Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (Weaverling Spindle). Bible: new.
- **Nell's flour from before** — "Reach flour tastes of less every harvest. This is from before." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. It echoes canon grain that "tastes of less each time" (Vol I §III.1). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§Nell). Bible: Vol I §III.1 (Economy).

##### Phase 17 objects

- **Lyra's slate and the flat stone** — "A slate on a cord round your neck, and a stub of chalk … You write in square capitals." A flat stone the size of a palm is set on a bare grave. Status: **canon (book)** for the slate; **[PROPOSED]** for the items. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§P17 PROLOGUE). Bible: the slate is already in Series Bible §Part Two — The Root (ch. 13) and Vol V §4.
- **The Harbour's Receipt** — "A slip of tarred paper with a chalk stamp, and the Harbour-Queen's hand across it. DUES PAID, AT THE SLACK." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 300–301). Bible: new.
- **The gauge-valve** — "A brass gauge-valve for a lift-tank … Fortis brass, the chandler says. Mostly." "Your engineer will say it is the wrong thread. It is the right thread." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 190–193, 302–303). Bible: new.
- **Marrin Tace's bottle** — "A blue glass bottle, wax-sealed, J. TACE and a four in chalk. It is not to be opened, not to be sold, and not to be taken aboard a ship." Its icon is blue glass with a chalk "4" (Rulings Phase 20 #7). Oona lets it down into the water on a cord, unopened, and rings once. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the item); **canon (ruled)** (the icon). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 91, 153, 304–306). Bible: new. The bottle-dealer's blue bottles are canon (Series Bible ch. 3).
- **Jory's Pilot's Whistle** — "A tin whistle on a bit of cord, worn bright at the mouth. It plays the six keys of the reef, and only those." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 216, 221–223). Bible: new.
- **A Small Bell (for the Cay)** — "A small bronze bell on an iron hook, no bigger than a cup." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the item); the custom is **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 307–308). Bible: the custom is already in Vol V §Customs (Rust Coast).
- **The chandler's diving-helm** — "A diving-helm of beaten brass, dented … New in his grandfather's time. Fortis brass, he says. It is not." Status: **canon (book)** (Book Two ch. 3, "new in his grandfather's time"). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 44, 84, 86). Bible: new. It is in the Book Two manuscript only.
- **Bronze rigging-chimes** — "At her shroud-head hangs a bar of small bronze tubes, and the wind plays it." Status: **canon (book)** ("carillon rigging at every shroud-head"). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 15). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Canon added in Book Two, Part One.
- **The Catechism of the Pure Note** — "Q. What is holiness? A. One note, held without end. Q. What is sin? A. The second note." Phase 24 voices it over black, with Ignatius asking and the congregation answering, a low F and a bell under it. Status: **canon (book)** (Book One, Prologue epigraph). Source: Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md (shot 0). Bible: the Catechism is referenced in Series Bible (canon).md §What everyone believes; the text itself is in the manuscript.
- **The lullaby of five notes** — "Five notes, a turn, the five again lower, a fall." In the Long Night it is held for about 12.7 seconds per verse at the Keeper's Stone. Status: **canon (book)** for the contour; **[PROPOSED]** for the timing (Phase 17 Question 3). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§LONGNIGHT header; §Mechanics notes). Bible: already in Vol V §The Cantors' Secret.
- **Ilse's line pinned to the ring-post** — "Sing it all through. From the first note to the last. It forgets. Nobody has been down in three hundred years. If you go, come and tell me what it said." Status: **canon (book)** for the first sentences (Ilse Brennan's letter); **[PROPOSED]** for the rest and the pinned copy. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Orchard props). Bible: already in Vol V §The Cantors' Secret.

##### Phase 20 and Phase 21 looks (all ruled)

- **The Chapel belfry** — Two round-headed openings on each face, stone arches, a moulded cornice, and inside an oak frame, pegged and braced, on a stone plinth. **No bell is ever drawn.** The oak frame for four bells and four hooks is earlier game canon. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 20 #4). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 4). Bible: new. Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach has the bells taken for bore-teeth.
- **The glass-fire lamp in the tower's south opening** — Once `rite_ready` is set, the lamp "where the tenor bell was" is drawn in the opening over the Chapel door. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 20 #3). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 3). Bible: new.
- **The bell-frame chalk ("TAKEN FOR BORE-TEETH, … THEY RANG IN E.")** — Phase 20 quotes it as "412 SR", unchanged. Status: **cut or changed**. The owner's later ruling moved the bells to 417, "the first year of the quarry", and the code now reads "TAKEN FOR BORE-TEETH, 417 SR. THEY RANG IN E." with a newer chalk line under it, "the Hedge can whistle. who taught it." (ROADMAP.md §R2, The owner's canon rulings; js/game.js:1519). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Unchanged…). Bible: new; Series Bible gives no date.
- **The Synod Kite Shield's "dead queen"** — "A crowned ivory head in profile on an oxblood field, in a bevelled brass rim", used on the icon, the card, the co-op figure and in the hand. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 21 #4; agrees with Phase 20 #7). Source: Phase 21 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 4). Bible: the dead Queen Maelis of the Reach and the Glass Court are already in Series Bible (canon).md §The Resonant Reach and §The Iron Synod. The shield art is new, and whose head it is goes unsaid.
- **Gear looks** — The ruled looks:
  - Corsair Buckler: iron rim and boss, red painted band.
  - Carillon Bell-Helm: a bell dome with a verdigris lip; the sight-slit is painted on, and no face shows from any angle.
  - Consortium Brigandine: brass rivets.
  - Vesper's Boarding Axe: red-wrapped haft.
  - Throwing Knives: held as a fan of three.

  Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 21 #5). Source: Phase 21 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 5). Bible: new.
- **Item icon looks** — The ruled icons:
  - Hymn-Nine Slate: a chalk "9".
  - Requiem's Chord-Plate: staff with one extra note.
  - Pressman's Plate: three worn digit cells.
  - Vox-Bottle: "a lilac wisp in violet glass".
  - Marrin Tace's Bottle: blue glass, chalk "4".
  - Cutter's Log: a small stamped label.
  - Mallow's Round-Book: Office blue with gilt bands and a broken chain.
  - Root-Note Reed: bound in green.
  - Lure Reed: two red threads.
  - First Reed: a hanging loop.
  - Split Funeral-Reed: nearly black.

  Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 20 #7). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 7). Bible: new.
- **The glass arm on the character card** — The Dissonant Blade's left arm is Aether-Glass in first person. On the card it climbs with glassing: hand first, the forearm from 15%, and the whole arm from 40%. These are "glassing thresholds … confirmed as canon". Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 21 #6). Source: Phase 21 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 6). Bible: glassing is already in Vol I §1.7 Vitrification; the thresholds are new. Kaelen's left arm is canon (Series Bible §Kaelen).
- **Character card looks** — Bare-armed travellers wear a shirt in the calling's coat colour, sleeves rolled. Default headwear follows the portraits: faces 1 and 4 hooded, face 3 in a captain's cap. The backdrop is a dark niche tinted by the calling's cloak, with a worn stone plinth. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 21 #7, #8, #9). Source: Phase 21 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 items 7–9). Bible: new.
- **The Chord (night-sky star-dust band)** — "A faint band of star-dust across the night sky" (`js/light21.js`). The owner kept it and the name: "it is the Chord … If any player-facing text ever names it, use 'the Chord'." Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings §Confirmed as proposed, Phase 21 #1). Source: Phase 21 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 1). Bible: "the Chord" is already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part VII — Factions › The Chord, as the protagonists' movement, used throughout. This is a name clash; see Contradictions.
- **Sun and moon on real arcs; the Octave lights overhead** — The sun rises east, stands about 66° at noon and sets west. The moon rises at dusk, is highest at midnight and shows the calendar's phase. The Octave lights (an aurora) reach straight overhead on the nights the game picks. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 21 #2, #3). Source: Phase 21 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 items 2–3). Bible: new. Vol VII has no moon or aurora.
- **Close-up ground detail** — Cobbles with mortar and moss, plank grain and nails, grass blades, pebbles, flagstone chips. Status: **canon (ruled)** (Rulings Phase 21 #10). Source: Phase 21 - New In-Game Text.md (§1 item 10). Bible: new.

##### Phase 20b and Phase 25 looks

- **Brick nogging in Oakhaven** — One house front in three fills its timber frame with brick. Phase 20b Question 1 asks whether that is right or timber-and-plaster only, and it was never answered (ROADMAP still lists it under "Owner's decisions"). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 20b - New In-Game Text.md (§[PROPOSED] canon details; Questions 1). Bible: new. Phase 20b says the bibles describe "timber and plaster under thatch and slate".
- **Painted ground, walls and trees** — Clover in damp hollows, dry straw on open flats, and trodden earth by roads, doors and walls. Three more house fronts: close studding, brick nogging, and a braced frame with cracked plaster. Two stone courses: dressed ashlar, and rubble with moss in the joints. The oak comes in rust, green-gold and dark copper, and the willow in lilac and darker blue. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (recommended "keep", never ruled). Source: Phase 20b - New In-Game Text.md (§What is new; §[PROPOSED] canon details). Bible: new.
- **Painting source for stand-ins** — The owner answered Phase 20b Question 2: start painting the rest with Canva. The owner chose Blender for the wall marks. Status: **canon (ruled)** (owner's answer, recorded in Phase 20b - New In-Game Text.md §Going on with the stand-ins). Source: Phase 20b - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: n/a (art process).
- **Pixel-3D figure depth and backs** — People are extruded to 0.6 of their width, and a figure's back is its darkened mirror. Phase 25 Questions 1–3 ask about depth, true backs and a rig for the Hollow, and none was answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 25 - The pixel-3D set.md (§Questions for sleeves). Bible: n/a (art).

##### Phase 22 objects

- **Station Six Tally-book** — "A Consortium tally-book from the pylon's iron box. Column after column of one number, a pitch, logged every hour; then, in 417, one line: LINE DEAD BOTH ENDS. HEDGE." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.tally`). Bible: new.
- **Absalom's Tinder-box** — "A beacon-keeper's brass tinder-box, the lid worn smooth by a thumb." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.tinder`). Bible: new.
- **Furze faggot** — "A bound bundle of dry gorse for a beacon." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.furze`). Bible: new.
- **Ram's-horn Button** — "A button carved from a ram's horn, from the scouring of the Ram. For luck." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.button`). Bible: new.
- **Farrant Fleece Mantle** — "Felted Farrant wool, the colour of the chalk. Hushes your footsteps like the Hedge-cloth, and is warmer." It was Tamar's mother's. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.mantle`, `tamar.reward`). Bible: new. "Hedge-cloth" is also new to the bible.
- **Mutton dishes** — Salt Mutton ("Salt, and more salt, and somewhere under it mutton"), Mutton Broth (with barley), Mutton Chops, Mutton Pie (a hot-water crust), Mutton and Barley Hotpot, Roast Leg of Mutton ("For a minute the Reach is a kind place"), and Camp-Roast Chops. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (§Items; §22b Items). Bible: new.
- **Wethercombe Apple** — "A red apple off the Bellwether's orchard." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.apple`). Bible: new.
- **Farrant's Crook** — "A shepherd's crook of ash with a horn head, three generations of Farrants worn into the grip … the hook staggers what it catches." It "has pulled ewes out of drifts and hounds off lambs." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`weapons.crook`, `tamar.home`). Bible: new.
- **Shearing Blades** — "Spring-steel sheep-shears, the bow filed sharp, from the barrow's antechamber." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`weapons.shears`). Bible: new.
- **Barrow-Bronze Leaf Blade** — "A leaf-shaped bronze sword from a grave in Ewe Barrow. It rings when it strikes, like a small bell: the Cacophony does not like it." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`weapons.leaf`). Bible: new.
- **Barrow-Warden's Helm** — "A bronze cap with cheek-guards, green with age … Whoever wore it guarded the door from the inside." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`armor.helm`). Bible: new.
- **Hedge-Wool Shroud** — "A cloak of Hedge-wool torn from the Old Fleece. It drinks every sound you make. It smells of the ground." Phase 22b Question 2 asks whether wearing it is too grim, and it was never answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`armor.shroud`; Questions 2). Bible: new.
- **Drover's Boots** — "Long greased boots for a drove-road: quiet in wet grass and thorn-proof." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`armor.boots`). Bible: new.
- **Hedge-Wool and the Marked Hank** — "A hank of grey wool from under the Hedge's roots, earth and thorn in it." The marked hank from the nest carries "an old raddle mark", and Tamar knows her mother's mark on it. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.hedgewool`, `items.hank`). Bible: new.
- **Woolwight's Bell** — "A sheep-bell on a rotten strap, with the clapper wrapped in wool so it never rings." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.bell`). Bible: new.
- **Barrow-Gold Torc** — "A twisted neck-ring of pale gold, from a grave older than the Reach." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.torc`). Bible: new.
- **String of Amber Beads** — "Amber beads on a gut string. There is a fly in one of them, older than anything." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.amber`). Bible: new.
- **Bronze Lunula** — "A crescent collar of beaten bronze, green at the edges." The summary calls it "a gold lunula"; see Contradictions. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.lunula`; §22b What is new). Bible: new.
- **Kit Farrant's Cap** — "A boy's woollen cap, wet through, from the Woolwight's nest." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.cap`, `cap`). Bible: new.
- **Drover's Camp Kit and Fishing Line** — "A bedroll, a tinder-box and a bundle of furze." "A hand-line on a cork, hooks in the cork." Both were Tamar's father's. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.campkit`, `items.line`, `tamar.woolThanks`). Bible: new.
