#### From Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

- **The Miller's Daughter (as a Song)** — Old Tam's song, both verses, "Essa's verse and mine", up and up for a high note nobody reaches. Sung loud and badly far above the Narrow, it pulls every hound in hearing to where it was sung, "the opposite of hiding". Status: **canon (book)** (the song and Tam singing it into the water-meadow: MS ch. 1, l. 426; ch. 6, ll. 2177–2190) [as a learnable lure Song, PROPOSED]. Source: js/narrow28.js, p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part One — The Narrow. Note: narrow28.js's header cites "ch. 18" for the water-meadow; it is ch. 6.
- **The Root note** — "Hold the long cane's note, low and steady, until it stops being a noise and becomes a place." Lyra: "Father set me the long cane first... the one it keeps losing." Status: **canon (book)** (the Root note: MS ch. 1, l. 364; Series Bible §Kaelen Vance, "He hums the Root note Lyra taught him") [the long cane is PROPOSED]. Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p23-cast.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible.
- **The tending breath / tending note** — "Low and even, through the clay jar, so the mother is mended and the hounds hear nothing." Bettany: "mostly breath, love; the voice only rides on it." Hummed through a Hollow door, it is taken up on your note. Status: **canon (book)** (the tending note and the clay jar: MS ch. 1, l. 248; ch. 13, l. 3956). Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Lyra Ashgrove, Vol V §Reach Customs ("tending breath").
- **The falling fifth** — "The Root, then down a fifth: the long cane first, then the short one." It is learned at Tobin's pipes: "the fifth falls out of it like a dropped stone. Lyra had it by dark, the notebook says." It stops a hound leader and the hush-mites; it is Pim's whistle ("two notes, down"); and Lyra whistles it through two fingers before her voice returns (the Whistled Fifth). Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 364: "Try the falling fifth"; ch. 6; ch. 20, l. 5444). Source: p28-narrow.tsv, p27-r2.tsv, p36-days.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part One — The Narrow.
- **Lyra's Whistled Fifth** — Before `lyra_voice`, Lyra's party ability: she whistles the falling fifth through two fingers, aimed at the one you look at, which stops it dead and makes the rest falter. Her tending breath is a smaller heal, "no voice in it, only breath". Status: **canon (book)** (MS: "Lyra cannot sing" in the Deep Woods) [the ability is PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv, p28-narrow.tsv, js/party.js. Bible: new as a mechanic.
- **Tobin's reed-pipes** — "Seven reed-pipes on their pegs above the chest, the last reeds in the Reach that have not turned. Someone oils them every month, because he always did." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 500, "his wind-reed pipes, a set of seven"; l. 534, "Did you remember to oil the reed-pipes?"). Source: p27-r2.tsv, p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: new to the bible.
- **Tobin's notebook, page two** — Teaches the falling fifth on the long cane and the short. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 6's notebook pages, used by R3). Source: js/narrow28.js. Bible: new.
- **Tobin's Tuning-Blade** — "A long blade of bell-bronze ground to ring at 174 Hz" in an oilskin bundle with a slate: "If Lyra finds this: I am going up to the Pellows'. Their girl is cooling. If I have to sing, I will go out into the yard, so the house is further away. Keep your voice in your chest, lark." It was ground "from the cracked treble that has stood in the chapel porch since my grandfather's day". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Release Readiness: "Canon has his lyre... and he carried no blade"; the game now has him leave it before going up). Source: p27-canon.tsv, p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Tobin's journal (twelve pages, spring 412 to spring 417)** — It ends at the Pellows' kitchen table. Dated pages include "Rootday, Firstfruit, 416 SR", "Hearthday, Stillmonth, 417 SR" (eleven hounds sitting at the east rows "like a congregation"), "Massday, Thawmonth, 417 SR" (the bells taken for the bore), "Memoryday, Seedmonth, 417 SR" and "Driftday, Lambmonth, 417 SR". "A Cantor who goes to a sickbed holding a sword has mistaken his vocation." Status: **canon (ruled)** (owner's library ruling 1: the journal "now ends that night at the Pellows' kitchen table"). Source: p27-canon.tsv; commit 052a6a6. Bible: new.
- **The dowsing rod** — "A rod of bronze from the chapel's cracked old treble, ground at the forge to 174. Held loose (J), it dips toward what is rooted below." It is sold by Orrin Pale. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The quarry lease** — "The Consortium of Bores takes the quarry, the shaft, and such resonances as lie beneath, for bread at a fixed price, for nine years." It is signed "Aldric Vance, Governor, 414", witnessed "Alder Mossbrook, for the Reeve-Moot". The purse for burning it is sixty sovereigns. Status: **canon (book)** (Aldric sold the Root: Series Bible §Antagonists; the brainstorm) [the wording and the date 414 are PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: Vol II says Mossbrook signed it in 404 (superseded).
- **Rooke's ward-book** — Chained to the Hollow's well-head: "ROOKE. THE HOLLOW. Thirty-one households, eighty-four souls," with green circles against most names, two pencilled circles, and across the top, in a different ink, "FOR THE INQUISITOR, ROOTDAY". Status: **canon (book)** (Rooke's book and the Inquisitor asking for the list: MS ch. 3, ll. 999, 1067; ch. 7, l. 2397) [the chained book and its header are PROPOSED]. Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **"Recheck next month"** — Rooke's own words for Pim (MS ch. 3, l. 999). In "The List" you write them in pencil against the Pells and the Ashbys, and Rooke later goes over it in his own hand. Status: **canon (book)** (the phrase) [the quest is PROPOSED]. Source: p30-hollow.tsv.
- **The hush-saw, the sap-iron, the brown bottle, the long drops** — Rooke's instruments: a fine-toothed saw with a felted grip, an iron from the brazier, a brown bottle, and the drops that "take your voice, not the hurt". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 3, ll. 971–975; ch. 13). Source: p30-hollow.tsv, p34-hob.tsv. Bible: new to the bible.
- **Lyra's chalk note** — On the Carders' chimney-breast: KEEP HIS HANDS WARM. DRY. WHEN THEY LOOK, HE HOLDS STILL. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p30-hollow.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The hot brick** — Wrapped in flannel and put in Pim's pallet: "The smell of the cellar gives way to the smell of hot wool." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p30-hollow.tsv.
- **The hush-whistle (staked)** — A hush-whistle pushed into the ground on its stake "pipes on by itself, thin and high: for a minute, this is a lamp". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p28-narrow.tsv. Bible: new; the MS has hush-lamps but no "hush-whistle".
- **Sable's desk and blotter** — A ruler, a nib, a blotter and a lamp. The blotter carries the sum "74,611 at twenty a night is ten years and two hundred and twenty days", and under it, "and then begin again". Once she has gone, the sum can still be read backwards in the blotter. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, l. 4113). Source: p31-grove.tsv. Bible: the 74,000 is in Series Bible §Sable; 74,611 and the arithmetic are new.
- **Sable's practice sheet** — "scriptorium. scriptorium. scriptorium." down the page; near the bottom the letters "begin to arrive a little late". Status: **[PROPOSED]** (it follows her fragment, the scriptorium). Source: p31-grove.tsv.
- **The Moot's deed-chests** — "Leases, banns, the minutes of three hundred years of the town deciding things. The Office went through them and took nothing. It already had the names." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p31-grove.tsv.
- **The Bell Yard name-boards** — WICK. JOINER. Chairs mended, coffins to order. / SALK. WHEELWRIGHT (widow, and the work still taken) / LOWRY. WARDEN OF THE LAMPS. Ring at any hour. / AMORY (Rosamund is at the basin) / RENN. COOPER. / AYE. COOPER. (a ring with a notch) / IMRIE. MIDWIFE. Knock twice and wait. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the quest and the boards; the names are canon or from the library). Source: js/grove31.js, p31-grove.tsv.
- **The talking crates** — Twelve crates in the Kestrel's hold, stencilled for the quarry garrison, thirty-six lilac spheres each in straw; they say names. "His crates for the garrison are in his ship's hold." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 4; ch. 8). Source: p33-crates.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Vane.
- **The 432** — The cores in the Kestrel's own hold, murmuring "like a bell" that the cutter's horn could hear: "Four hundred and thirty-two people talking in their sleep. We're a bell, and I've been ringing it for two years." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 21 epigraph, l. 5484, "Your cargo is not silent"; Series Bible: "Four hundred and thirty-two people in a hold who have to go somewhere"). Source: p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Book Two premise and Vol III (b) §State-of-play ("Cores: 432").
- **The twelve seized vox-bottles** — An iron-bound evidence chest in the cutter's hold: twelve blue-glass bottles in felt sleeves, tagged TAKEN AT PORT CANTABILE — RUST COAST TRADE — FOR THE HALL OF CARRIAGE, every one murmuring. Unstoppered, they speak one sentence each ("Tell Harl I paid the rent") and a Quiet follows. Carried aboard, they ride with the 432. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (p35-cutter.tsv header). Source: p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Teller's hold-key and tag** — An iron key on a bosun's lanyard: TELLER — HOLD, and on the back, scratched with a pin, 432. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p35-cutter.tsv.
- **OC-742's log** — "TASKED: follow DK by sound from Oakhaven. REPORT TO: the Arbiter's column, Tramway, ninth day. CARGO: none; held, 12 seized vox-bottles. The bosun's count of DK's hold: 432." The last line, "DK making for", stops. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the Dread Arbiter "nine days behind with a column" is canon: Series Bible). Source: p35-cutter.tsv.
- **The willow-crown** — Vane "put the willow-crown through" the cutter, which came down in the woods to the east. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 21, ll. 5590–5598: the Kestrel goes into the crown of a silver willow and the cutter goes down through the canopy). Source: p35-cutter.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part Four — Coda.
- **The ten small cloaks** — Undyed grey wool, a child's size, with name-tapes in red thread "by somebody's mother". There are ten cloaks and eleven pegs, the red one on the peg nearest the alcove has no tape, and one shoe lies on the floor. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 20, ll. 5304–5318) [grey wool and the red cloak's peg differ from the MS; see Contradictions]. Source: p27-r2.tsv, p37-halder.tsv.
- **A child's slate (IS IT TRUE?)** — Taken from the end of the Cloister risers and held up into the fog in letters six inches high. The Wearer answers "I can't read". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 20, ll. 5400–5406; in the book Lyra holds it). Source: p38-wearer.tsv.
- **Hymn 9** — The Cloister's hymn on the board by the risers: "Hold us in the little light... until the morning comes." The fog sings it in Lyra's whole voice, seven times. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 17, l. 4790; ch. 20, ll. 5424–5438). Source: p38-wearer.tsv, p27-r1.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part Three, Vol V, Vol VI. The bible gives no words.
- **Apples in the Rain** — The orchard working-song Tobin hummed off-key on the fourth bar; the Wearer sings it at the porch. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1, l. 436; ch. 20, l. 5444). Source: p38-wearer.tsv. Bible: new.
- **The harvest home (the Last Sheaf)** — Orla's harvest song, all four lines; Davin has it "even the fourth line". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 4; Vol VII (b) §The Last Sheaf). Source: p36-days.tsv, p38-wearer.tsv. Bible: already in Vol VII (b).
- **The calling-song** — Tobin's last song across the Pellows' yard, "the turn and the falling fifth". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1; ch. 6, l. 2135). Source: p27-canon.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part One — The Narrow.
- **Tobin's funeral office** — Sung by Lyra past the last lamp "in a voice like a torn sheet, in the dark, badly, at full volume", eight bars in harmony with the Wearer, and the coda alone: "I know it's a two-voice part." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 23, ll. 6040–6090). Source: p39-lamps.tsv. Bible: Series Bible §Part Four — Coda ("funeral office").
- **Recorded voices the Wearer uses** — Tobin calling; Old Tam ("Walked right out into the meadow for you, he did. Didn't make the high note."); Davin's fourth line; Pim's fifth "with too much air in it"; Ada Pellow; Kaelen's confession; your own voice "from the Hedge at the dead hour"; and Lyra at twenty-nine: "I've shown him how to hold his hands out stiff, Da." Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 20, l. 5444; ch. 23, l. 6112) [your voice is the game's]. Source: p38-wearer.tsv, p39-lamps.tsv.
- **The Rhymes of the Hedge-Nursery (the skipping rhyme)** — "Voice at the hedge, voice at the door, / answer it once and you'll answer no more. / Too loud brings the hounds, too still brings the Hush, / so murmur, my lamb, in the Narrow. Hush." It is skipped by the children of the Hedge-nursery "two ends of a rope and nobody counting aloud". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1 epigraph, ll. 235–238) [the Hedge-nursery is PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: the rule line is in Series Bible §Part IV; the full rhyme is new to the bible.
- **Flume-reed** — "Cut green from a bed by the Flume. It holds any pitch you give it, and it is dead by this time tomorrow. Not the old wind-reed, which went to sugar-glass in 412." Status: **canon (book)** (the wind-reeds died in the Cascade: Series Bible §The Resonant Reach) [Flume-reed is PROPOSED]. Source: p27-r2.tsv. Bible: new.
- **Broadsheets of Cantabile** — Three weeks old, stuck up with fish-glue: the price of Reach timber at Fortis, a woodcut of a bell with a line through it, and SYNOD REASSURES, with SIX TIMES THIS MONTH in charcoal beside it. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Cantabile broadsheets come in at Stillwater: Vol II). Source: p29-thin.tsv.
- **The Wethercombe notes** — The fold note (AT THE FOLD / "Mother, the soup is on the hook. Do not let it boil.") and the cottage letter ("I have gone up to my sister's at Fortis and I am not coming back to a combe where they burn fires on the hill to keep the dark off the ewes. If you are reading this, you are welcome to the apples."). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p29-thin.tsv.
- **Bettis Renn's letter** — "Name, age, trade. That is what the kind clerk asked, and I answered, and then I asked to go second... My name is Bettis Renn. I am writing it so that it is somewhere." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (wording; the facts are canon). Source: p32-canon.tsv.
- **Umber's letters and the Loose Leaves** — The Veil's day-pages, "thin as onion-skin and every line is a name". Leaf nine thousand and six is Hew Callan's. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p40-umber.tsv.
- **Abner Fenning's goods** — Oiled leather "so it doesn't creak", instruments strung with his own gut (Songs cost a quarter less voice, and their timed effects are shorter), and "Abner's Tempering Brazier & Anvil". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p34-hob.tsv.
- **The hound pellets (three, once)** — Ignatius's three pellets of glass shot. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p27-r2.tsv.
- **The clapperless bell** — The Deacon of the Unrung Bell's gift. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p32-canon.tsv.

### Events, dates and calendar

#### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

##### Book One's calendar in the game

- **Day 0 = Nullday 6 Harvestmonth 419** — G.day 0 is Nullday the sixth of Harvestmonth. Harvestmonth ends at G.day 24. G.day 25 is Rootday 1 Ashfall, and G.day 31 is Memoryday 7 Ashfall (Canopy). This checks against V7's formula, and matches B1's audit (Day 1 is a Nullday). Status: **canon (book)** (V7b §2 fixes the Rite on Hearthday 16 Harvestmonth, which fixes day 0 as the 6th; Phase 11 had marked "the sixth" **[PROPOSED]**). Source: P11 (§Canon calendar). Bible: V7b §2 (implied). The bible is incomplete: V7 §7 does not record 6 or 16 Harvestmonth.
- **The Rite: Hearthday 16 Harvestmonth (G.day 10)** — Rumours count down the days to it. "Hearthday is never cheerful." Status: **canon (book)** (V7b §2; SB §Audit: the casing on Day 11 Hearthday). Source: P6 (d_hearthday); P11. Bible: already in V7b §2.
- **The Last Sheaf hung: Hearthday 8 Harvestmonth (G.day 2)** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12 (§Invented facts). Bible: V7b §5 says "from about day 2" (approximate).
- **The Last Sheaf postponed: Stasisday 10 Harvestmonth (G.day 4)** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: new. This conflicts with the Church's arrival day in B1 (see Contradictions).
- **The Eel-Run night: Driftday 12 Harvestmonth (G.day 6)** — From evening to before dawn, tied to the Driftday Flume-walker. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: already in V7b §2 ("inside Harvestmonth" in 419). The exact date is new.
- **The Drowned Lanterns night** — The first night the player spends in Whisper-Grove after Part Three begins, which is also an Octave-lights night. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12; P14. Bible: already in V7b §4 ("the night the Grove's water is highest"). The game date is new.
- **Octave-light nights** — The eve of the Rite (Growth/Light/Mass), the first night after the Greening (Growth/Decay/Memory), the first night in Whisper-Grove (Memory/Light/Decay), the first night in the Deep Woods (Growth/Decay/Time), and about one night in ten. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P14. Bible: new.
- **The moon: eight phases, one a day** — The moon moves one phase a day over eight days, with almanac lines: "No moon tonight … the hounds come bolder"; "The moon is full. The Hush keeps further off when the sky is lit, the hunters say." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Moon phases). Bible: new. Note: an eight-day lunar month is unusual and nowhere in V7.
- **The market falls on Massday** — Every Massday (day % 8 == 3). Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11. Bible: new.
- **Gazette dateline "<Dayname>, 419"** — "Oakhaven District Desk · Printed under the Pure Note." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§3 UI). Bible: new. V7 §8 says the Office dates as "F/3 and a docket number".

##### Tobin's journal, 412–417 (dated events)

- **Spring 412: tending the east rows** — Pear grafts on the south wall (the Bergamot took, the Pippins sulk). Two frost-cracked orchard reeds are replaced from the beds by the Flume. "Thirty springs" of tending. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 1, "Rootday, early spring, 412 SR"). Bible: new.
- **High summer 412: Lyra and the long cane** — See People. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 2). Bible: new.
- **Ashfall 412: the bells taken; the Cascade; Essa sung down** — "The Choir-Church sent a column, a cart, and a young man with a very clean voice." It took six men and a block to lower the great bell, which did not ring. "The grain from the south has stopped. We are told the Cascade was nobody's fault." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 3). Bible: the Cascade of 412 is already in SB §Part V. **Contradicted** on the bells (see Contradictions).
- **Frostmonth 413: surveyors at the Carders' mill** — See People. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 4). Bible: new.
- **Harvestmonth 414: the quarry lease read in the Greenhall** — See Institutions. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 5). Bible: new. **Contradicted** (see Contradictions).
- **Ashfall 415: the tuning-blade** — See Objects. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 6). Bible: new.
- **Frostmonth 416: three First Reeds silent on Cooper's Row** — Taken down, a reed "spoke perfectly well … in the wind it was silent." Orla could not hear her own kettle boiling. "A place where a sound should be, and is not … It was not unkind." This is the Hush's first touch on Oakhaven in the game. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 7). Bible: new.
- **Ashfall 416: news that Whisper-Grove "has stopped"** — See People (the drover). Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the 416 Silencing itself is **canon (book)**, SB §Part V). Source: P6 (page 8). Bible: already in SB §Part V (416).
- **Spring 417: the bore begins; the Root heard** — Tobin went down "in the first week" and sang the Root note to the wall, and "the wall hummed back". "The engineers say it is 'within tolerance'." Status: **canon (book)** (the bore in 417: SB §Part V). The visit is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 9). Bible: already in SB §Part V.
- **High summer 417: the hounds come; the three parishes summoned; the carder's plan** — Tobin called the Cantors of the three nearest parishes to the Greenhall. Two came, and the third wrote "We have stopped being able to send." A carder proposed "a stopper … A hedge three men deep that swallows what we make." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 9 calls this "existing canon", but it is Phase 6 invention and not in B1). Source: P6 (page 10); P9 (§2). Bible: new.
- **Late summer 417: the Hedge planted** — "Four hundred hands, and the Governor's two." "Thorn in the trench, a man's height of packed wool either side." The carders worked all night by lamp. The town called out together and "there was no echo at all". "The gate is shut at dusk. Harrow keeps the key." Status: **canon (delegated)** (R quotes this text as a basis for its sheep ruling). Source: P6 (page 11); R (§The Watch Downs become a region). Bible: already in SB §The Resonant Reach and §Part V (the Hedge, 417). **Contradicted** on the season (see Contradictions).
- **Autumn 417: Tobin's last page, "For Lyra"** — "Something outside the thorn whistled my own fifth back to me … It was not unkind." "I have chosen the south road and the culvert, and a place under the oaks to put the blade down." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (page 12). Bible: already in SB §Lyra / §Part V (Tobin taken in 417). **Contradicted** (see Contradictions).
- **The Downs flocks eaten inside the Hedge, 417–419** — The Downs sheep were driven down to be shorn for the Hedge's wool, kept inside, and eaten over the two shut-in years. "Nothing took them." Status: **canon (delegated)**. Source: R (§The Watch Downs become a region). It rests on P6 (Tobin page 11) and P9 (the Carders' mill). Bible: new. It also cites "salt mutton is the Reach's staple", which V1 contradicts (see Contradictions).

##### Other dated events

- **Greenroot's end (417)** — Hearthday 417: seven reeds sounding and thirty-one at supper on the green. Massday: Tobin's letter. Nullday: "We have stopped being able to send." Then the count falls: six, four, two ("tables carried into the chapel … to see one another speak"), then "One, and it is mine." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§5). Bible: new. **Contradicted** (see Greenroot in Contradictions).
- **Joram Pellow's death (418)** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9. Bible: new.
- **The Tarrows' departure ("three weeks gone")** — "They went out on the last cart before the gate was barred, three weeks gone." The cart came at seven, and Nan died an hour after. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13. Bible: new. This conflicts with Oakhaven being shut in since 417 (see Contradictions).
- **The Foundry morning, 412** — Status: **canon (book)** (SB §Part V; Prologue). Source: P6 (dream); P9 (Kaelen "The Foundry"). Bible: already in SB §Part V.
- **The Silencing of Whisper-Grove, '16** — "Nobody has put down since the Silencing in '16." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (d_part3). Bible: already in SB §Part V.
- **The Rift opened, 413** — Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (r_baskets; Codex; map note). Bible: already in V3b §Timeline corrections; V6 §2.
- **The Edict of Symmetry: the city played nine hours** — Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (x_organum_nine; o_notice). Bible: already in V6 §3.1 and §5 (389).
- **Sable's spring 416** — "I would have said it myself, in the spring of 416." Status: **canon (book)** (V3b: F/3 redrafted spring 416; SB: she signed in 416). Source: P6 (Sable letter). Bible: already in V3b.
- **Weekday names in use** — Rootday, Hearthday, Massday, Stasisday, Dawnday, Driftday, Memoryday, Nullday appear throughout (e.g. Quist dusts the desk on Rootday, Hew's Dawnday supper). Status: **canon (book)** (V6 §0; V7). Source: P6; P9; P11. Bible: already in V6 §0 and V7. "Thursday" in the Halder letter breaks this (see Contradictions).

#### From Phases 15–25 (B)

- **The Hedge planted (417) with the Reach's wool** — "The autumn of 417", as the Rulings cite from Book Four. The owner's later ruling says "the summer of 417 ('two summers ago'), after Tobin, and the Consortium paid for it" (ROADMAP.md §R2, The owner's canon rulings). Status: **canon (book)** for the year. The season is disputed; see Contradictions. Source: Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md (§The Watch Downs become a region). Bible: Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md §0 has "the planting of the Hedge … 417"; the season is new.
- **The two shut years (417–419) and the eating of the flocks** — "Over the two years Oakhaven lived shut in, they were eaten." Status: **canon (delegated)** (Rulings §The Watch Downs become a region). Source: Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md. Bible: new.
- **The Western Tally Line goes dead (417)** — "LINE DEAD BOTH ENDS. HEDGE." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`items.tally.desc`). Bible: new.
- **The Bellwether shut and reopened** — Shut "the night the Hedge closed"; reopened "the week Tamar came back up". Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`gideon.topic`). Bible: new.
- **The Chalk Ram last scoured ten years ago** — "It's been ten" against a seven-year custom. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`kit.intro`). Bible: new.
- **Forty back-ends of fold-fires** — Absalom lit them forty years running until the Hedge. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`absalom.normal`). Bible: new.
- **The 409 flood-relief convoy and WATER, 409** — The brief's "a relief convoy's depot from 409" was reconciled with a Silencing in 416 like this: the Grove's water rose in 409 (WATER, 409 on the stair wall); a 409 flood-relief depot was taken over after 416 by the Synod's restorative administration; it was abandoned when its workers began to repeat themselves. Phase 16 Question 1 asked "Keep that, or move the date?", and it was never answered. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (labelled "[PROPOSED reconciliation]"). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (Questions 1; §DEEP canon notes). Bible: Vol I §III.7 still dates "Relief convoys stopped in 409" under its old Silencing of 401. See Contradictions.
- **The Silencing of Whisper-Grove (416)** — The game places the Silencing in 416. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (Questions 1). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part V — Timeline (416 SR) and Vol V §0. Vol I §2.2 still has 401.
- **The quarry lease date** — First 404 (Mossbrook), now 414 (Aldric, witnessed by Mossbrook). Status: **cut or changed** (ROADMAP.md §R2). Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md (§The Factor's safe…). Bible: Vol I §III.1 and Vol II §People You Can Use Today have 404.
- **The bells taken for bore-teeth** — 412 SR in the Phase 20 doc; 417 now. Status: **cut or changed** (owner's ruling, ROADMAP.md §R2). Source: Phase 20 - New In-Game Text.md (§2 Unchanged…). Bible: new. Series Bible has no date.
- **"Prologue: The River Gate" — Rootday 19 Frostmonth 419** — Lyra comes through the river gate in the dark, two months after the Greening. The Kestrel set her down at Greenroot Hamlet on Memoryday (17 Frostmonth), and she walked two days; the snow began the first night. The extra stops at "You'd best come and see the Governor". The title is **[PROPOSED]**: Phase 17 Question 1 asked to keep it or rename, and no answer was found. Status: **canon (book)** for the moment (Book Four, Part One, ch. 4). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§P17 PROLOGUE; Questions 1). Bible: already in Vol VII - The Calendar of Aethelgard.md §7 (19 Frostmonth, Rootday: "Lyra reaches Oakhaven").
- **The leaf-fall: Nullday 18 Frostmonth, the dead hour** — "Nullday night. Last night but one, at the dead hour. All at once." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Vol VII §7 (18 Frostmonth).
- **The Kestrel left Oakhaven in Harvestmonth** — Lyra has not been inside the Hedge since: "They said you'd gone up in the airship and the airship'd gone down in the Woods. They said the Church had you." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Why this moment; Dorran's lines). Bible: already in Vol VII §7 (Harvestmonth).
- **A trip to Port Cantabile — Memoryday 7 Ashfall 419 SR** — Three days after Book Two ch. 1 (4 Ashfall). The weekday agrees with Vol VII, since 13 Ashfall is a Dawnday. The Burning (15 Frostmonth), Bettany's death and the counting-house murder are deliberately not staged. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the date and the errand). Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (strings 4, 7; §What is canon…). Bible: Vol VII §7 has 13 Ashfall Dawnday; 7 Ashfall is new.
- **Jory Tace lost three years ago** — Lost "off the Cay passage three years ago". The bottle was bought "the spring after" for four sovereigns. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 204). Bible: new.
- **The winter key since the Fracture** — "She has been in the winter key since the Fracture. Seven years of winter." 412 + 7 = 419. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 167). Bible: already in Vol V §Reef-Readers.
- **The Screaming Year** — Hesper: "Since the Screaming Year? More than I will say in the Yard." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 257). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part V — Timeline and Vol IV §1.4.
- **"The hounds came down out of the hills the same year the quiets came up out of the trenches."** Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 322). Bible: new.
- **The Long Night — Stasisday 30 Frostmonth (year unsaid) to first light, Dawnday 1 Stillmonth** — Ten hours, from the first to the dead hour. "Whether any other stair was kept tonight, you cannot know." Status: **canon (book)** for the dates and weekdays; **[PROPOSED]** for the extra. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (§Intro card; §Dawn…; §Mechanics notes). Bible: already in Vol VII §1, §3 and §7. See Contradictions on "three hundred years".
- **Eclipses ("dark noons")** — A new-moon Dawnday picked by a day hash, 18 a canonical year, never on a feast, festival or story day, and never during the Rite or the escape. "The sun is a black coin with a pale ring round it." "At noon the moon crossed the sun, and the day went to dusk for a minute and a half." Status: **[PROPOSED]** (Phase 15: "Eclipses are not in the author's canon"). Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (header; §Lore notes; §Eclipse). Bible: new.
- **Rite timing references** — Lyra's voice "is not mine to spend before Hearthday is past". The Rite is on Hearthday. Phase 24 puts the Flambeau on the road "nine days before the Rite". Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Training: Lyra); Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Vol VII (b) §5 (the Rite on Hearthday the sixteenth) and Series Bible (canon).md §Timeline (Book One).
- **The three hundredth sunrise, 412 SR (the Prologue)** — Phase 24 Question 1 asks whether a later phase should open on Solenne on the risers before the flight. It was never answered. Status: **canon (book)** for the scene; **[PROPOSED]** for the question. Source: Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md (Questions 1). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part XI (The Cracked Bell).
- **"It is autumn, in the four hundred and nineteenth year…"** — The opening's narrated INTRO paragraphs, unchanged. Status: **canon (book)**, as game INTRO text. Source: Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md (shot 1). Bible: Vol VII §7 (419).
- **Driftday** — Hetty: "Eat it slowly; it's the last till Driftday." Status: **canon (book)** (the weekday). Source: Phase 22 - New In-Game Text.md (`hetty.thanks.2`). Bible: already in Vol VI §The eight-day week.

#### From Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

- **Game day 0 is Nullday, Book One's Day 1** — The eight-day week runs Rootday · Hearthday · Massday · Stasisday · Dawnday · Driftday · Memoryday · Nullday, so the game's day 1 is Rootday, day 2 Hearthday, day 3 Massday, and day 5 Dawnday. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1 is Nullday) and consistent with Vol VI §The eight-day week — RULING. Source: js/days35.js; commit e25b999. Bible: already in Vol VI and Vol VII.
- **Nullday: the Madder birth** — A girl born to the Madders. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 1). Source: p32-canon.tsv.
- **Rootday: the Grafters' round and Hob's toes** — Rooke takes Hob's three toes; the Grafters' wagon is in the Hollow from 08:00 to 14:00. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 3) [the game's every-Rootday timing contradicts "the second Rootday of every month"]. Source: js/hollow30.js.
- **Hearthday afternoon: Lyra teaches Pim the whistle** — On the Carders' step. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 6, l. 2013). Source: js/days35.js.
- **Hearthday evening: Davin Hale dies at the Boar** — From 20:00 on game day 2. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 4). Source: js/days35.js, p36-days.tsv.
- **Massday: the boots and the lure** — Rooke has Davin's boots from Massday morning; at the Listening Hour (from 19:00) the Hedge whistles Pim's whistle from the culvert and he goes. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 6). Source: js/days35.js.
- **Dawnday sunrise: the Church comes up the Greenroot road** — Under glass-fire, singing. The warrant is read, and Ignatius and his Legionnaires take the chapel. It comes sooner if the coil is done first (the game's own trigger, `rite_ready`). Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 7) [the early trigger is PROPOSED]. Source: js/days35.js, p36-days.tsv.
- **The Rite and the Greening** — The shaft warning: "the heists close, and when the Greening comes, the town goes with it." Status: **canon (book)** (the Rite on Hearthday; Vol VII (b): "the Church set the Rite for Hearthday the sixteenth of Harvestmonth"). Source: p27-r1.tsv.
- **Tobin taken: spring 417, at the Pellow farm, before the Hedge** — Status: **canon (ruled)** (library ruling 1). Source: p27-canon.tsv.
- **The bells taken in 417 for the bore's teeth** — "The Chapel's six, the clock-bell off the Greenhall, the little bells in the reed-rows." The chapel chalk reads "TAKEN FOR BORE-TEETH, 417 SR. THEY RANG IN E." Status: **canon (ruled)** (library ruling 2: "417, 'the first year of the quarry'"). Source: p27-canon.tsv. Bible: Series Bible §Part V has the quarry bore beginning in 417.
- **The Hedge planted in the summer of 417** — After Tobin, paid for by the Consortium. Status: **canon (ruled)** (library ruling 3). Source: ROADMAP R2, Library Notes. Bible: already in Series Bible §Part V ("417 SR ... The Hedge is planted").
- **The quarry lease, 414** — See Objects. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the date). Vol II's 404 is superseded.
- **The Veil folded, c. 300 SR** — Status: **canon (ruled)**. See Institutions.
- **Whisper-Grove's Unnaming, 416** — Halder was taken on the second day in the first hour, the Renns on the fourth day ("of the harvest, in the sixth hour"), and Sable wrote 74,611 names in eleven days. The game's Part Three card still says "seventy-four thousand people forgot themselves in one night in 416" (see Contradictions). Status: **canon (book)** (the days: MS ch. 14, ch. 18). Source: p31-grove.tsv, p32-canon.tsv, p40-umber.tsv.
- **Hob's death aboard, the fourth hour** — Part Three's opening. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 13, l. 3914). Source: p34-hob.tsv.
- **Day 6 of the flight** — OC-742's log line: "Day 6. DK took on a press and an archivist at Whisper-Grove." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: p35-cutter.tsv.
- **The coda: the second hour** — Lyra walks out past the last lamp at the second hour. If the strike comes too early, it goes away for a week, or until the lamps are set again the next night. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 23, l. 6040) [the retry is PROPOSED]. Source: p39-lamps.tsv.
- **The Eel-Run, announced on its day** — Status: **[PROPOSED]** (the announcement is a game fix; the Eel-Run itself is bible canon, Vol VII (b), "late Firstfruit"). Source: commit dd2c3bf.
- **Firstfruit, not August** — Status: **canon (ruled)** (library ruling 9). Source: p27-canon.tsv.
- **Fourteen or fifteen of Leaffall, 419** — The twice-taken man's entry keeps the MS's month "Leaffall". Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, l. 4165). Source: p31-grove.tsv. Bible: Leaffall is not in Vol VII's calendar (see Contradictions).
- **Stasisday (the gallery)** — Sable first saw the proctor on a Stasisday. Status: **canon (book)** (MS ch. 14, l. 4153). Source: p31-grove.tsv.

### Sayings and language

#### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

- **"Does it ring?" / "True enough."** — Used constantly, and played on: "Does it ring? The orchard rings. True enough." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6; P9. Bible: already in V2 §Dialect (Reach) and V5 §Reach Customs.
- **"Sap and splinters."** — Hetty's oath. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6; P9 (Vane). Bible: already in V2 §Dialect.
- **"You're heavy?" / "Heavy enough."** — The Fortis greeting. Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (x_fortis; p_letter; Codex). Bible: already in V2 §Dialect (Iron Peaks) and V5.
- **"A man's word weighs what he has kept of it" / "A promise weighs nothing until it has been kept" / "a man whose word has mass"** — Fortis sayings. Status: **[PROPOSED]** (built on V2's "a heavy man"). Source: P6 (p_letter; p_order). Bible: already in V2 §Dialect (base); these phrasings are new.
- **"Everyone can carry a tune, nobody can read one."** — Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P12 (townsfolk). Bible: already in V1 §III.1 and V7b §1.
- **"The ninth is not stone."** — "There is a half-line my nan used to hum and stop." Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (x_ninth; o_leaf; dream). Bible: already in V1 §The Doctrine of the Ninth Anchor; V3.
- **"Eight is not the octave. Count again."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (o_leaf). Bible: new.
- **"Never answer it. Answering tells it you are there."** — Status: **canon (book)** (the SB mimic rule). Source: P6 (d_hedge_answered). Bible: already in SB §Part IV.
- **"Keep talking."** — Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6 (map note). Bible: already in V6 §1–2.
- **"Line-talk"** — Status: **canon (book)**. Source: P6. Bible: already in V1 §4.2.
- **Hetty's house rules** — "Singing is free in here and it will get you killed." "I have never lost a patron to a hound. Only to a song, and a song is not a thing I serve." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (a_hetty_*; Hetty letter). Bible: new.
- **"A room that goes quiet is a door left open."** — "My mother taught me that, and the Hedge taught her." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (a_lamps). Bible: new as a saying; it echoes SB §Part IV ("a child who does not cry at all is a door left open").
- **Weather lines** — "Rain … soft as a hand on a hood. Sound will not carry in it, which is a mercy and a danger both." "The oaks … will ring like a bad conscience." "The Hedge likes mist." "Walk like you owe it money." "The Hedge cannot find us under cloud." "Sound carries on a night like this, so keep it in your chest." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (§Weather and hour). Bible: new.
- **"The oaks ring like bronze"; "I would give a month's wages for one plain tree."** — Status: **canon (book)** for the ringing (SB §The Resonant Reach); the second line is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (a_oaks). Bible: already in SB.
- **"Red was redder when I was a girl"** — "Everyone over thirty says so." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (a_colour). Bible: new. Compare SB §Part IV "The World Went Flat".
- **"A slap is paperwork in Oakhaven. A bruise is a bounty."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (c_assault). Bible: new.
- **"A Cantor's voice is the one tool he cannot sharpen."** — Bettany's answer: "That was a poor reason to leave it out in the rain." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 1). Bible: new.
- **"A Cantor's voice is a thing you spend, like lamp oil."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (l2_orla_pim). Bible: new. Compare V5's Ilse Brennan: "The voice is only the most common way of spending it."
- **"Keep it in your chest, where it keeps."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 12; weather). Bible: new.
- **"We are safe … as a candle is safe in a jar."** — Lyra adds: "A candle in a jar is also no use to the room." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Tobin page 11); P9. Bible: new.
- **"A martyr is just a musician who gave up before the coda."** — In Phase 9, Lyra attributes it to Tobin. Status: **canon (book)** (SB gives it as Lyra's line). Source: P9 (Lyra "Tobin"). Bible: already in SB §Lyra Ashgrove. The attribution differs; this is compatible if she is quoting him.
- **"A book that is read to the end is not lost."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (§8). Bible: new.
- **"Leather is quieter than people."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Hob). Bible: new.
- **"The Ledger prefers an honest hole to a tidy page."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (Sable kept). Bible: new.
- **"Anyone can who will stay to the end. That is all family ever was."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P13 (Wenna). Bible: new.
- **Tomas: "A struck bough remembers the hour."; "A man could miss a creak."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9. Bible: new.
- **"A creak is a word, out there."; "Bless the Flume."; "The eel does not take credit, love."** — Market speech. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P11 (§Vendors). Bible: new.
- **"It is like prayer, only it works."; "The grain does not read notices."** — Festival speech. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P12. Bible: new.
- **Companion sayings** — Kaelen: "That is arithmetic with the sign wrong"; "Seen is heavier, in a way, and easier to carry." Lyra: "Waste is a reed nobody cut"; "A day is exactly long enough for the thing you meant to say"; "We choose, and then we pay … That's not a complaint. It's tuning." Sable: "Someone has to forgive the remembering." Vane: "I knew the way you know a stair is missing in the dark." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P9 (companion conversations). Bible: new.
- **"Is it in pain?" / "It is a root, sir. Roots don't."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (echo sh_rv). Bible: new.
- **"Mama says the loud one is the one that gets heard."** — Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: P6 (echo lp_gift). Bible: new.

- **"Does it ring?" / "True enough."** — The Reach greeting and answer, used by Orrin Haft, Dessa Quill, the Compact steward and the Long Night keepers ("Does it ring? Don't answer"). Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Phase 16 - New In-Game Text.md; Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: already in Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md §Dialect (Realm I) and Vol V §Reach Customs (expanded).

- **The pilot's rhyme (Hollis Teel)** — "*E for the waking, F-sharp the flood, / G is the gold of the morning mud; / A is the grey, and B is the slack, / C is the turning, and she is coming back.*" Phase 17 Question 5 lists "Teel's rhyme" as an invention. Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 17 - New In-Game Text.md (string 180). Bible: new. Vol IV says "shanties encode navigation".

- **The Church's sermon sayings** — "Now there is one Note. One Note, one town, held. Hold it and you are held." "A quiet is only a note that has not been asked to sound." "A frightened town sings out of tune, and a held town sings one note, and the quiet has nothing to take." "The Church does not tell the time. It keeps it." "Say nothing under the Note." The congregation answers "Held." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§The deacon; §Sermons). Bible: new.
- **"One note. One law. One hand."** — The Inquisitor's words, carried over the Synod column in the opening. Status: **canon (book)** (Book One). Source: Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md (shot 4). Bible: new. It is in the manuscript only.

"The Church says it is weather. The Church would." Status: **[PROPOSED]**. Source: Phase 15 - New In-Game Text.md (§Eclipse). Bible: new.

- **"Too loud brings the hounds, too still brings the Hush."** — Status: **canon (book)**. Source: Game Design Document - Book One Prototype.md (§4.1); Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md (shot 3). Bible: already in Series Bible (canon).md §Part XII.
- **"Keep your voice in your chest."** — The old INTRO's last words, now Harrow's. Status: **[PROPOSED]**, as earlier game text, voiced in Phase 24. Source: Phase 24 - New In-Game Text.md. Bible: new.
