- **Skarth** in the Iron Peaks, its high shelf and the standing-ground under **the Tooth**. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_carrying_of_ketil. Bible: new.
- **Bastion Fortis:** the Lower Gate, the outer stair, the visitors' row by the west door of the Weighing Hall, and the fourth terrace (the quartermaster's). **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_bastion_instructions, lib_f_ruddock_timber_run. Bible: the Weighing Hall is in Vol V §The Weighing Hall.
- **The Organum:** the Choir-Hall bellows, the Organum road's oaks (galls), the east rank of pipes, and a counting-house in the Brass Quarter. **[PROPOSED]** (the Brass Quarter itself is MS l.726). Source: lib_b_shepherds_ear, lib_g_bistre_commonplace, lib_g_blue_ribbon, lib_e_oakhaven_prospectus. Bible: the Choir-Hall is in Series Bible §The Organum and Vol VI §3.1.
- **Whisper-Grove:** the Carters' Yard (a street); the lamp-makers' loft in the Bell Yard; nine lamp rounds; eleven lamps on the Bell Yard round; the blue door at the top of the Ashlane steps; the Moot-house gallery, muniment store and cellar; the west wall of travellers' and householders' books. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: the g_ books, lib_f_lantern_on_the_basin. Bible: new; the Weeping Plaza, Bell Yard, Ashlane and Moot-house are only in the MS (ch.14–16).
- **The House of the Silent Veil:** the labyrinth below the kitchens; the lower hall (Memoryday); the scriptorium cabinet of scribes' names; the south wing; the fourth desk on the south side; and the pool by the House, where lanterns are set from the steps under the arch. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: the Veil books. Bible: the labyrinth and stacks are in Vol V §The Stacks and Vol I §III.7.
- **The Fenmark register and the Mossbank strays** (unnamed Woods places). **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_h_daybook. Bible: new.

#### Institutions and offices

- **The Fellowship of Lamp-Makers and Lighters of Whisper-Grove**, with its six Ordinances and a warden. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: new.
- **The Moot of Whisper-Grove:** every householder may sit and speak once (twice if very old); nothing is decided until the lamp-man lights the door lamp; it sits from the second hour of the afternoon; its rolls are tied in blue; it bans fires on the plaza. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_plaza_and_canopy, lib_g_basin_table, lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: new (Vol I and II "Moot" hits are the Reach's Reeve-Moot).
- **The Moot schools of Whisper-Grove print a Primer** (with the 413 Lesson for Quiet). **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_grove_primer. Bible: new.
- **The Bellows:** about 400 members on Tier Eleven, voting by pipe-rail taps; a sit-down in spring 415; they print chapbooks. **canon (book)** for the Bellows (400, no leverage), with the vote, the sit-down and the chapbooks **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_between_the_falls, lib_c_hand_on_it. Bible: Vol II §Local Powers.
- **"Unhaus"** is written in the indenture books for Tier Eleven, which has no House. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_between_the_falls. Bible: new; Tier Eleven of no House is in Vol II §Settlements.
- **The Undertow is one of the Row's four papers.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_undertow_reach. Bible: the four papers are in Vol II §Settlements / §Local Powers.
- **The Harbour lets the Row print because the Row never asks anyone to do anything.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_undertow_reach. Bible: new.
- **The Harbour ruled that a ship over the harbour is "at sea, however high", so the Ninth and Sixteenth Articles apply aloft.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_going_up. Bible: the Articles are in Vol IV §The Free Captains and the Articles.
- **The Harbour-Queen amended Article the First in her own hand in 414: "or until she cannot".** **canon (book)** (Book Two). Source: lib_b_true_and_modest. Bible: new to the bible (Book Two Part One).
- **The Choir-Church's annual "going-out" of Flambeaux** from the Basilica of the Pure Note, with a printed sermon. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_only_song_sermon. Bible: new; the Basilica is in Vol III §Appendix and Vol VI §3.3.
- **The Basilica is at Sol-Luminary.** **[PROPOSED]** (implied by "Canon … of the Basilica, Sol-Luminary"). Source: lib_f_constancy_of_the_light. Bible: new (no volume locates it).
- **The Little Catechism of the Pure Note** is printed (416) under the seal of "His Resonance the High Patriarch", and is to be said on one pitch. **[PROPOSED]**; the two core questions are **canon (book)**. Source: lib_e_little_catechism. Bible: Series Bible §What everyone believes / Part III.
- **The Arbiterium's Digest of the Standing Law:** third issue 414, ten articles. **[PROPOSED]** except the canon pieces (writ posting, reading aloud, black ribbon, Attainder and Grace, Symmetry). Source: lib_e_digest_reach_law. Bible: Symmetry is in Vol VI §The Edict of Symmetry (389); the others are MS ch.21 and 24 and Vol VI §5.
- **Article 7: no assembly of more than twelve to sing, save in the Church's keeping.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_digest_reach_law. Bible: new.
- **Article 10: a sap-marked person is struck from the bench roll and the census, and their testimony needs a sound witness's affidavit.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_digest_reach_law. Bible: new.
- **Article 9: registration with the Office at the first relief station is a condition of transit.** **canon (book)**. Source: lib_e_digest_reach_law. Bible: Series Bible §Part Three (relief station); MS ch.18.
- **Article 6: all bells, chimes and resonant metal are requisitioned to the public works under the Consortium's warrant, with a receipt.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_digest_reach_law. Bible: new.
- **The Grafting Office's handbook:** ten articles, Oakhaven Works Issue 417. Article 4 is **canon (book)** (MS ch.3 epigraph); the rest are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_grafting_handbook. Bible: new; the sap-mark is in Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures.
- **The Consortium's Second Subscription for the Oakhaven Works:** shares of 10 sovereigns in lots of 10; a dividend on the Dial Day after the casing; the motto "What the ground gives, the Consortium gathers". **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_oakhaven_prospectus. Bible: new; the Seven Chairs are in Series Bible §The Iron Synod.
- **The Office of the Catalogue supplies the Oakhaven pumps' labour under contract** (Hollows). **canon (book)**. Source: lib_e_oakhaven_prospectus. Bible: MS ch.5; Series Bible §Part One (pumps).
- **The Office's grey ink by the cask, its wages, and the 410 request for twelve scribes for "a great register of the Woods… by name and voice and age".** **[PROPOSED]**; the hint is allowed by r12. Source: lib_a_what_is_written. Bible: new.
- **The Veil before the Office had 312 scribes; when it joined, three walked into the Woods.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_what_is_written. Bible: new; the absorption c. 300 SR is in Series Bible §Part V — Timeline.
- **The Veil was folded into the Office "as of Thawmonth" (c. 300 SR); the scribes who objected were sent to field ledgers in the Woods.** **canon (ruled)** (the softening at the owner's call, 7ccd72e; the date ruling in dd2c3bf). Source: bk_veil (upgrades.js). Bible: Series Bible §Part V — Timeline (c. 300 SR).
- **The stamp "RECEIVED INTO THE OFFICE … RETAINED AGAINST NEED".** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_veil_rule. Bible: "retained against need" is in Vol VI §3.5 Bin seven.
- **The Clerk of Visitors to the Martial Compact.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_bastion_instructions. Bible: new.
- **The Watch (Oakhaven) commissions night-hummer guides.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_ears_after_dark. Bible: new.

#### Events and dates

- **Tobin was taken in the spring of 417 at the Pellow farm, before the Hedge.** **canon (ruled)** (r1). Source: lib_a_wool_wall, lib_e_off_the_slate. Bible: Series Bible §Part V — Timeline (417) and Vol V §0; "Pellow" in Series Bible §Part Two.
- **The bells were taken in 417, "the first year of the quarry", for the bore's teeth: the Chapel's six, the Greenhall's clock-bell and the reed-row bells.** **canon (ruled)** (r2). Source: lib_a_greenroot_road, lib_a_first_history, lib_e_digest_reach_law, lib_c_under_a_hands_width ("on a Massday", **[PROPOSED]**). Bible: new as a dated fact (MS ch.1).
- **The Hedge was planted in the summer of 417 and paid for by the Consortium; the town did the work, with an engineer pegging the line.** **canon (ruled)** (r3); the engineer is **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_wool_wall, lib_a_first_history. Bible: Series Bible §Part V and Vol V §0 give 417.
- **Hounds came to the orchard edge at the turn of 417 (eleven, on a Massday night).** **canon (ruled)** (r7). Source: lib_a_wool_wall. Bible: new; the game's tobin.js p.9 matches.
- **Hounds in the wider Reach and on the Coast from 412.** **canon (ruled)** (r7). Source: lib_b_narrow_cry, lib_c_bellwether_book, lib_f_long_mole_songs. Bible: Series Bible §Part IV.
- **The spring 413 grain paper was "grain against survey"; the Frostmonth 413 survey on the Flume bank; the Harvestmonth 414 lease for nine years, carried with one vote against.** **canon (book)** for the 414 lease (Tobin's Journal p.5; the d73de90 ruling on the lease, 414), with the paper's wording **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_greenroot_road. Bible: new.
- **The works were built in 415–416; the mast took a summer to raise.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_greenroot_road. Bible: new.
- **The quarry was pumped dry in 11 days in the lease year (414).** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_stone_then_glass. Bible: new.
- **The hard winter of 412–413 had 17 dead; graves were dug by fire; harness was boiled by Candlefast.** **canon (book)** (MS ch.2), with the burning and the count **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_hard_winter, lib_a_first_history. Bible: new.
- **The orchards went "on a still morning at the back end of summer" 412.** **[PROPOSED]** (the cross-check standard). Source: lib_a_hard_winter. Bible: new.
- **The Thermal Margin disaster, 417:** a pressure order by runner on a Massday, nine days held, the fires gone cold on the tenth, 907 on shift, 900 dead, 7 survivors. **canon (book)** for the 900, four seconds and F-sharp (Vol I §III.3, Vol II); the date and details are **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_four_seconds. Bible: Vol II §Settlements.
- **The Bellows' three-day sit-down, spring 415.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_between_the_falls. Bible: new.
- **The Great Beat missed one fall on a Driftday at the back end of a year** (beat-drift). **canon (book)** for beat-drift (Vol II hazard), with the event **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_hand_on_it. Bible: Vol II §Hazard Table — The Caldera.
- **A capstan chorus on the Long Mole brought the first hounds in off the harbour in 412.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_long_mole_songs. Bible: new (MS ch.4 has the first hounds off the harbour in the Screaming Year).
- **The Harbour's ruling on skyships aloft.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_going_up. Bible: new.
- **Battery Eleven's second gun burst at the Refusal, Reedmonth 417.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_keep_talking_letters. Bible: new; the Refusal is in Vol VI.
- **Lathbury Mill emptied in the spring of 418.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_still_wheel. Bible: new.
- **A Church examiner's report on the Reach Cantors (c. 358), "Agricultural custom".** **[PROPOSED]**; the phrase itself is **canon (book)**. Source: lib_b_the_pruner. Bible: the phrase is in Vol VII (b) §1.
- **The Veil surveyed by the Synod c. 290 SR, before the absorption.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_veil_rule. Bible: new.
- **The Office's clerks counted the shelves in 416 and the vellum left the House in four wagons; the House's bell was requested; the House closed for the winter.** **canon (book)** for the vellum (Vol V §House), with the details **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_h_daybook, lib_g_orpiment_meditations. Bible: Vol V §The House of the Silent Veil.
- **Office clerks registered the Ashlane's children by "the voice" in Sheafmonth 416.** **canon (ruled)** as a hint (r12). Source: lib_g_midwife_daybook. Bible: new.
- **The 407 high water, the highest in 30 years, when 4,000 lantern papers were given out.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: new.
- **In 419 the Church set the Rite for Hearthday 16 Harvestmonth and the Last Sheaf was forbidden; the stand stays uncut in the north field.** **canon (book)**. Source: lib_a_almanack_419, lib_e_off_the_slate, lib_d_narrow_larder. Bible: Vol VII (b) §The Last Sheaf.
- **The Eel-Run was late in 419.** **canon (book)**. Source: lib_a_almanack_419, lib_d_rudds_almanac, lib_b_year_of_nights (418 late as well, **[PROPOSED]**). Bible: Vol VII (b) §The Eel-Run.
- **The Root Feast of 419 was kept loud inside the Hedge, the first since the Hedge.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_almanack_419. Bible: new.
- **Ringing-Wood Day 418 was held in the Hedge's lee with nobody watching and no beams named.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_fellers_book. Bible: Vol VII (b) §Ringing-Wood Day for the festival.
- **Septimus Fallow wrote to the Arbiterium about grace in 416; writs read aloud in 413 and 414 cost lives at the gate.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_digest_reach_law. Bible: new. *See Contradictions.*
- **The Undertow's compositor went missing, and a Driftwood Cay loader of "components" vanished.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_undertow_reach. Bible: new.

#### Customs, sayings and lore

- **The sexton tolls the town bell for the dead.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_hard_winter. Bible: new.
- **Proposals are made at a hand's width; one that is overheard and can be repeated does not count.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_lamplighters_note. Bible: new.
- **An eel put in your bucket at the Eel-Run is "very nearly a proposal".** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_year_of_nights. Bible: new.
- **Hearth bread for the Last Sheaf:** one round loaf, broken, never cut; the first piece to the eldest, the heel to the youngest, crumbs to the hearth; the Sheaf-child gets the first slice. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_narrow_larder, lib_c_last_sheaf_play. Bible: hearth bread is in Vol VII (b) §The Last Sheaf.
- **The Last Sheaf play:** the Thief of Bread became the Voice at the Gate; whoever answers brings the cider; the Voice may not use a true dead name; nobody claps. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_last_sheaf_play. Bible: new.
- **The Sheaf Song couplet "Cut it low, and cut it slow, / the last of the year is let to go"; Wethercombe cuts its own last stand to it.** **canon (book)** (game text Phase 12, itself marked [PROPOSED] there). Source: lib_c_bellwether_book, lib_c_last_sheaf_play. Bible: the Sheaf Song is in Vol VII (b) §The Last Sheaf.
- **Bellwether songs:** the Drove-Road, Fold-Fire, Scouring the Ram (every seven years), the Low Song; and the house rule to write a new song in or buy a jug. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_bellwether_book. Bible: new.
- **The "old uses" of the eight days; don't fell a tree on a Rootday; on Nullday keep somebody by you and leave the lamp in.** **[PROPOSED]** (folk custom; the book itself says this is not the Rota). Source: lib_a_almanack_419. Bible: new.
- **The Cantor's curriculum:** entry at 8; a year of breath; "A breath, a note, a fifth, a call, a farewell"; no catechism; a palm on the bark gets yes or no. **canon (book)**, mostly Vol V §4, with the summary formula and the yes/no question **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_orchard_craft. Bible: Vol V §Reach Customs (expanded); tending breath and felling-song are in Vol V §5.
- **Pruning terms "the long cane" (Tobin p.2) and "letting down".** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_a_orchard_craft. Bible: new.
- **The five-note lullaby "made for something very large that could not sleep"; the Longest Night humming done "in place of" something.** **canon (book)** as hints of the Cantors' Secret. Source: lib_a_orchard_craft, lib_e_off_the_slate. Bible: Vol V §The Cantors' Secret.
- **Reeds-miners: lifting sour wind-reed crowns.** **canon (ruled)** (r13). Source: lib_b_reeds_miner. Bible: new.
- **Felling lore:** walking a grove for a month; the knock-notes; a Cantor sings a tree down, paid in cider; beams named on the end-grain; iron-bough grows a hair a year after cutting. **[PROPOSED]**; the felling-song is **canon (book)**. Source: lib_d_fellers_book. Bible: Vol V §5 (felling-song), Vol VII (b) (beams named), Vol I §6.5 (iron-bough grows).
- **Felling in the Narrow:** carded wool on the blade, four strokes a bundle, cut in a gust, leave the tree a day, never run with a log. **canon (book)** (game text Phase 9, "Tomas's Last Load"). Source: lib_d_fellers_book. Bible: new.
- **Bee-telling:** births, deaths (oldest first) and strangers, at dusk, with the two-knuckle one-tap knock; untold bees leave; free honey for Cantors. **canon (book)** (game text Phase 9, the bee quest), with the honey and the order of the dead **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_telling_the_bees. Bible: new.
- **Hush-lamp construction:** brass collar, beeswax seal, a little sharp of the Root, never tuned to a living person, the wick rule. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_reed_and_lamp. Bible: new.
- **A cut reed dies within a day unless it is given a pitch, and Flume-reed survived because it was "never taught".** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_reed_and_lamp. Bible: Vol I §6.5 has the wind-reed dying in a day.
- **Reed-Hanging is now done mouth-to-reed behind shut doors; Flume-reed First Reeds are tuned at the door by the Cantor.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_reed_and_lamp, lib_a_almanack_419, lib_b_year_of_nights. Bible: Vol VII (b) §Reed-Hanging.
- **Night-hummer lore:** never stand up; three kinds of silence; hounds carry stolen sounds; birds go still before them; a name draws faster than a scream; a trick works once. **canon (book)** mostly (Series Bible Part IV; MS ch.1, 2, 4, 6), with the three silences and the birds **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_ears_after_dark. Bible: Series Bible §Part IV — The Rules of Dread.
- **The Narrow's cooking rules:** felted lids, no iron on iron, take the kettle off before it sings, crack bones under water. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_narrow_larder. Bible: new.
- **Acorn cake of iron-bough acorns; eel boiled twice in dock-leaf; three cuttings of flour.** **[PROPOSED]** (the eel is from upgrades.js and calendar11.js). Source: lib_d_narrow_larder. Bible: new.
- **"Nobody will lend you a good jar" (the Clever Dorcas tale).** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_fireside_tales. Bible: new.
- **The Fireside tales (Clever Dorcas, Ebbo Stint, Why the Oaks Ring).** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_fireside_tales. Bible: new.
- **Herbal lore:** willow peeled on the north side; reed-pith chewed raw; moss closing over a splinter; moon-moss for deafness; the mute-drops' smell. **canon (book)** in part (Vol I §III.1 Resonant Moss; game items), the rest **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_yewdale_herbal. Bible: Vol I §III.1.
- **Two cut fingers bound together heal into one.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_yewdale_herbal. Bible: new.
- **Weather-signs and which ones now lie; "the Reach was kept warm from underneath"; the legend that the oaks will drop every leaf in one night.** **[PROPOSED]**. The legend foreshadows Vol V §Leaf-Fall, and the warmth echoes Vol V's "The Root kept the cold out". Source: lib_d_rudds_almanac. Bible: Vol V §Status.
- **Eighth-day naming over a pool, by three mouths.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_midwife_daybook, lib_g_blue_ribbon. Bible: new.
- **The remembering loaf for Canopy:** scored once per name, broken not cut, "everyone eats a piece of everyone"; the candle facing in is "for the house and not for the street". **canon (ruled)** (r6: candles facing in before 416 stand), with the loaf **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_basin_table, lib_g_blue_ribbon. Bible: Vol VII (b) §3 has the candles.
- **Never cook a fish from a remembering pool; lantern cakes; straw-box broth; Moot pasties.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_basin_table. Bible: new.
- **Lantern Night: the people nearest say the name after you; the Fellowship gives free paper, wax and wick and never asks whose name.** **[PROPOSED]** for the repeating and the gift; the lanterns themselves are **canon (book)**. Source: lib_f_lantern_on_the_basin, lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: Vol VII (b) §The Drowned Lanterns.
- **Grove courtesies:** "little mother/father", "dearling", "my dear one"; asking a name is not rude; a lost child stands under a lamp. **[PROPOSED]** (Grove speech is warm per Vol II Realm VII). Source: lib_g_grove_primer, lib_f_lantern_on_the_basin, lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: new.
- **The second half of the skipping rhyme: "So sing in the middle, and sing it soft, and never sing in a rush".** **[PROPOSED]**; the first line is **canon (book)**. Source: lib_g_grove_primer. Bible: Series Bible §Part IV.
- **"Goodnight, Grove" is said at the last lamp of a round.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: new.
- **Grove masons take only their supper for mending the plaza.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_plaza_and_canopy. Bible: new.
- **The town's books are shelved by size, not by name.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_plaza_and_canopy, libraries26. Bible: new.
- **The Moot gave the House vellum, and the House copied the Moot's rolls once a generation against fire.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_plaza_and_canopy. Bible: new.
- **"The Echo catches them, and the Canopy keeps them" (a Grove saying); the Church's man calls the Canopy the Spire of Memory.** **canon (ruled)** as a saying (r12). Source: lib_g_plaza_and_canopy, lib_a_what_is_written, lib_g_basin_table. Bible: Vol I §1.9 Death, Echo, and the Release.
- **Veil practice:**
  - the scribe's name is sealed in the Prior's hand in the cabinet and returned at the door;
  - scribes are styled by colour or creature;
  - novices don't write their names on House paper;
  - on Memoryday a page of the dead is read at dusk in the lower hall, "every eighth day";
  - lanterns are set from the steps under the arch;
  - the stair-prayer;
  - and "Nothing in this book is a record".
  
  **[PROPOSED]** (the Memoryday reading follows the [PROPOSED] Rota of Vol VI §0.1). Source: lib_e_veil_rule, lib_g_bistre_commonplace, lib_g_pitched_hand_exercises, lib_h_daybook. Bible: new; the three rules are MS ch.14, the motto and "voice for entries" are in Vol V §House.
- **The Rule's sayings:** the curve and the cup; "a name kept is a door left on the latch"; a held name may not be spent, lent, sold or set to work (a scribe who does is "a kind of merchant"); "We have outlived eight crowns". **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_veil_rule. Bible: new.
- **The pitched hand:** six marks (line, hook up, hook down, dot for a rest, tie, stroke through for a breath), five heights; it writes how a name sounds. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_pitched_hand_exercises. Bible: the pitched notation of the motto is in Vol V §House.
- **The Veil's ink receipt** (oak-galls from the Organum road, never pool water, green copperas, a willow stick); lampblack from east-stove soot; the Office's grey ink is poor for a thousand years. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_bistre_commonplace. Bible: new.
- **The House's own bell marks the scribes' day ("second bell", "third bell"); the Office asked for the bell in 416.** **canon (ruled)** (r4). Source: lib_a_what_is_written, lib_g_pitched_hand_exercises, lib_h_daybook. Bible: Vol V §The House of the Silent Veil.
- **Cinderhold:** speak three counts and then shut your mouth; four finger-taps on the wrist mean "he kept his hand on it"; the whistle-sign is the flat of the hand. **[PROPOSED]**; the ingot oath and dialect are **canon (book)**. Source: lib_b_between_the_falls, lib_c_hand_on_it. Bible: Vol II §Dialect.
- **Coast:** shanties are charts; the coral has gone flat, so pilots sing flat on purpose; open a bottle at sea in the dark with your ear to the reed; "a quiet goes to the light", so no lanterns in a silent hold. **canon (book)** in part (Vol IV §1.5, Series Bible Part IV quiets), the rest **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_long_mole_songs. Bible: Vol IV §1.5 and Series Bible §Part IV.
- **The Mole-bell is struck once for each soul, never for traitors.** **canon (book)**. Source: lib_f_long_mole_songs. Bible: Vol V §Customs.
- **"I'm chorus to you" (the debt oath).** **canon (book)**. Source: lib_f_long_mole_songs. Bible: Vol II §Dialect.
- **Fortis:**
  - the bell on the Wall struck in threes opens any door to a stranger (the oldest law);
  - a visitors' row records lost visitors' weight;
  - Chimes are refused and sovereigns taken;
  - "Middling" is an insult;
  - no trains run on Nullday in the Peaks;
  - Sounders walk ahead of the Tramway gangs;
  - ringing iron-bough props for the deep levels.

  **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_bastion_instructions, lib_f_ruddock_timber_run. Bible: the Culture of Weight and the Sounders are in Vol V.
- **Skarth's dead are stood up under the Tooth with stones about the knees; the saga is said standing.** **canon (book)** for standing burial (Series Bible Part VI), the rest **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_carrying_of_ketil. Bible: Series Bible §The Iron Peaks.
- **Sandglass:** a husband is mourned 300 days; a House posts its mourners "overdue"; crate contracts run three years; "the book of the indecent"; burial in the Erg at dusk. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_overdue_ghazals. Bible: scheduled mourning is in Vol II §Settlements.
- **Null-Watch:** names draw the Quiet; the Discharge rite; "gone to listen". **canon (book)**. Source: lib_f_keep_talking_letters. Bible: Vol VI §1, Vol II §Dialect.
- **Null-Watch letters are read by a clerk at the Organum on the way through.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_f_keep_talking_letters. Bible: new.
- **The Flambeaux's creed:** "The third hour of the night is the hour the question comes"; the second note is "a crack" (the catechism agrees). **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_only_song_sermon, lib_e_little_catechism. Bible: new.
- **The Church's official cause of the Fracture: "a dissonance was raised at the Dawning".** **canon (book)**. Source: lib_e_little_catechism. Bible: MS l.5722 (the Inquisitorial report).
- **The prayer at the hush-lamp: "Light, keep me true…".** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_little_catechism. Bible: new.
- **The Quiet Faith's 40 hand copies; a brother keeps his tongue to read the Abbess's grey-sealed letters.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_e_already_quiet. Bible: the Tongueless and the Abbess are in Series Bible §The Tongueless.

#### Things, prices and the natural world

- **Skyships have a Fortis-cut lodestone core in an iron cage amidships, which makes them light, and drift-glass lift-tanks, which make them rise.** **canon (ruled)** (r5). Source: lib_a_going_up, lib_d_tank_and_envelope, lib_f_fairweather_companion. Bible: Vol I §III.4 and Part VIII; drift-glass is MS ch.2 and 21.
- **Drift-glass costs 110 sovereigns a tank.** **canon (book)** (Book Two). Source: lib_a_going_up. Bible: new to the bible.
- **Rigging lore:** "a soul is a hundredweight and a half with his boots on"; tap the cradle, not the tank; below 100 ft a sour tank draws hounds; a clipped tank is good for 40 miles. **canon (book)** for the 40 miles (MS ch.24), the rest **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_tank_and_envelope. Bible: new.
- **Weaverling silk patches a tank-weep (Deep Woods, 419).** **canon (book)** (game, kestrel16 and data.js). Source: lib_d_tank_and_envelope. Bible: new.
- **Moss-poultice:** the Consortium's costs a sovereign a pot on the Cay; the Reach price is a tenth. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_c_saffery_lightfoot. Bible: Resonant Moss Poultice is in Vol II §People.
- **Prices:** lamp-oil 4 pence a pint, rising to 5; the almanack one Chime; the Undertow a penny (two from the lockman); serial parts and some books 1 sovereign; iron-bough 20 sovereigns for 40 logs at Stillwater and 2 sovereigns a log at Fortis; Tramway freight 8; lock dues 1. **[PROPOSED]** (the 4 pence is canon via the review). Source: various. Bible: new.
- **Scrip will not change to sovereigns at face value; the Boar & Dulcimer takes it "at a rate".** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_letters_from_the_gate, lib_b_stone_then_glass. Bible: scrip is in Series Bible §Part One.
- **The works-gate pass is a green glass disc; the Catalogue circular is pinned inside the gatehouse.** **canon (book)**. Source: lib_b_letters_from_the_gate. Bible: MS ch.5 (Root-glass disc; Circular 12).
- **A Legion core at the gate repeats "second shelf, behind the flour" and "mind the step, Gran".** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_letters_from_the_gate. Bible: new.
- **Oakhaven's lamps:** 212 on one round; eleven seconds from catch to whistle; 22 lamps in hearing of Tannery Row. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_b_year_of_nights, lib_c_lamplighters_note, lib_d_ears_after_dark. Bible: new.
- **Since the quarry: bees draw comb overnight; bread rises unstoppably; hay and saplings regrow; nails grow a quarter-inch a night.** **canon (book)** (law going soft, Series Bible Part VI), with the bees **[PROPOSED]**. Source: several. Bible: Series Bible §The Resonant Reach.
- **The oaks ring since the bore drew on the Root, "wood gone soft and dense" (the Consortium's explanation).** **[PROPOSED]** for the explanation. Source: lib_c_fireside_tales. Bible: new.
- **Mute-drops smell of bitter almond and wet slate.** **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_d_yewdale_herbal. Bible: new.
- **Orpiment, the brightest yellow and a poison, and minium have faded since 412 (everyone over thirty says it of every colour).** **canon (book)** for "red was redder", the rest **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_orpiment_meditations, lib_g_bistre_commonplace, lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: Series Bible §What is true.
- **Grove lamp oil:** Reach rape oil, cheaper than willow-oil and smoky, mixed half and half since 398. **[PROPOSED]**. Source: lib_g_lamp_book. Bible: new.

#### The serial (Cantabile broadsheet)

- **The serial is a Cantabile broadsheet tale of the Caldera, printed on Broadsheet Row and carried up the river through Stillwater; "the names are changed".** **canon (ruled)** (the owner asked to bring the serial into canon; Review resolution, 27 Oct). Source: books.js. Bible: Broadsheet Row and its route are in Vol II §Settlements.
- **A collapse of the "lower works" in a Caldera works; the assessment office, compensation office, works infirmary, Rim road, lower weighing house, old survey house, docket numbers, "HOLD FOR REWEIGH".** **[PROPOSED]** (deliberately placed outside real Furnace Houses). Source: books.js. Bible: new.
- **A sovereign a part; Hetty's paid-for bundle; the Cantor's lending shelf until the Rite.** **[PROPOSED]** (game). Source: books.js. Bible: new.

### The thirteen rulings

**The questions.** These are verbatim from `Library of the Reach - Notes for the Owner.md` at `636c236`, under "Questions that need your ruling". They were ruled in October 2026 and applied in `052a6a6` (27 Oct), `7ccd72e` and `dd2c3bf`.

**The principles.** Verbatim from the current notes:
- "Manuscript against an in-game book or journal: the manuscript wins."
- "A volume against an in-game book: the volume stands, and the book changes to match."
- "A book that comes close to a spoiler: it stays, unless it makes the twist too easy to guess."
- "New lives for minor canon figures: all fine."

| # | Question (verbatim) | Ruling and what changed (verbatim, condensed with "…") |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **When Tobin was taken.** "The manuscript says spring 417, at the Pellow farm, before the Hedge. js/tobin.js says autumn 417, after it. The library leans on neither." | "Spring 417, at the Pellow farm, before the Hedge (the manuscript)." Tobin's journal now ends that night at the Pellows' kitchen table. "The pages were re-dated and re-ordered: the bells move to 417 and the hounds to Stillmonth 417. The page about planting the Hedge is gone, and the chapel's 412 page keeps its bells." His register, the cairn slate, the inquiry's logbook lines and the prologue's voice line follow the manuscript: "The 'Wearer', the North Gate and the culvert are gone from them, and the Madder child is the Pellow child." "In the library, The Wool Wall and Off the Slate now say spring. The Greenroot letter answering Tobin is set in winter." |
| 2 | **When the bells were taken.** "tobin.js p. 3 says Ashfall 412; the manuscript says the first year of the quarry (417). Every library book now says 417." | "417, 'the first year of the quarry', for the bore's teeth (the manuscript). The chapel's chalk, the townsfolk line about the Church and Tobin's pages now say 417. The blade of 415 is ground from an old cracked treble." |
| 3 | **When the Hedge was planted.** "The manuscript says 'two summers ago'; the journal says late summer; Book Four says autumn 417." | "The summer of 417, 'two summers ago', paid for by the Consortium (the manuscript). A First History of Oakhaven now says summer, and The Wool Wall says Tobin did not live to see it. Book Four's 'autumn' is left for that book." |
| 4 | **Bell-hours in the Veil's House.** "Vol V's 'second bell' against Vol VII §5's striking of bell-hours. The Veil books keep the House's own bell." | "Vol V's 'second bell' is the House's own bell. Vol VII §5 retires only the old eight carillon bell-hours, so the two volumes agree and the Veil books stand." |
| 5 | **What lifts skyships.** "Vol I says a lodestone core; the manuscript says drift-glass. The books follow the manuscript." | "Both: a Fortis-cut lodestone core that cancels the mass (Vol I) and drift-glass lift-tanks that give the lift (the manuscript). Going Up, The Tank and the Envelope and Fairweather's Companion now name both." |
| 6 | **Candles facing in at Canopy.** "The Whisper-Grove books treat this as an old custom; Vol VII (b) reads as if it began after 416." | "Vol VII and VII (b): Canopy is old and has not been held since 416. Every Grove book that keeps it is dated 416 or earlier, so all stand." |
| 7 | **Hounds in the Reach before 417.** "Some books have hounds in the Reach from 413; the journal calls the 417 hounds the 'first'. The books read 'first' as the first at the orchard edge." | "The manuscript has every hound between the quarry and the Flume hearing Tobin in the spring of 417, and everyone knowing by then that bells brought them. Hounds in the wider Reach before 417 stand. Tobin's 'first hounds' became the hounds at the orchards, at the turn of 417, and so did The Wool Wall's." |
| 8 | **Veil scribes' names.** "The books use ink and pigment designations that pass from holder to holder (Umber, Bistre, Woad, Orpiment, Ochre, Gall) [PROPOSED]." | "No volume names them, and Sable already fits the pattern. The ink and pigment designations stand [PROPOSED]." |
| 9 | **Calendar names.** "Book One's 'Leaffall' is not in Vol VII. The books avoid it, and Tomas's quest text still says 'August' (see the roadmap's R2)." | "Tomas now says Firstfruit, not August. The game's text uses only Vol VII's months, and Leaffall stays in the manuscript." |
| 10 | **Pre-Unnaming names in the Spectral Woods.** "Vol II says 'no living names'. The Grove books read that as describing the Hollows after 416, and give the townsfolk plain names." | "Vol II's 'no living names' stands. The Grove books name only people before 416 (the manuscript names them too, and the Ledger holds those names). The Moot-house's shelver is unnamed." |
| 11 | **The Eight Keepers.** "The fable comes close to the Compact; cut the Owl's line 'We were told that somebody would' if it says too much." | "Too easy: the Owl's promise pointed straight at the Compact, so 'We were told that somebody would' is cut. The fable keeps its Sparrow." (It is replaced by "Nobody answered. Nobody knew, and it is rude to make a thing up for a sparrow.") |
| 12 | **Hints at the harvest.** "The midwife's day-book (Office clerks asking for 'the voice' of each child) and the Grove histories (the Canopy keeps the dead) stay at suspicion. Please confirm they are within what the player may suspect." | "They stay. The midwife's clerks ask for 'the voice' and do not say why. 'The Canopy keeps the dead' is a Grove saying, and Vol V and Tobin's own pages already say it. The older lore book bk_veil said more than that, and has been softened." |
| 13 | **New lives for canon minor figures.** "Tomas as a former 'reeds-miner', and his mare Dumpling. Orrin Pale as sexton in 412. Garra Thorne writing a schoolbook. Ennis Crowle, Dorran Sedge and Absalom Rudd as authors. All are marked [PROPOSED]." | "All fine." |

**Rulings attached to the thirteen:**
- **The Greenroot Cantor.** "Also fixed: two Cantors called Ilse. Ilse Brennan is canon (Book Four), so the Greenroot Cantor (Phase 9, [PROPOSED]) is now Mabyn Verrow."
- **bk_veil, "softened at your call".** The lore book "The Folding of the Silent Veil" (upgrades.js) "used to state the harvest as fact: names removed from living debtors to make orderly workers, at 741 Hz, with memories surrendered as bond. It is now a dry Office memorandum, dated c. 300 SR by the Series Bible's timeline. The Veil is folded into the Synod as the Office of the Catalogue; its House stays open under the Office's seal. The Registrar-General finds the order's ways of recording a name 'have uses beyond the keeping of the dead'. The objecting scribes are sent to field ledgers and 'not to be told what the ledgers are for'. That is a suspicion, not an answer." The commits are `7ccd72e` and `dd2c3bf` ("The Veil memorandum is dated c. 300 SR (Series Bible)").
  - Old text: "a name removed from a living debtor produces an orderly worker. Eight seconds of contact at 741 Hz suffices… each surrendered three personal memories as bond."
  - New text: "The Order of the Silent Veil is folded into the Office as of Thawmonth. Its House in the Woods is to be surveyed, and its vellum carried to the Organum archive. The Registrar-General has taken a close interest in the order's methods of recording a name, and is satisfied that they have uses beyond the keeping of the dead. The scribes who objected have been reassigned to field ledgers in the Woods. They are not to be told what the ledgers are for. Their objections are filed."
- **The cross-check's standards before the rulings** (`636c236`). About 40 small fixes made these the norm:
  - name collisions between books removed;
  - the dates of the bells, the lease and the Hedge;
  - the morning the orchards went;
  - mild Reach winters;
  - the tongue-stone custom dates from 417;
  - drift-glass lifts skyships (later widened by r5);
  - the day-book's scribes take ink names, Ochre and Gall;
  - Pennick's annotated rigging handbook moved aboard the Kestrel.
- **The serial's own rulings** (`37f20cd`, the Review "Resolution, 27 October 2026"). The ruling that settles most findings: "The serial is now a Cantabile broadsheet tale of the Caldera."
  - Renames: Matthis Kessler for Jory Moor, Wendel for Aldren, Justiciar R. for Varek, Bastien Corvo for Iven Pell.
  - Terms: sovereigns, not silver; docket, not dispatch; the lower works, not the eastern works; the Rim road.
  - Price: 1 sovereign a part.
  - "The Cantor's lending shelf", removed when the Rite seals Lyra's house.
  - Hetty's capitals; prefaces; a broadsheet sprite.
  - Finding 5's echo kept "as a deliberate echo". Update of 29 Oct: "the talking crates are in the Drift-Kestrel's hold, as in the book".
- **Owner notes, "Weaker books (for a later pass)", not rulings:**
  - the two lamplighter books share a device;
  - Fairweather and A First History read like tours;
  - the Little Catechism is mostly a list;
  - the Grove books blur.

  ROADMAP R8 keeps "A pass revises the weaker books" open.

## Part IV — The release road and the canon pass, 25 to 29 October 2026

### Phase by phase

#### Phase 23: the cast (25 Oct; commits 1649039, 13e6220)
Character creation gained a lore panel, "The world in 419 SR", with eight short entries a newcomer is expected to know: the Reach, the Fracture, the Octave, the Narrow, Aether-Glass, the Iron Synod, the Office of the Catalogue and the Free Captains. Each of the six callings (Dissonant Blade, Cantor, Corsair, Archivist of the Veil, Flambeau Penitent, Quarry-Hand) got a Biography and a Summary. The six invented face archetypes (The Hooded Return, The Braided Vow, The Old Salt, The Veil-Sworn, The Iron-Collared, The Frost-Marked) were then replaced by six Book One characters as playable likenesses: Kaelen Vance, Lyra Ashgrove, Captain Vane, Sable, Anselm Rooke and Mags Tennant. Each has a history, what they carry out of the gate, a six-fact dossier (Years, Born, Trade, Mark, Ties, Wants) and a line in their own voice. Several dossier facts contradict the Series Bible (ages, Mags's rank, Rooke's history, Sable's oath; see "Contradictions still standing").

#### R1: the release blockers (26–27 Oct; commit dd2c3bf, PR #64; recorded in the Phase 27 text record)
R1 fixed story-breaking routes and broken promises rather than adding lore. The Cooper's Row house is now "left behind in Oakhaven" once Part Three begins, and a warning at the shaft ("This is the Rite. Oakhaven will not be the same after tonight") lists the threads still open. The six Sheep-Walk and Hanger quests now close with logbook lines when the Reach is left, naming Tamar Farrant's ewes, the half-scoured Chalk Ram, Absalom Rudd's fold-fires, the Woolwight and Kit Farrant. The Red Cloak can now end without Sable, by sitting with the Aegis while it chimes the alto line. The lore book "The Folding of the Silent Veil" is dated c. 300 SR, as in the Series Bible, and only hints at the Catalogue's use of names.

#### R2: the canon sweep (27 Oct; commits d73de90, 052a6a6, 7ccd72e, 6e03ed0; PR #63, #65)
R2 matched the game to Book One and the Series Bible line by line, and applied the owner's rulings on the library's thirteen questions. Tobin was taken in the spring of 417 at the Pellow farm, before the Hedge; the bells went in 417 for the bore's teeth; the Hedge went in that summer; the Greenroot Cantor became Mabyn Verrow. The Boar & Dulcimer now keeps its own rules (silent applause; Garrow Finch's dice moved to the cellar), the Legionnaires die saying invented names, the Cloister's name-tapes follow ch. 20, the Mausoleum is the House of the Silent Veil with four Veil scribes in its tombs, the quarry lease is Aldric's (414, witnessed by Mossbrook), and Tobin's lyre lies under a cairn by the old stile with his seven reed-pipes in Lyra's house. Orrin Pale became Oakhaven's sexton and took over Ignatius's stock. Near-collisions were renamed: Tobit Dree, Bethan Marl, Aveline Sorrel, Barnaby Haft, and the culvert twins Ned and Wyn.

#### R3: the Narrow comes home (27 Oct; commits 95ed37b, 0fd82c2; PR #67)
R3 made Book One's rule, "too loud brings the hounds, too still brings the Hush", into law and systems. Inside the Hedge, witnesses answer loudness with the ward-sign, and a second breach is "a breach of the Narrow", fined. Hush-lamps keep the Hush off, and a hush-whistle can be staked in the ground to pipe as a lamp for a minute. Songs are learned from people: Lyra (the Root note), Bettany (the tending breath, for eighteen), Old Tam (The Miller's Daughter, a new loud lure song) and Tobin's reed-pipes (the falling fifth). The Listening Hour came to Oakhaven with Godwin Pettigrew the lamplighter, night-hummers at three crossings and the dead-hour call "all's well, all's quiet, keep to the Narrow". It also added hound packs with a leader, the canon combat rules (fear is loud, Quiets go to lanterns, a burst hound leaves a dead patch), and the Concordance as unseen faction standing.

#### R4: the thin places (27 Oct; commits d3b92b1, c02e937; PR #68)
R4 filled empty maps with people, things to use and reasons to be there. Oakhaven's baker's (Agnes Tull) and chandler's (Ambrose Vettle) opened, and the town gained life by the clock: the iron-oaks chiming in wind, a sapling through the cobbles each morning, the baker's thumping at night, the sexton salting the chapel paths at dawn, a daily tanker-wagon under Legionnaire escort, and dogs howling at noon. The Watch Downs got Abel Sedge the turf-cutter and a dew-pond; Wethercombe's farmhouse (Mother Hurst), barn (Jem Cotter) and shut-up cottage opened, and the Bellwether a room and a slate of tabs. The Flume Road got Ennis Crowle the beekeeper (canon in Vol V), the tannery two hands (Sim and Moll Fletch) and a barred back door to the Hollow, and the Low Rings and Stillwater Landing a punt-poler, a salvager, a smuggler and a broadsheet-seller.

#### R5a: the Hollow and the Carders' house (28 Oct; commit 9a9cd1a; PR #70)
R5a built the Hollow, Book One's burl-sick lanes behind the tannery: thirty-one households and eighty-four souls behind barred, humming doors, with sap-marks, a well-head and Rooke's chained ward-book. Lissa Merrow sits on the red-shutter step with her tin cup, and on Rootday mornings the Grafters' felt-wheeled wagon comes in with a brazier, three masked Grafters and a Legionnaire. The quest "The List" [PROPOSED] lets you hum the tending note through the two pencilled doors (the Pells and the Ashbys) and write "Recheck next month" against them. The Carders' house behind the blue door opened, with Lyra's chalk note and a siphoned root-cellar that teaches the Hush and the hush-mites, and Nell's quest "Keep him out of the damp" [PROPOSED]. The canon pass later added Hob Wendle on his step here.

#### R5b: the Moot-house gallery and cellar, the Bell Yard and Ashlane (28 Oct; commits 865a07a, 6d0b217; PR #72)
R5b opened the Moot-house gallery in Whisper-Grove, where Sable first saw a proctor, and her desk in the muniment store with the sum 74,611 names at twenty a night. It also opened the cellar with Corwin Aye's barrel, which has not leaked in nineteen years. An archway off the Sunken Court leads to the Bell Yard and Ashlane: swept streets, three years of dead flowers in a window-box, dry lamps, carts with their shafts up, Hollows at their tasks and proctors walking. Halder Wick's house (the table for four) and Ivo Renn's (two bowls) are open. The quest "The Bell Yard Roll" [PROPOSED] puts seven households in your ledger from their name-boards, and "Do not comfort yourself in the margin" takes you to the twice-taken man and Sable's statement of the Veil's three rules (ch. 14).

#### Phases 32 to 40: the canon pass (28 Oct; commits ad72340, f4db2f7, d9fc079, c9e83b8, 12ae249, e25b999, 4c17766, f6e24bb, 90e2680, 3c9b261)
The owner ruled: *"Bettis Renn should be Ivo's wife just as in the book. Match any inconsistency to match the book."* Phase 32 made the text-level fixes from the brainstorm's §5: Bettis Renn, Form 9, Tam's Essa, the Hollow's place, each bell's own last words, Aldric's count, the herald's hands, twenty-six souls, Emory Wain, the Madder birth on Nullday, Kaelen's coil, and the hush-gift child renamed Linnet. Phases 33 to 40 made the structural changes, one each:
- the talking crates are in the Kestrel's hold;
- Hob Wendle is the Hollow's field-hand who dies aboard, and the tanner becomes Abner Fenning [PROPOSED];
- the Office cutter is a courier hunting the Kestrel's own 432 cores;
- the nine days run in order (Davin on Hearthday, Pim's lure on Massday, the Church on Dawnday);
- Halder Wick's table goes unanswered;
- the Wearer is met once;
- the coda is the ring of lamps;
- Sable stays in Whisper-Grove, and the scribe in the Reach's mist is Umber [PROPOSED].

## Part V — Contradictions still standing

Places where the game, the library, a bible volume or the books disagree, and nothing has settled it yet. The manuscript wins over the bible, and the game follows the book; each item says which side would have to change.

### Found in Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

These are sorted roughly by weight. Each needs sleeves' ruling before the bible absorbs the game text.

1. **How Tobin died.** P6 Tobin page 12 (autumn 417, after the Hedge) has him choosing "the south road and the culvert" and putting his blade down "under the oaks", with "The Madder child was warm this morning". B1 (ch1 backstory) has him taken "in the spring, at the Pellow farm, out past where the Hedge runs now". There he sang the Pellows' youngest back from a cooling fever, "until the hounds came and there was nothing left of him but a lyre by a stile". P9's Lyra ("a child in the lane went still") fits neither exactly.
2. **The Hedge's planting season.** P6 Tobin page 11 says late summer 417. P6's dream "Three Deep" says "It is spring". B1 says "planted two summers ago", which is summer. R quotes Book Four: "the autumn of 417". Four versions across three sources.
3. **Lyra's age and training.** P6 Tobin pages 1, 2 and 6 show Lyra as a novice learning "the long cane first" in 412 and unable to manage "a first fifth" in 415. SB makes her 33 in 419 (26 in 412) and already the trained Cantor. V2 and V5 have her trained at the Reedside Conservatory by Ilse Brennan.
4. **The reeds died in the Cascade, but the game grows them.** SB and B1 say "every reed in the Reach turned in an hour to something like spun sugar" on the day the Spire broke (412). V5 calls the pitch-reed "the last sugar-glass wind-reed". Against that, P6 page 7 (416) has a First Reed that "spoke perfectly well", and P9 has fresh wind-reed beds by the Flume in 419. V7 ("Reedmonth: the wind-reeds come up") and V5 (Dorran Sedge's reed-beds) support regrowth, so the bible needs one line saying green wind-reed still grows yearly and only the old orchard reeds died.
5. **Who took the bells, and when.** P6 page 3: the Choir-Church took them in Ashfall 412 "for the Pure Note". B1: "the Synod had requisitioned every bell in the Reach in the first year of the quarry. The bore's teeth needed bronze." P12's Bell-Bronze Glaive comes from "a chapel bell the Synod did not take", against B1's "every bell".
6. **The quarry lease.** P6 page 5: read in the Greenhall in Harvestmonth 414, bread for nine years, one dissenting vote. B1 (Aldric): signed in the spring after the Cascade famine winter, when "the Consortium came up the Greenroot road with forty wagons of grain and a paper." V1 and V2 (superseded) say the Moot sold the rights in 404.
7. **The Tanner twins and "Joss".** P6 names the twins Joss and Wyn and puts their coats under the Tannery culvert, with Hob Wendle grieving. P12 names them "Joss Wendle, Wyn Wendle". In B1 the Tanner twins were quarry-platform workers ("the Tanner boys") who "died of slippage … in the spring", and Lyra sang them "into the ground side by side". In B1 Joss is Nell Carder's husband, killed by hounds two winters ago, and SB's audit lists "Joss Carder" among the dead.
8. **Hob Wendle's trade.** B1: "a field-hand of forty". P6 letter: "Wendle, tanner." P12 has him selling a lute "strung with his own gut".
9. **Old Tam's nine winters.** P9: Tam kept the night watch on the Flume bridge with Joram "nine winters". B1: Tam "had been a night-hummer since the first autumn of the Hedge [417], and a basket-maker for sixty years before that".
10. **When the Madders' girl was born.** P9: "on Massday". B1 ch1: the birth is in the first chapter, which falls on Day 1, a Nullday (SB §Audit).
11. **Greenroot.** P9: seven houses, thirty-one souls, all gone by the end of 417, with "nobody has gone down [the road] since". V2: Greenroot Hamlet has 400 souls, a mill, a chapel with a silent reed, and an overgrowth ward. V5: Lyra's team "is set down at Greenroot Hamlet" on 17 Frostmonth 419. B1: Kaelen plans "Greenroot, then the Salt-Chime Route", a mail-cart comes "up from the Greenroot road" in 419, and the Church arrives by it. SB says Greenroot lies "downriver", while R and P9 put it "three miles east", which may or may not agree.
12. **Ada Pellow's home.** P9: "seventy years in one street", on Cooper's Row. B1: "the Pellow farm, out past where the Hedge runs now" (Mistress Pellow). This is reconcilable if the farm is family land, but it should be stated.
13. **Who is inside the Aegis.** P9 follows B1 ch17: the core is WG-41,005, a boy. SB §The Twelve and V5 make the Aegis Wren Halloway, a ten-year-old girl alto, entry 41,112.
14. **The game's Wren.** P6's epilogue has a living girl called Wren (saved or taken in the north-west of the Deep Woods). This collides with Wren Halloway (SB, V3b, V5), who has been Unnamed since 416. V3b already renamed a Vol IV character because "Wren Halloway exists".
15. **Sable's fragments.** P6's sable_returned letter says she lost the scriptorium smell and keeps her mother's hands and her name. V3b: at the end of Book Two she has only the scriptorium smell left; her mother's hands "went on Wren" and her name "went at the Malting".
16. **Sable in Part One, and the first name returned.** P6 has Sable met outside the Hedge in Part One and returning Hew Callan's name at the quarry. B1 first meets her in Whisper-Grove (Part Three), and SB says Bettis Renn is "the first person ever given a name back".
17. **Bettis Renn's sex.** P9 has Sable say she paid "one thing, for a man on a bench". B1 makes Bettis Renn a woman: "Wife of Ivo Renn, cooper, of Ashlane." P6's Bettis letter says "You sat with me on the bench".
18. **Lyra's voice at the end of Book One.** P6's applied fix says the coda "cost her what was left of her voice. She talks in chalk now." In B1 ch24, "Lyra answers the herald herself", and V7 has "Lyra's voice goes" on 13 Ashfall in Book Two.
19. **Killing the Wearer early.** P6 lets the player kill "whatever was wearing voices at the Hedge" in Part One (wearer_dead). In B1 ch23 Lyra kills the Wearer at the coda, and V5 rules that "the Hedge, not the Wearer, speaks in the voices of the dead".
20. **"It is Thursday."** P6's Halder Wick letter uses a weekday that does not exist; the week is the eight Anchor-days (V6 §0, V7). The same letter still calls his wife "Ada", which Phase 9 renamed Bryony.
21. **What the Oakhaven rite is called.** P6's review fix retitled Gazette No. 36 because "the Rite of Pure Unison is the earlier Sol-Luminary rite". But Gazette No. 31 still "announces a Rite of Pure Unison for Hearthday", No. 36's body heading still has the old title, and V7b §3 (approved) calls the Oakhaven rite "The Rite of Pure Unison". B1 uses "Rite of Pure Unison" only for 411–412.
22. **Where the mooring mast stands.** P6: "on the east side of the square". B1: "above the roofs to the north, where the Consortium's mooring mast stood over the quarry", "a hundred feet above the Oakhaven quarry".
23. **Harrow's "eleven years".** P6: Harrow has kept the North Gate shut at night for eleven years. P12: he has hung the sheaf on the Hedge-gate "eleven years running". But the Hedge and its gate date from 417 (SB, V5), and P6 page 11 (417) introduces "The gate is shut at dusk. Harrow keeps the key."
24. **What moot-curd is.** P12: "soft salted curd in a dock-leaf". V1 §III.1: "a sharp white cheese … aged beside a humming stone and tastes faintly of static."
25. **Salt mutton as the Reach's staple (R, delegated).** R's sheep ruling says "Salt mutton is the Reach's staple (Vol I; Phase 6)". V1 lists salt mutton under Fortis (§III.4 Food: "Root stew, salt mutton, black bread"), and P6's only mutton line is in Brannoch's letter from Fortis. V1's Reach food is cider, hearth bread, river eel and moot-curd. The ruling's conclusion (the flocks were eaten) can stand, but its citation is wrong.
26. **Null-Watch troopers named.** P6's line-talk transcript calls troopers "Tam" and "Orrin". V2's convention: "Null-Watch personnel use rota numbers in service, because names invite the Quiet." V6 uses numbers (94, Two-Eleven, Nineteen). "Orrin" also collides with Orrin Hale (V6).
27. **Vane's crates.** P9: "I carried twelve crates for two years." B1/SB: twelve crates is this one consignment (ch4); his confession is "thirteen hundred crates in two years" (ch13), and 1,342 across eleven consignments (ch16).
28. **How long Kaelen has been in the hut.** P9: "Nobody came to the hut in four years." SB: he has lived there since the Fracture (412), and in B1 ch3 he comes to Lyra's door "for the first time in seven years".
29. **The colour of the glass arm.** A P6 rumour says the glass-bearer's arm is "the colour of lamp-glass, green-gold at the wrist … The Governor's son has the same." Kaelen's arm is "cyan Light-glass" (SB). P14 uses green-gold for Growth root veins.
30. **Where WG-41005's record is.** P9 Sable: "The Organum, third floor, the Hall of Carriage. I know the cabinet." V3b and V6: the third-floor cabinet holds the staff copy-cores; the Concordance, which records every core, is on the second floor.
31. **The postponement comes before the Church arrives.** P12: the Church postpones the Last Sheaf on G.day 4 (Stasisday 10 Harvestmonth). B1 and SB §Audit: the Church arrives in Oakhaven on Day 6, Dawnday, which is G.day 5. This is minor, since a notice could come ahead.
32. **The Last Sheaf cut.** P12 lets the player cut it in secret. V7b §2 says "It was never held in the open. The sheaf … is still standing." V7b allows the cut as a game option, so this is a branch, not an error, but canon is "still standing".
33. **"Ignatius the smith".** P12's Resonance Rod is sold by "Ignatius the smith". The only Ignatius in canon is Ignatius Crane, the Flambeau and Inquisitor. Either the vendor needs another name or the text is wrong.
34. **Mire-drone details.** P9: "the size of a calf … everything near it gets a little heavier". V2: "bovine-sized … 60 Hz drone that induces nausea and despair in a 200m radius". This is minor.
35. **"The Long Octave".** SB uses it as the name of an era ("The Long Octave: eight crowned realms"). P6's torn leaf treats it as a Conservatory pitch table.
36. **Tobin dates by the month.** His journal dates entries "Ashfall", "Frostmonth" and "Harvestmonth". V7 §8: "The Reach dates by what is growing, not by the month, and a Reach character giving a calendar date is a Reach character who has been away." Minor, but it is a voice rule.
37. **The Tarrows' "last cart before the gate was barred".** P13 has the family leaving Oakhaven by cart "three weeks gone", expecting to be "Back by the first frost". R and B1 have Oakhaven shut in behind the Hedge since 417, with no carts going out.
38. **Greenroot road traffic.** P9: "Nobody has gone down it since the summer the third Cantor sent her note." B1: the mail-cart brings a letter "up from the Greenroot road", and the Church and the Consortium's wagons use it. P9 itself also says "The Church came up it."
39. **Name collisions (not errors, but the bible should decide):**
    - Wenna Marl (P13) and Abbess Wenna of the Unrung Bell (SB).
    - Ilse Verrow (P9), Ilse Brennan (V2, V5) and Ilse of the Sky Choir (B1).
    - Hesper Pell (P9), Hesper Sowerby (Annet's mother, B1) and Hesper (Sky Choir, B1; V4).
    - Joss: Joss Carder (B1), Joss the Tanner twin (P6) and Joss Pell (P9).
    - Mags Harker (P6) and Mags Tennant (SB).
    - Nan Fell (P6) and Nan Tarrow (P13).
    - Tam: Old Tam, Tam the Null-Watch trooper (P6) and Tam Sowerby (B1).
    - Garrow Finch (P11) and Ulf Garrow (V5).
    - Dunna Sedge (P12) and Dorran Sedge (V5).
    - The Greenroot Pells, Rowena Pell (V2) and Cosimo Pell (V2).
    - Wick Pell (P9) and Halder Wick (SB).

### Found in Phases 15–25 (B)

1. **Salt mutton: the Rulings misread Vol I.** The Rulings say "Salt mutton is the Reach's staple (Vol I; Phase 6)". In Vol I - The Expanded World Bible.md, "Root stew, salt mutton, black bread" is the **Bastion Fortis** food line (§III.4). The Reach's food line (§III.1) is "Cider, hearth bread, river eel … moot-curd". Phase 22's "the Reach's staple" item text inherits the error. Phase 6 may still support it but was not checked.
2. **"The Chord" is already canon for something else.** Phase 21 #1 rules the night-sky star-dust band "the Chord". Series Bible (canon).md uses "the Chord" for the protagonists' movement throughout: §Part VII — Factions › The Chord ("We are not a unison"), §Game layer: Concordance ("a standing with the Chord") and Book Two ("the Chord arrives…"). Vol I also calls Lyra "Cantor of the Living Chord". Player text naming the sky band would collide with the faction.
3. **Mags Tennant's rank.** The game calls her "Mags Tennant, first mate" (Phase 16; `js/kestrel16.js:270`, `js/data.js:958`). Book One ch. 4 has "six feet and some inches of Rust Coast **bosun**", and Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures has "the deaf bosun".
4. **Phase 16 Question 2 rests on a false premise.** It says Mags and Pennick "are Book Two characters in canon". Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures lists both as **Book One supporting cast** aboard the Drift-Kestrel, and Book One has them aboard (for example "Pennick was still asleep on the lift-tanks"). There is no conflict in having them in Book One.
5. **Pennick's character.** Series Bible says "Pennick, a young engineer who talks to the lift-tanks". Phase 16 makes him a pessimist welder, and Mags says "Tobiah talks to the tanks. Pennick talks to nobody and the tanks listen", which reverses the canon tic onto Tobiah.
6. **Kaelen's mother is alive in a held letter.** Phase 16's letter from "Mam" says she keeps his chair, and Kaelen says she "has never dusted anything in her life". Book One ch. 5 recalls "his mother … the winter he was twelve, in the last week of the fever, lying in the big bed in the Governor's house … humming the same five notes", which strongly implies she died. The line is still in `js/heist16.js:220`.
7. **Pim's father is alive in a held letter.** Phase 16's letter is from "Da" at the Landing ("Do as Nell says"). Book One ch. 1 says Nell Carder "had been widowed two winters ago, when her husband Joss saw a pack of hounds", and "He's all I've got left of Joss." The line is still in `js/heist16.js:241`.
8. **Factor Vosk's sex.** Ines Tarn: "The safe is Vosk's. **He** had it made with three tumblers" (`js/heist16.js:344`). Canon has Factor **Adela** Vosk, "her green lenses" (Book One chs 2, 7 and 10; Series Bible §Antagonists and Key Figures).
9. **Hob Wendle's trade.** Phase 15 and 16 make him a tanner ("Hob Wendle, tanner"; "I have buried two apprentices"). Book One ch. 3 has "Hob Wendle … a field-hand of forty, big-shouldered and sheepish, whose roots had come out through the soles of his feet". Canon's tanners are the "Tanner cousins", a family name.
10. **The quarry lease, then and now.**
    - Phase 16 had Mossbrook signing in 404.
    - Release Readiness flagged that canon has Aldric signing, and the R2 sweep changed the document to "Aldric Vance, Governor, 414 … witnessed: Alder Mossbrook".
    - Mossbrook's own dialogue still says "I signed a lease. In four hundred and four" (`js/heist16.js:331`).
    - The Phase 16 rumour line also says "A lease was read aloud at the moot, in four hundred and four, in the Reeve's own hand"; it was not re-checked in code.
    - Vol I §III.1 ("the Moot sold the quarry rights in 404") and Vol II §People You Can Use Today (Mossbrook "Signed the quarry lease in 404") are now contradicted by the game. The bible itself is split: Series Bible says Aldric "sold the Root".
11. **The Silencing date and the 409 depot.** Vol I §2.2 and §III.7 still date the Silencing of Memory to **401**, with "Relief convoys stopped in 409 because the workers could not continue". Series Bible §Part V and Vol V §0 date the Silencing to **416**. Phase 16 patched this with a 409 *flood*-relief depot that the Synod took over after 416. Vol I was never corrected, and the patch was never ruled.
12. **Where the Low Rings are.** Vol II §Settlements makes the Low Rings one of **Oakhaven's four quarters**, with Reedside, the Ladder and Quarry Gate, which puts them inside the Hedge. Phase 16 reaches them by gangway from Stillwater Landing, outside the Hedge, on a route that stays open until the Greening.
13. **Lantern Row house numbers.** The station ledger puts the Fell household at "No. 11". M. Orly's last page says "Bettrys at No. 9 asked me where Wren's cloak was". In the register Bettrys is a Fell.
14. **The lunula is gold or bronze.** Phase 22b's summary lists "grave goods (a torc, amber, a gold lunula)". The item is "Bronze Lunula — A crescent collar of beaten bronze".
15. **Teague is alive after his canon death.** The Cantabile extra (7 Ashfall 419) has "Teague, Harbour office" keeping the book (`js/cantabile17.js:246`). Series Bible §Part One · The Rust Coast (ch. 1) and §Canon added in Book Two record "**Dead:** Colm Teague, nineteen, Harbour crew, taken by the quiet" on **4 Ashfall 419**.
16. **Phase 17's own canon list is wrong in places.**
    - It lists "Marrin Tace and Jory's vox-bottle (J. TACE…)", "the Office's receiving clerk and his six-sovereign offer" and "slack water … as the moment dues are paid" as canon. No bible volume or book has Tace, the six-sovereign clerk or slack-water dues. Vol IV §Hooks has only an unnamed fourth-landing widow.
    - Its own Question 5 lists Marrin Tace and the slack-key dues as inventions.
    - It claims Article the Twenty-first as its own invention, but Vol IV already has it, as [PROPOSED] with identical wording.
17. **Article the Ninth: two versions inside the bible.** Vol IV §The Free Captains and the Articles marks as [PROPOSED] "The Ninth — a ship that takes aboard a Rust Coast crew takes aboard their dead". Series Bible §Canon added in Book Two has the canon Ninth: a vessel under no hand belongs to the Harbour. The game uses the Series Bible version, so Vol IV's proposal is dead and should be struck.
18. **Who owns the Cantabile counting-house.** Vol IV §The town as written: "the counting-house (the Organum's)". Series Bible: "a new Flambeau counting-house". The game: "the oldest building in Port Cantabile, built by the Synod's tax-farmers … given to the Church", with the Flambeaux in it "since the spring".
19. **How long the voyage to Cantabile took.** Phase 17's notes say the Kestrel reached Port Cantabile on "4 Ashfall, twenty days out". Series Bible says Book Two opens "eleven days after the *Kestrel* left Oakhaven", and ch. 2 "Eleven days to come four hundred miles".
20. **The Long Night and "three hundred years".** The extra says "Nobody has gone down in three hundred years" and is set "after Book One" on a Longest Night with the year unsaid. Vol V §The Cantors' Secret and §Ilse Brennan says that on 21 Frostmonth 419 Ilse Brennan stayed in the gallery and the Root "sang the lullaby to the end for the first time in three hundred years". Lyra also went down. Any year from 419 on contradicts the line. In 419 itself, the Longest Night is also Vaelen's Grand Unison (Vol V §0).
21. **The Hedge's planting season and Tobin's taking.** The Rulings cite Book Four: "planted in the autumn of 417". Phase 17 first had Tobin living inside the Hedge "in the autumn it was planted". The owner's later ruling (ROADMAP.md §R2, PR #63) says Tobin was taken in **spring 417** at the Pellow farm before the Hedge, and the Hedge went in "the **summer** of 417 ('two summers ago')". The prologue line was rewritten ("Your father never stood inside it"), but the Rulings document's "autumn of 417" now conflicts with the owner's ruling. That ruling's own rule is that the manuscript wins over in-game books, but Book Four is also a manuscript.
22. **Mutton in a starving town.**
    - Release Readiness lists "Mutton is on every menu in a starving town whose flocks were eaten" as a canon contradiction.
    - ROADMAP records "sheep and mutton on every menu" as the **owner's own request** for Phase 22.
    - The R2 fix keeps Oakhaven to "dear salt mutton off Vane's boat".
    - The Phase 22 doc's Hetty line ("Supper's mutton, because supper is always mutton") is superseded in code by "Salt mutton, off Captain Vane's boat, and dear. The flocks went two winters ago" (`js/sheepwalk22.js:221`). The doc was not updated.
23. **The Wearer is beaten at the Hedge.** The Borrowed Collar trophy is granted for beating "the thing that wore the villagers' voices at the Hedge" (`wearer_dead` without `wearer_coda_dead`). The GDD calls the Wearer "unkillable in Book One". Series Bible §Part Four — Coda has the Wearer burst only in ch. 23, the coda. The killable Hedge fight is earlier game content, not from this scope.
24. **Which lift-tank was holed.** Book One ch. 21: the cutter's shot "clipped the **number-three** lift-tank". Phase 16 has "number-two" as the tank closed with weaverling silk and Tobiah's patch, and number three is the "new" tank built from the cutter's.
25. **Wren Fell and Wren Halloway.** Phase 16 proposes Wren Fell, nine, as the Wren of the Hollow loop "Wren, the cloak". Canon's Wren is **Wren Halloway**, a ten-year-old Cloister alto and the Aegis's core (Series Bible §The Aegis Sentinel), and the Cloister has "eleven small cloaks on iron pegs" (Series Bible §Part Three, ch. 17). The loop more naturally points at her. This is a possible collision, not a proven contradiction.
26. **Near-collisions of names introduced in this scope.** The ROADMAP R2 sweep renamed four (Pellam Dree to Tobit Dree, Orrin Haft to Barnaby Haft, Annet Sorrel to Aveline Sorrel, and Wenna Marl to Bethan Marl, the last from earlier content). These remain:

    | Name in this scope | Collides with |
    |---|---|
    | **Anselm** Fenn | Surgeon-Grafter **Anselm** Rooke |
    | Anselm **Fenn**, Jory **Fenn** | canon Maddock and Elsie **Fenn** |
    | Night-Reckoner **Dray** | canon Fennick **Dray** (Vol V); Ben **Dray** (the Cloister tape, R2) |
    | Dessa, Aster and Tansy **Quill** | canon **Jory Quill**, the Static Remnant |
    | Jory Mapp, Jory Fenn, Jory Tace | **Jory Quill** |
    | MARREN on a pigeonhole; Ruth Marren | Ilias **Marren**, the Tide-Weaver |
    | Oswin **Marl**, **Marl** Dunning | each other |
    | **Orrin** Pale | **Orrin** Hale (Vol VI) |
    | **Tamsin** Marr | **Tamsin** Reed |
    | Gerrit **Hale** | the canon Hales |
    | Pol **Wick** | Halder **Wick** |

27. **The Phase 22 doc does not match the code.** The fantasy flora, the ordinary flora strings, the trap labels and the six quest-closing logbook lines are in `js/sheepwalk22.js` and `js/hanger22.js` but missing from the Phase 22 doc. The closing lines are, for example, "We left the Reach before all of Tamar Farrant's ewes were home…", "The Chalk Ram above Wethercombe is only half scoured. Nobody will finish him now.", "Kit Farrant was still out when we left the Reach. I did not find him in time." The doc presents its Hetty line as current.
28. **Release Readiness cites sources that were not found.** It cites as canon "the Boar's board says NO SINGING. NO TOASTS. NO DICE" with Hetty's "not since Davin", and "Hew Callan … 'I was there. I counted every stroke.'" Neither was found in any bible volume or in the Book One, Two, Three or Four manuscripts. They may be earlier game text.
