### Found in Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

1. **The Carders' house is in the wrong place.** The game puts the blue door on the north row, between the Greenhall and the granary. MS ch. 6, l. 2031 has it "halfway down the lane behind the tannery", and ch. 11, l. 3462 has it under bramble two doors from Orla's. (js/hollow30.js; PLAYTEST R5a.)
2. **The Grafters' timing and kit.** The game sends the wagon every Rootday morning; MS ch. 1, l. 328 has them come "on the second Rootday of every month". The game's masks have no mouth and its aprons go over Office grey. In ch. 3 (ll. 941–945) the masks are stiff leather with glass eyepieces and wire over the mouth, worn over white leather aprons and white smocks, and the felt is on the harness and the hooves are booted, not the wheels.
3. **Phase 23's dossiers against the Series Bible:**
   - Kaelen "About thirty-six" (the bible: 34), and an arm "of Aether-Glass" (the bible and MS: Light-glass).
   - Lyra "About thirty-six" (33).
   - Vane "About forty-five" (41), and the Kestrel bought "the year after" 414 (the bible: 417).
   - Sable "About fifty" (the bible: "age unknown; she has forgotten"); her walking out "when the Synod absorbed the order" (c. 300 SR, and Vol III (b) swears her in in 416); and the satchel of names (the book: the Bound Ledger, and she stays in Whisper-Grove).
   - Rooke "Past sixty", raised off the tanners' lane, twenty years of cutting, a mother behind the tannery (MS ch. 3, l. 951: "fifty or near it", from the Organum four years ago).
   - Mags "First mate" (the bible and MS: bosun; "first mate" is Vesper's role).
4. **The Unnaming "in one night".** The Part Three card (re-emitted in p40-umber.tsv) and data.js say seventy-four thousand people forgot themselves "in one night in 416". The manuscript has people taken over at least four days (Halder on the second day, the Renns on the fourth) and Sable writing the names "in eleven days" (ch. 14, l. 4127). Only Vol I (superseded, dated 401) says "overnight".
5. **Two Linnets.** The canon pass named the hush-gift child Linnet. The game already had Linnet Tarrow, eight, whose counting-beads are a reward in the Tarrows' house quest (house13.js, Phase 13). PLAYTEST asks whether the name sits right but does not mention the clash.
6. **Two (or three) Umbers.** The new Umber [PROPOSED] is Sable's junior, alive in 419. The Phase 26 library already has a Veil scribe styled Umber: the author of "What Is Written: A Short Account of the Silent Veil, for Novices" (410 SR, fifty-one years in the designation), and the copyist of "The Rule of the House" (c. 290 SR). Either designations pass down, which no ruling says, or one should be renamed.
7. **"The last Veil-sworn scribe".** The Series Bible §Sable calls Sable "the last Veil-sworn scribe". Umber is "a runaway of the Veil", Sable's junior, who says Sable is "the last archivist of the Silent Veil". Whether Umber was ever sworn is unsaid.
8. **Essa at the sluice.** The canon pass moved Old Tam's Essa to the pox, forty years ago. The library chronicle "The Fires on the Ground" (Orrin Pale, 413 SR) still buries "Essa Brindle, who went into the Flume at the sluice-gate". This is the old game version of Tam's wife, left behind in a book.
9. **The Fenning twins are still a culvert mystery.** R2 fixed the name collision and gave the book's Tanner twins their own rumour, but the game still has a second pair of tanner's twins lost in the culvert. The book has no such pair, and Pim's culvert lure is the book's only culvert disappearance.
10. **Mossbrook and the lease date.** Vol II has Reeve Mossbrook sign the quarry lease in 404. The game has Aldric sign it in 414, witnessed by Mossbrook. Vol II should be marked superseded on this point, or the bible should record 414.
11. **The Low Rings' location.** Vol II makes the Low Rings Oakhaven's own flooded lower quarter. The game places them beside Stillwater Landing.
12. **"The whole of it is the Organum."** Phase 23's lore panel says the eight Spires "together... are the Octave, and the whole of it is the Organum". The Series Bible §The Organum makes the Organum the Synod's capital city, "built over the place where the eight Anchor-roots meet".
13. **"Tempest Coast".** R2's "On the Eight Anchors" puts Velocity "on the Tempest Coast". The bible calls it the Tempest Archipelago.
14. **The Requiem's "three hundred years".** The coda's Requiem bursts into "three hundred years of funeral offices". Vol V gives the House eleven hundred years.
15. **The Cloister cloaks.** The game has "undyed grey wool" and the red cloak on "the peg nearest the alcove". MS ch. 17, l. 4684 has the cloaks "mostly brown", and ch. 20, l. 5322 has the red cloak hanging from the Aegis's hand. The MS is itself split: ch. 17 counts eleven cloaks, ch. 20 ten.
16. **Hush-mites.** The name ties the mites to the Hush, but the game calls them "a Cacophony swarm". Vol IV §1.7 rules that the Hush is not the Cacophony.
17. **Sable's Malting fragment in the bible.** Vol III (b) says "her true name went at the Malting" and her mother's hands went on Wren. MS ch. 18 says that after the Malting her name and the scriptorium were "Still there. Both still there", and the lost one "had been warm and... had had hands". The game follows the MS ("one thing... I could not name it").
18. **Lissa's hands.** In the game Lissa says "It is only in my arms, Rooke says, up to here", with no mention that her hands are gone. The Series Bible and MS ch. 1, l. 384 and ch. 3, l. 1079 have the Grafters take her hands.
19. **The sapling's place.** The game's sapling comes up on the corner by Cooper's Row, wrist-thick. The MS's is a knee-high young oak by the well (ch. 1, l. 304). This is small, but it is a canon detail.
20. **The ROADMAP is behind the game.** ROADMAP R9 still lists the crates, Hob and the three Wearer fights as open contradictions, and R9's open question on Sable is unticked. PLAYTEST.md marks all eight structural items done.
21. **Near-collisions introduced in this range:**
   - Agnes Tull vs Garret Tull.
   - Ambrose Vettle vs Ambrose of Registry Square (Vol VI).
   - Orrin Pale vs Orrin Hale (Vol III, Vol VI).
   - Ysolde Marrow vs Ysolde Carrow; and the pump-man Marrow on the beam.
   - Corvin Ash vs Corwin Aye and Severin Ashe.
   - Edda Quill vs Jory Quill and Edda Varn.
   - The Ashbys vs Fenella Ashby (Vol VI).
   - The Pells vs Rowena Pell (Vol II).
   - Abel Sedge vs Dorran Sedge (Vol V).
   - Gil Wade vs Gil Tarrow (in game).
   - Tobit Dree vs Tobin, Tobiah, and the tape BEN DRAY.
   - Mags Harker vs Mags Tennant.
   - Bosun Teller vs Maud Teller (Vol VI).
   - Pol Wick and Gerrit Hale reuse canon surnames.

### Found in The Library of the Reach (C)

Between the books and the bible, the manuscript, the rulings or the game. Most serious first.

1. **There are two Umbers.**
   - The library has Umber as an elderly scribe of the House (lib_a_what_is_written, 410), who had held the designation 51 years and volunteered for the Grove register. An earlier Umber copied the Rule c. 290 (lib_e_veil_rule).
   - Commit `3c9b261` (28 Oct, after the library) makes **Umber** the game's runaway in the mist outside the Hedge in 419 (js/data.js): "Sable's junior", carrying "the Loose Leaves: the Veil's day-pages, which I carried out of the scriptorium under my coat".
   - The library's designation-passing rule (r8, lib_g_bistre_commonplace) can only reconcile them if the 410 Umber died or laid down the pen between 410 and 416, and a young scribe received the designation. Nothing says so.
   - The "day-pages" carried out also sit oddly beside the Duty-Scribe's Day-Book, which "stays here. It is not a record. Nobody will take it."
   - The owner should rule on one Umber or two.
2. **The Bellwether Book against r3.** G. Thrale's entry is dated "417, back-end. Shut tonight. The wool has gone down to Oakhaven for the Hedge." In this library "back-end" means autumn (Rudd: "WHEN THE SORREL REDDENS, which is the back-end"). r3 puts the planting in the summer of 417, and The Wool Wall has the Bellwether shuttering during the summer shearing. The entry was not updated with the r3 changes.
3. **The Narrow Larder:** "The hens went quiet first, the autumn the Hedge went in." This reads against r3's summer, though it can be read as "the autumn of the Hedge year". It was left unchanged.
4. **Stale notes fields** (not shown to the player, but they will mislead whoever reads the sources):
   - lib_e_oakhaven_prospectus notes still say "the Hedge planted in autumn 417 (Phase 20-21 Rulings)".
   - lib_g_orpiment_meditations notes cite "lib_h_daybook (Hesketh Calder's day-book…)", but the day-book's scribe is now Ochre.
   - lib_d_telling_the_bees notes suggest "Lyra's lending shelf", which is now "the Cantor's lending shelf" (removed at the Rite).
5. **Fourteen desks, sixteen scribes.** The Duty-Scribe's Day-Book has "Fourteen at the desks" on its first day and "**Sixteen** at the desks" on its second. The manuscript (ch.14, l.4135 and l.5052) and What Is Written give **fourteen desks**. The built scriptorium room has 8 desks, a layout simplification.
6. **Ilse Brennan's voice.**
   - Her keeper dialogue (libraries26.js, "Her voice") says: "Eleven years ago I sang one note too many for a dying boy. He lived."
   - Vol V §Ilse Brennan (78) and lib_e_off_the_slate say she lost it in 408 "after singing nine nights in the Hollow during the throat-fever".
   - The year agrees (419 − 11 = 408), and so does Vol II's "Debt of Overtone eleven years ago". The circumstance does not: one dying boy against nine nights of the sick. By the owner's rule a volume stands over a game text.
7. **Bells rung after 412.**
   - lib_a_hard_winter says Orrin Pale tolled the town bell flat in the first week of autumn 412, tied off the rope, and "I have not tolled since".
   - js/tobin.js p.3 (Ashfall 412) says "They will ring for Essa on Rootday".
   - Tobin's 417 page (p27-canon) says "Harrow says it was the evening bell that brought them. We have stopped ringing it."
   - These can be different bells (the death-toll against an evening bell), but read together they suggest the town's bells rang until 417 while the sexton says his stopped in 412.
8. **"The first year of the works."**
   - Cyril Tolley (lib_b_letters_from_the_gate) writes "They took all the bells in the first year of the works".
   - The same library dates the works to 415–416 and the bells to 417 (lib_a_greenroot_road, r2: "the first year of the quarry", meaning the bore).
   - A one-word fix would align it ("of the bore" or "of the quarry").
9. **Losses at Oakhaven's gate in 413–414.**
   - Septimus Fallow (lib_e_digest_reach_law) read writs aloud "in '13 and in '14, and both times we lost somebody at the gate that night".
   - r7 lets hounds roam the wider Reach before 417. But The Wool Wall has the 417 orchard hounds as the town's first fright ("the first loud sound any of us had made since the hounds came").
   - The manuscript has Oakhaven learning that bells bring hounds only by 417.
   - Deaths at Oakhaven's own gate in 413–414 press hard on that. The owner may want "the gate" here read as the Hedge-gate after 417, or the years changed.
10. **The Veil memorandum (bk_veil, c. 300 SR) against the library's Veil.**
    - The memo says that at the folding "its vellum [is to be] carried to the Organum archive" and the House "is to be surveyed".
    - In the library the surveyors came first, c. 290 (lib_e_veil_rule's colophon).
    - The House still held its vellum until 416 (Orpiment, the Day-Book, and Vol V: "the Office took the vellum to the Organum archive"). Only "our oldest books" were in the Organum by 410 (What Is Written).
    - This is reconcilable (a partial transfer c. 300) but is not stated anywhere.
11. **"Planted it yourselves in a night".** The Undertow (419) says this, while The Wool Wall, A First History and the manuscript have a season's work in the summer of 417. It is an outside paper's error and is plausible in-world, but it is a fact-level contradiction if the line is taken as reporting.
12. **Two lamplighters on Cooper's Row in the autumn of 418.**
    - Godwin Pettigrew (lib_b_year_of_nights) lights all 212 lamps "from the tannery steps to the top of Cooper's Row" and knows each reed.
    - In the same autumn, Crispin Whitlock (lib_c_lamplighters_note) is the lamplighter who "came down Cooper's Row with his pole as usual".
    - Each lamplighter puts a two-note or third-apart reed into a Cooper's Row-area lamp that season, so the town would have two "lamps that do not keep their single note". The owner notes already flag the shared device; this is the factual clash under it.
13. **Canopy "not celebrated since 401" (Vol I §4.5, and Vol I §1.9's "archived before 401").** Vol VII §4 says "Not celebrated since 416", and the Grove books keep it to 415. r6 sides with Vol VII. The Vol I line is bible-internal residue that should be marked superseded.
14. **"Candlefast"** (lib_a_hard_winter: "the week before Candlefast", "By Candlefast"). It comes from the manuscript (ch.2, l.849) but is not a feast or month in Vol VII. The r9 policy ("the game's text uses only Vol VII's months") covers months, not feasts. It should be registered in Vol VII (b) or flagged, as Leaffall was.
15. **The Basilica's location.**
    - lib_f_constancy_of_the_light places "the Basilica" at Sol-Luminary.
    - lib_e_only_song_sermon has the Flambeaux sent out from the Basilica of the Pure Note.
    - The bible never locates the Basilica (Vol III's Appendix lists it under the Choir-Church; Vol VI §3.3 has crates "Basilica of the Pure Note. For correction." at the Organum's Hall of Carriage). This is not a contradiction yet, but the book fixes a location that the bible leaves open.
    - Separately, the bible has two High Patriarchs (Severin Ashe in the Series Bible, Oren Calloway in Vol I and II). The library wisely names neither.
16. **The Tanner twins' season.** lib_b_stone_then_glass avoids it because the manuscript disagrees with itself: ch.10 says spring, while ch.1 (l.512) has Lyra singing them into the ground "at the end of summer". This is a manuscript-internal question for the owner.
17. **Sable's desk.** The library (Day-Book, Orpiment, the room's label) uses Vol V's "third desk from the east end on the north side". The manuscript (l.4135, l.5052, l.5678) has Sable: "Fourteen desks. Mine was by the second window." They are compatible but never reconciled.
18. **Outside scope but seen in passing:** ROADMAP R9 still describes Tobin's journal as "spring 412 to autumn 417". r1 ended it in the spring of 417 at the Pellows' table, so that line is stale.

**Checked and consistent:**
- Every dated weekday in the letters and ledgers (Tolley, Hatherly, Ruddock) agrees with Vol VII §6 (1 Stillmonth is a Dawnday, so the order is Dawnday, Driftday, Memoryday, Nullday, Rootday, Hearthday, Massday, Stasisday).
- The almanack's 419 feast days agree with Vol VII §4.
- The Margin's 907 − 7 = 900 matches Vol I and Vol II.
- Wendmere drowned in 413 and its bell rang six years, matching Vol IV and Vol V.
- The Harbour-Queen's amendment of 414 (Brisk, 417, "three years ago") matches.
- Fairweather's "last year" (the Rift, 413) and "the year before last" (the Bleach, 412) match for 414.
- The Veil's absorption, "110 years ago" from 410, gives c. 300 SR, matching the Series Bible.
- The hard winter's seventeen dead match MS ch.2's arithmetic.
- No book describes the Hush directly.

## Part VI — Open questions for the owner

Questions the phase documents asked, or the compilations raised, that nobody has answered yet.

### From Phases 6, 9–14 (A)

R answers only Phase 20 and 21 items. None of the questions below has a ruling. The Festivals addendum (V7b) settles only the festival concepts.

1. **Phase 9's sign-off list** ("[PROPOSED] items … needing Commander sign-off", P9 end of TALK), never ruled:

   - The Foundry morning is dated 412. (Canon.)

### From Phases 15–25 (B)

*"Answered" means in the Rulings, the ROADMAP or the code. Everything else is still open.*

**Phase 15**
- Lore review of the sermon line "since the Grove went quiet the Echo no longer catches the dead". There was **no ruling**, but the premise agrees with canon (Vol I §1.9; Vol V §The House of the Silent Veil). Only the Church's claim to "catch" them is new.
- Eclipses as a whole. "Eclipses are not in the author's canon; everything about them is [PROPOSED]". Never ruled.

**Phase 16**
1. Lower Plazas depot date: keep the 409 flood-relief reading, or move the date? **Never answered**, and Vol I's 401/409 dating is still uncorrected.
2. Mags and Pennick in Book One: keep, or swap in unnamed crew? **Never answered**, but moot: both are Book One cast in canon (Contradiction 4).
3. The heist "cathedral" as the Chapel of the Carillon's vestry and Held Letters: OK? **Never answered.**
4. Wren Fell as the Wren of the loop "Wren, the cloak"? **Never answered** (Contradiction 25).

**Phase 17**
1. The prologue's name and moment ("Prologue: The River Gate", 19 Frostmonth): keep, or rename? **No ruling found.** ROADMAP records it as delivered under that name.
2. Unlocks after either ending, and the "Open them anyway (they spoil the ending)" button: keep both? **No ruling found.**
3. The Long Night's three Cantors (Marl Dunning, Tansy Quill, Jory Fenn) and the lullaby's contour and timing. **Never ruled.**
4. "Stillmonth" in French as "Mois d'accalmie", not yet in the glossary. **No ruling found in scope.**
5. Cantabile inventions (Marrin Tace, the Harbour Office staff, Teel's rhyme, the slack-key dues, Article Sixteen salvage). **Never ruled.** Article Sixteen is canon in Vol IV anyway.

**Phase 18 and 19**
- No lore questions. The Phase 19 level cap of 30 for imported characters ("[PROPOSED]") was never ruled.

**Phase 20** (all answered by the Rulings)
- The Watch Downs and their missing sheep: answered (**canon (ruled)**; sheep **canon (delegated)**).
- The beacon's view: answered (kept).
- The lamp and the belfry looks: answered (kept).
- The pylon and the stone ring "checked against the bibles": answered ("the pylon and the stone ring are canon"). What they *were* is left to their builders.
- The six letters' colours: answered ("they match").
- The Hedge Knoll line: answered (no condition needed).
- The Downs area card: answered for now ("Kept for now … will want a card of their own" once a region). **The Downs' own card is still owed.**
- `TX.wall`: answered (deleted).
- The French for the eased-graphics notice: answered (delegated).
- The "Chapel belfry" capitalisation: answered (stays lower-case).

**Phase 20b**
1. Brick nogging: right for Oakhaven, or timber-and-plaster only? **Never answered.** ROADMAP still lists it.
2. Go on with Canva for the rest of the stand-ins? **Answered**: start painting the rest (recorded in Phase 20b), and Blender for the marks.
3. The Chapel bell stays undrawn, "closed as *not until the story rings one*". Consistent with Phase 20 #4, which was ruled; no separate answer needed.

**Phase 21** — all ten items answered (kept). The Chord name clash was not raised at the time (Contradiction 2).

**Phase 22**
1. Vane carrying ewes in the Kestrel's hold: does it fit his runs (garrison crates to the quarry)? **Never answered.**
2. The pylon as a Consortium tally-line: keep it? **Never answered.**
3. The Chalk Ram's seven-year scouring as Reach folklore? **Never answered.**
4. Is fresh mutton at the Boar plausible before Tamar's flock? **Answered in effect, not by a ruling.** The R2 sweep and the code make Oakhaven sell only "dear salt mutton off Vane's boat" (ROADMAP §R2; `js/sheepwalk22.js:221`). ROADMAP also records mutton on every menu as the owner's own request.
- The Rulings' own outstanding task: "the region's first inhabited place is a good spot for a player to learn [the sheep's fate] … That line is to be written and reviewed like any other." **Written** (Tamar, `tamar.hedge` / `journal.truth`), but still [PROPOSED] and **not yet reviewed**. Phase 29 (`js/thin29.js`, out of scope) also gives "There are no sheep" to new speakers on the Downs and in Wethercombe.

**Phase 22b**
1. The Woolwight: is "stolen fleeces over something that is not a sheep" the right amount of explanation, or a pure mystery? **Never answered.** The bull's-skull reveal was the owner's own request, but the lore question stands.
2. Is the Old Fleece's shroud as armour too grim? **Never answered.**
3. Should dungeons and the Hedge refuse camping too? **Never answered.**
4. The glass eel: fantasy fish, or plain eel? **Never answered.**

**Phase 24**
1. Should a later phase open on the Prologue itself (Solenne on the risers, 412 SR) before the flight? **Never answered.** ROADMAP still lists it.
2. Harrow's two gate lines: keep, cut or reword? **Never answered.** ROADMAP still lists it.
3. Is the roughly ninety-second sequence too long for a second playthrough? **Never answered.**

**Phase 25**
1. Extrusion depth: are people at 0.6 of their width too thick or too thin? **Never answered.**
2. Paint true backs for the named people later? **Never answered.**
3. A rig for the Hollow? **Never answered.**

**Rulings, still open**
- What the snapped pylon, the standing-stone ring and the broken tower stump *were* ("up to whoever builds each place"). Phase 22 proposed the pylon's identity (Phase 22 Q2). The ring and the stump are **still undecided**, and the ring is next in order (2).
- Whether to have a Downs area card of its own.
- PR #45 (the far-town `FAR_MIN`, the French notice, deleting `TX.wall`, merging Phase 21's French) was "open and awaiting merge" when the Rulings were written. Its merge was not checked here.

**Release Readiness and the GDD**
- The GDD's §10 open design decisions: free order or book order, how much of the Echo system goes in, and the graphic ceiling. No answers were found in scope.
- Release Readiness §3/§4 proposals (the Hollow, the Carders' house, Lissa Merrow, the Madder birth, etc.). ROADMAP R3–R5 shows many later built (out of scope). Whether each new line is canon has not been reviewed here.
- The sources of "NO SINGING. NO TOASTS. NO DICE" and of Hew Callan's line remain unverified (Contradiction 28).

### From Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass (D)

1. **Abner Fenning's name.** PLAYTEST: "a name I chose; tell me if you'd like another." Not yet ruled.
2. **Umber.** PLAYTEST offers "The alternative is to cut the Reach scribe entirely; say if you'd rather." Also open: whether she needs her own face (she uses Sable's coat sprite and the goodwife's portrait), and how she relates to the library's older Umber.
3. **Linnet.** Does the name sit right, and which Linnet keeps it (the Tarrow child or the hush-gift child)?
4. **C. Linley.** The ninth tape is the game's invention ("the book leaves it unread"). Does the owner want a name there at all?
5. **The twelve seized vox-bottles** [PROPOSED]. Keep the smaller keep-or-free choice? PLAYTEST asks "Does it still feel worth doing?"
6. **The Requiem branch.** The brainstorm's §7 question 6 ("keep it for the Requiem branch beside #9, or retire it?") is resolved in code as "keep, after the Wearer", but not ruled by the owner.
7. **Bettis put back.** The book carries her aboard; the game also lets Sable put her back. Should both threads stand?
8. **The Aegis child's identity.** The Series Bible's Wren Halloway (entry 41,112) or the manuscript's boy (WG-41,005, surname W or M)? The game follows the manuscript and names no one (brainstorm §5 item 18, §7 question 4).
9. **Leaffall.** The manuscript dates Whisper-Grove "the sixteenth of Leaffall, 419" and the twice-taken man "the fourteenth or fifteenth day of Leaffall". Vol VII has no Leaffall. R2's note: "Leaffall stays in the manuscript." It needs a ruling for the bible's calendar (brainstorm §5 item 23).
10. **The rope's last load and Tam Sowerby.** These remain from the brainstorm's §5 items 22 and 24, unbuilt, and still need a ruling before any rope scene.
11. **The variable counts.** Brainstorm §7 question 5: a spread around canon's numbers (rope, pump-men).
12. **The Concordance name.** The game layer uses it for faction standing; Vol III (b) uses it for a floor of the Hall of Carriage. Should one be renamed?
13. **Sable's age and oath in the likeness.** Should the Phase 23 dossier be corrected to the bible, or does it stand as a game-only likeness? This is the same for the ages of Kaelen, Lyra and Vane, Rooke's history, and Mags's rank.
14. **Names for canon roles.** These have no ruling: Orrin Pale (the sexton), Godwin Pettigrew (the lamplighter), Agnes Tull (the baker's wife), Tobit Dree (the lockkeeper), Essa (Tam's wife), and Brannoc Hewe, Sella Marr, Hal Ransome and Edda Quill (Legionnaires). Should the bible adopt them?
15. **Ennis Crowle's fate.** Vol V has her sung down first in the Felling (Frostmonth 419). The game shows her alive in autumn 419, which is consistent, but her library book and quest should not outlive that if the Reach's winter is ever played.
16. **Recorded voices.** Sable's Deep Woods greeting, Bettis's old line and Hetty's new polite greeting now fall back to the synthesised voice until re-recorded (PLAYTEST). This is not lore, but it is owed.

## Appendix — How each compilation cites its sources

Each compilation was made on 29 Oct 2026 by reading the repository only. Their notes on scope, labels and source abbreviations are kept here so that every citation above can be followed.

### A · Phases 6, 9–14

Compiled 29 Oct 2026 for sleeves. Read-only research: nothing in the repo was edited.

**Scope.** Every distinct piece of world content (people, places, creatures, institutions, customs, songs, lore objects, events, dates, sayings) in the Phase 6, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13 and 14 "New In-Game Text" files. Also checked: the Vol VII (b) Festivals addendum, the Phase 20-21 Author Rulings, every World Bible volume and the Book One manuscript. Pure UI and controls text is skipped, as are Options rows that carry no lore.

**How to read a status.**
- CANON-RULED: sleeves ruled it in.
- CANON-BOOK: it already stands in the books or an existing bible volume, or restates them.
- DECIDED-DELEGATED: Claude decided it under delegation.
- PROPOSED: the game invented it and nobody has ruled on it.
- CUT/CHANGED: it was ruled out or replaced.

Where an entry adds detail to canon, the status covers the *new detail*, and the sentence names the canon base.

**Source abbreviations** (all files under `story/`)
- P6, P9, P10, P11, P12, P13, P14 = `Game Layer/Phase N - New In-Game Text.md`
- R = `Game Layer/Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md`
- B1 = `Books/Book One - The Silenced Province/Book One - The Silenced Province (full manuscript).md`

**Bible abbreviations** (all files under `story/World Bibles/`)
- SB = `Series Bible (canon).md`
- V1 = `Vol I - The Expanded World Bible.md`
- V2 = `Vol II - Gazetteer and Bestiary.md`
- V3 = `Vol III - Canon Reconciliation and the Road to Book Five.md`
- V3b = `Vol III (b) - Errata and Rulings.md`
- V4 = `Vol IV - The Hush, the Organum, the Rust Coast.md`
- V5 = `Vol V - The Winter Gazetteer.md`
- V6 = `Vol VI - The Rift, the Organum Revisited, and the Press.md`
- V7 = `Vol VII - The Calendar of Aethelgard.md`
- V7b = `Vol VII (b) - Addendum - Festivals of the Reach.md`

---

### B · Phases 15–25

*Research digest for the series bible. Book One browser game, Phases 15, 16, 17, 18, 19, 20, 20b, 21, 22 (with 22b), 24 and 25, plus the Phase 20–21 Author Rulings, the Release Readiness report and the Game Design Document. Phase 23 is not covered. Compiled 29 Oct 2026 from the repo at `/home/user/echoes-of-the-shattered-octave` (read-only).*

**How to read this.**
- Game-layer files are in `story/Game Layer/` and bible volumes are in `story/World Bibles/`. Each entry names the file it came from by basename.
- **Rulings.** "Rulings" means `Phase 20-21 - Author Rulings.md`, and a ruling beats a phase doc. Some later changes from Phase 27 and the owner's rulings in PR #63 appear only in `ROADMAP.md` §R2 — Canon sweep. Those changes replace things in this digest's scope, so they are cited where they apply.
- **Status.** Each entry is marked CANON-RULED, CANON-BOOK, DECIDED-DELEGATED, PROPOSED or CUT/CHANGED.
  - A phase doc sometimes marks something [PROPOSED] when a bible volume already has it. Those entries are CANON-BOOK, with a note.
- **Bible line.** "Bible: new" means a case-insensitive search of all ten bible volumes found no match for the name.
- **What is left out.** Pure UI and controls text: Phase 18 GPU, PWA, daily-challenge and ghost strings, the Phase 19 co-op lobby, and the Phase 21 Options and photo mode.

---

### D · Phases 23 and 26–40: the release road and the canon pass

**Scope.** Everything world-facing built between Phase 23 (character creation, 25 Oct) and the canon pass (28 Oct), with the README work of 29 Oct (no world content) noted only in passing:
- Phase 23: `tools/i18n/p23-cast.tsv`, `p23-dossier.tsv`, `p23-likeness.tsv`, `p23-creation.tsv` (English read from `tools/i18n/strings.en.json` and `js/data.js`, because these four sheets are keyed by id).
- R1 and R2: `p27-r1.tsv`, `p27-r2.tsv`, `p27-canon.tsv`.
- R3: `js/narrow28.js`, `p28-narrow.tsv`. R4: `js/thin29.js`, `p29-thin.tsv`. R5a: `js/hollow30.js`, `p30-hollow.tsv`. R5b: `js/grove31.js`, `p31-grove.tsv`.
- The canon pass: `p32-canon.tsv` to `p40-umber.tsv`, `js/days35.js`.
- Also read: `PLAYTEST.md`, `ROADMAP.md` (Release road), `story/Game Layer/Story Brainstorm - Book One in play.md` (§0, §1, §5, §6, §7), `Release Readiness - length, areas and enrichment.md` §2, `Library of the Reach - Notes for the Owner.md`, and the commits `9c90bc6..origin/main`, plus `13e6220` and `1649039` for Phase 23.

**Conventions.**
- "MS ch. N, l. X" is a line in `story/Books/Book One - The Silenced Province/Book One - The Silenced Province (full manuscript).md`.
- Bible checks were made by searching all ten files in `story/World Bibles/` (case-insensitive) and the manuscript, for each name. "Bible: new" means no volume names it. Section names follow each file's headings; the Series Bible's line numbers moved while this was written, so they are not given.
- The owner's ruling of 28 Oct, quoted wherever it applies: *"Bettis Renn should be Ivo's wife just as in the book. Match any inconsistency to match the book."* (PLAYTEST.md, "The canon pass").
- The owner's library rulings of 27 Oct (PR #63), quoted where they apply: *"Manuscript against an in-game book or journal: the manuscript wins. A volume against an in-game book: the volume stands, and the book changes to match."*
- Status is the first status word in each entry. Where an entry mixes (a canon thing given an invented name), the canon part is named first and the invented part follows in brackets.

### C · The Library of the Reach

Prepared 29 Oct 2026 for the series bible. I only read the repo; nothing in it was edited.

**Scope.** This digest covers the 65 in-world books of the Library of the Reach (built 27 Oct 2026), the four libraries and their keepers and quests, the owner's thirteen rulings, and the earlier "Moor" serial (now the Cantabile serial).

**Sources read in full:**
- `story/Game Layer/Library of the Reach - Notes for the Owner.md`. I also read its pre-ruling version at `636c236`, which states the thirteen questions.
- `Review - The Moor serial.md`. It was re-read after it changed on disk today (commit `2ab9ea7`); finding 5 gained an update note.
- `Session record - stand-ins and books.md`.
- The game code: `js/library26-books.js` (all 65 texts), `js/library26.js`, `js/libraries26.js`, `js/books.js`, and `tools/library/placement.json`.
- The book sources: `tools/library/books/*.json`. Each book's `notes` field lists its sources and its [PROPOSED] inventions.
- The translation files `tools/i18n/p26-books.tsv`, `p26-libraries.tsv`, `p26-library.tsv` and `p27-canon.tsv`. Every English line in them is either in the JS or is a formatting string.
- `js/upgrades.js` `bk_veil`, the `js/tobin.js` page dates, and the `js/data.js` Umber NPC.
- The commit messages `9167988`, `37f20cd`, `129f31e`, `a581fb4`, `9e77182`, `c8a2406`, `73b69cf`, `636c236`, `fd20732`, `052a6a6`, `7ccd72e`, `d73de90`, `dd2c3bf`, `3c9b261` and `2ab9ea7`, and ROADMAP R2 and R8.

**Bible check.** Every name and term below was grepped (word-boundary match, case-sensitive with a case-insensitive recheck) in all ten files of `story/World Bibles/` and in the Book One manuscript. The grep ran against **Series Bible v1.3**, which landed today in `2ab9ea7` mid-research; the greps were re-run after it landed. Section names are as they stand now.

**Labels used below:**
- "Bible: new (MS ch. n)": the fact is in the Book One manuscript but in no bible volume.
- "Bible: new (game text …)": the fact appears only in an earlier game-text phase.
- Series Bible v1.3 now points to a **World Bible Vol VIII, *The Game Layer***, as the place for game canon. That file does not exist yet in `story/World Bibles/`, and this digest is input for it.

**Other copies of the texts.** The book texts are also exported, as of `2ab9ea7`, to `story/Library of the Reach/` (one Markdown file per book, plus the serial) and `story/Game Layer/Phase 26 - New In-Game Text.md`. Those are generated copies; the JSON and JS are the source.

**Statuses:**
- **CANON-RULED**: fixed by an owner ruling, which is cited.
- **CANON-BOOK**: the book repeats canon from the bible, the manuscript or the novels.
- **PROPOSED**: only the game book says it.
